The people's side only keeps the balance work of P14-01 and every fingerprint as they are |
  | Move costs, sway 10, sidestep 6, jump 4, run 7 per second | as above | unchanged | the style tables were balanced on these costs (P14-01); the drain is the rate of pressing, not the price of one press |
  | Attack tokens per target (Easy 1, Normal 2, Hard 2, Expert 3), grant gaps (120 / 90 / 200 / 200 ticks), rests (200 / 150 / 300 / 300 ticks) | as listed | unchanged | measured above: the enemies leave 82 to 93 % of a group fight quiet; a try with a slower Easy (150 / 240 ticks) changed the seeded Easy brawls and the item-throw rate without helping the tank |

  The numbers live in `PLAYER_STAMINA_EDGE` and `STAMINA_EDGE_NONE` (next to the attributes), `ATTACK_NO_REGEN` and `GUARD_NO_REGEN`; `deriveStats` gives every fighter `edge: STAMINA_EDGE_NONE` and MatchSim swaps it for the people's side next to the CPU damage level (`giveStaminaEdge`: the party of a plain match and of Co-op, a person's slot in `buildLineup`).

All four fail on the old code. No fingerprint or hash was re-recorded.
- Open: (1) a player who mashes without ever pausing is still at the bottom of the tank 67 % of an Easy 1v3 (was 82 %): stamina is the brake on mashing and an empty tank still attacks, weaker; more would make Easy mashing free. (2) Hard and Expert keep today's rules (the Press script is under 25 % of the tank 13 to 19 % there, the longest spell 13 s in the Hard and 23 s in the Expert 1v1): the owner asked for Easy; say if Hard should get a smaller edge. (3) A latent hole of P18-34 found on the way: a CPU on a pick-up plan can take a neighbouring throwable that its team may not fetch yet (the Hard seed 14 test fight took a crate at tick 76 and a bottle at tick 984 for the hammer beside it; the old code did the same at tick 932, hidden by a longer fight); not fixed here, nothing in the final code touches it. (4) The owner's Easy Arcade run is on the P16-03 list (Stage 19). (5) Review of 2026-11-05 found that the first version left every lineup out (`!this.setup`), so the menu Brawl never gave its person the edge (16.0 and 15.8 % measured on a Brawl lineup); fixed in `buildLineup`. (6) Normal wins of the Press script in 1v3 and 1v4 rise by 20 to 40 points (see the tries table); say if the owner wants Normal closer to the old rules and accepts a higher Normal share (about 13 to 14 %, the review's edge) in return.

### P18-25 + P18-32 thrown bodies (2026-11-05)

- Owner (2026-11-03, verbatim): "when throwing an enemy the thrown enemy cant hit other enemies". P18-25 was the check and the decision, P18-32 the implementation; merged. Decided by the coordinator's delegate under the owner's standing go ("balanced"); the owner can change any number.
- What the check found on the old code: a thrown body hurt nobody. `MatchSim.separate` lets a THROWN fighter pass through every other body, and nothing else looked for a touch: in flight a body met only scenery (`Combat.impactCheck`), hazards and its own landing. That held for all 14 throws (the 12 of the front, rear and low grips, Sambo Hip Throw, BJJ Guard Pull), in every setup: enemies, a co-op ally, a fighter lying down, with Friendly fire on or off. A 1v1 has nobody else in the path, so it never showed there. The P4-01 rule (damage goes only to another team, CPU enemies never hurt each other, Friendly fire is the players' setting) is the reason a strike could not do it either.
- Rule (who is hurt): every fighter in the path that the thrower may hurt, i.e. `MatchSim.canHurt(thrower, other)`. That includes the thrown fighter's own teammates (a thrown enemy meets the other enemies; a CPU that throws player 1 hurts player 2) and excludes the thrower's own team unless Friendly fire is on (the players' team only). So the P4-01 rule stays for strikes, grabs and tackles and gives way for a thrown body only. "No effect" is out because the owner asked for an effect. The thrower and the thrown fighter are never hurt by it.
- Rule (what is hit): the body touches a fighter when their circles meet (the sum of the two radii, 0.68 m) and they differ in height by at most `BODY_HIT_BAND` 1.25 m. Each fighter is hit once per flight. Not hit: a fighter that a strike may not touch (`Combat.strikable`: invulnerable, knocked out, in its wake-up grace, in a clinch or grapple, held by somebody else), and a fighter that is flying itself (a knockdown flight or a juggle). No block and no sway, like a thrown item.
- Effect: power = horizontal speed at the touch (m/s) x (`BODY_WEIGHT_BASE` 0.8 + `BODY_WEIGHT_STEP` 0.1 x the thrown body's weight class 0..4), i.e. x 0.8 for a Light body to x 1.2 for a Super heavy one. A standing fighter staggers below `BODY_KNOCKDOWN_POWER` 5 and is knocked down from 5 (flung on along the flight at 4 m/s, lift 2.6, like a thrown item's knockdown). Stagger: 24 ticks x the fighter's stun stat, pushed 0.8 m. Damage before the stats: 2 + 0.8 x power (4.3 at the weakest, 8.2 at the strongest), times the thrower's damage stat and boost, the other's `taken` (and `impact` for a knockdown); a fighter already lying takes half (`BODY_DOWN_SHARE` 0.5) and no reaction, as a ground hit. Hit stop 6 (stagger) or 9 (knockdown); the hit goes out as the normal 'hit' event (`mv.id` 'body_throw', tier 2 or 3), so spark, flash, camera, sound, rumble, the damage pop and the credit to the thrower (combo, momentum, knock-out, scenery impact credit) are the existing ones. A knockdown by a body is a plain knockdown flight and hurts nobody in turn (no chain).
- What the numbers mean: the 14 throws fly 3.6 to 6.4 m/s (hip toss 5.6, side throws 6.4, German suplex 3.6). On a middle (class 2) body, from 5 up are the Hip Toss, Side Throw, Rear Spin Throw and Sambo Hip Throw; the Single-Leg Sweep (4.97 at the touch), Back Suplex (4.4), Rear Takedown (4.6), German suplex, Double-Leg Takedown, Pull-Through Trip and Guard Pull stagger. A heavier body adds knockdowns (Single-Leg Sweep and Rear Takedown from class 3, Back Suplex at class 4), a Light (class 0) body takes the Hip Toss's away (it staggers).
- Rejected: no effect (the owner asked for one); a fixed knockdown for every throw (the slow suplexes would then hit as hard as the side throws, and the weight of the body would not matter); the same for every fighter including the thrower's team (it would make Friendly fire off pointless in co-op); hurting everyone except the thrower and the thrown fighter (the thrower's own team would then be hurt with Friendly fire off, which is what the setting is for); a slow-down of the body at each touch (it would make the second fighter in a line take less for no reason a player can see); bodies hitting each other during the fall after a knockdown (a chain through a whole crowd from one throw); block or sway against a thrown body (a thrown item can be blocked because the defender sees it come, a body arrives with the throw's own warning and its owner's choice of lane).
- CPU: a Hard or Expert CPU (the difficulties with `env`, which already look for scenery and walls to throw into) chooses the throw direction by `AIController.groupThrowDir`: the direction in whose lane (a strip two bodies wide, from the foe to a throw's reach = launch speed x 2 x arc / gravity, plus the radii) stands the most fighters it may hurt (alive or lying, not out); no one in any lane returns '' and the old choice (a hazard lure, scenery, a corner escape, then the style roll) goes on. It comes after the corner escape, so a CPU in a corner still throws its foe out of it. Easy and Normal keep the old choice; their throws still hurt whoever happens to be in the path. No new random number is drawn when a lane is found, so a 1v1 (nobody else to count) draws exactly as before.
- Measured on the old and the new code (headless `SimRunner.run`, 100 seeded fights per row, AI vs AI, seats swapped on even seeds, 90 s clock for the lineups; Win% = team A, the first named; throws and body hits per fight; the brawl rows are the player against N CPU enemies, an AI player):

  | Row | Win% before | Win% after | Seconds before / after | Throws per fight before / after | Body hits per fight (after) | Knockdowns among them |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 2v2 Normal | 46 | 45 | 24 / 24 | 0.76 / 0.76 | 0.01 | 0 of 1 |
  | 2v2 Hard | 48 | 53 | 27 / 27 | 0.37 / 0.29 | 0.04 | 4 of 4 |
  | 2v2 Expert | 47 | 50 | 35 / 34 | 0.31 / 0.25 | 0.06 | 5 of 6 |
  | 2v2 mirror Hard | 41 | 41 | 26 / 26 | 0.25 / 0.24 | 0.03 | 2 of 3 |
  | 3v3 Hard | 52 | 48 | 38 / 38 | 1.33 / 1.41 | 0.41 | 35 of 41 |
  | 3v3 Normal, Pit | 49 | 48 | 31 / 31 | 2.21 / 2.21 | 0.32 | 16 of 32 |
  | 1v3 Hard | 8 | 6 | 61 / 61 | 0.32 / 0.27 | 0.01 | 0 of 1 |
  | 1v3 Normal | 1 | 1 | 38 / 38 | 1.02 / 1.02 | 0.08 | 2 of 8 |
  | 1v5 Hard | 0 | 0 | 71 / 72 | 0.56 / 0.68 | 0.17 | 10 of 17 |

  Reading: the body hits average 7.3 damage (3v3 Hard 297 over 41 hits) against 100+ health, under half a hit per crowd fight on average (0.41 at the most), so the win rates move inside the noise of 100 fights (+-10 points) in both directions and the fight lengths do not move. The 3v3 Hard row, where the CPUs aim their throws, has the most (0.41 a fight) and still moves the team rate by -4 points. A 1v1 is untouched: 1v1 fights of three preset pairs at Normal, Hard and Expert, 40 seeds each (winner, seconds, ticks, rounds, health, damage per move type), give bit-identical results on the old and the new code, so the P14-01 style-pair win rates (35 to 65 percent at Easy and Normal, 40 to 60 at Hard and Expert, 136 pairs) are exactly the recorded ones. The balance stays as decided; the numbers above are what to look at if the owner finds throws too strong in crowds: raise `BODY_KNOCKDOWN_POWER` first (6 leaves only the side throws), then lower `BODY_DAMAGE_PER_POWER`.
- Self-tests (breakpoint-tests.js, section "Thrown bodies (P18-25 + P18-32)", 8 tests): (1) every throw of every move style in all grips and directions sends the thrown enemy through a standing enemy, which takes exactly one hit from the thrower, a stagger or knockdown by its power, and nobody else is touched (14 throws); (2) one throw through two standing enemies hits each once, the nearer first, and a fighter off the lane is not hit; (3) an ally is hit only with Friendly fire on; a CPU that throws player 0 hurts player 1 and not its own team; (4) a fighter lying down takes the reduced damage and stays down, a knocked-out and a just-risen fighter are not touched; (5) the threshold, the weight classes, the damage formula and the decided constants, with the speed and weight set exactly; (6) one hit per fighter however long the touch, the height band, no hit on a fighter that is flying (a juggle included), no chain from a knocked-down fighter, none from a fighter knocked down by a strike; (7) the CPU lane choice in all four directions at Hard and Expert, and that it ignores its own team, a knocked-out fighter and an empty floor; (8) a throw played the way a player plays it (grab, Grapple) through the next enemy, with the same seed playing the same hits twice. All 8 fail on the old code (5 on the missing body hit, 3 on the missing code itself: the body fields and `groupThrowDir`). Five deliberate breaks of the new code (ally check removed, once-only removed, no knockdown, no half damage for a lying fighter, flying fighters hit) were each caught by at least one test.
- Fingerprints: none changed and none re-recorded. The three Free Fight hashes, the old-layout hashes and the hazards-and-items hashes are 1v1 fights, in which nobody stands in a path; the brawl tests that hash or compare counts passed unchanged.
- Checks: headless run with `BP_FILTER="Thrown bodies"`: 8 / 8; full headless run through `heavy.mjs`: 513 / 530, exactly the 17 known failures (16 "needs the main menu", 1 "blank faces"), listeners 25 / 25 before and after; `breakpoint.html` is 1,184,374 bytes (LF, BOM counted) of 1,200,000. Still open: the same tests in Chrome, and the owner's feel check (for the P16-03 list; the coordinator adds it): throw an enemy through a group in Brawl and in co-op with Friendly fire off and on.

### P18-18 Free Fight merge decided (2026-11-06)

- Owner (2026-11-01 and again 2026-11-04 05:40, verbatim): "Free Fight Brawl and CoOp Brawl collapse into Free Fight"; Versus, Brawl, Co-op Brawl and Free Fight become one mode named Free Fight with all the options of the four, built on P4-08's lineup (slots, teams, Even teams, clock). Tournament (P11) and online (P12) use the same setup. Decided by the coordinator's delegate under the owner's standing go ("balanced"); every "Decided" line can be changed by the owner. P18-19 builds it, P18-42 / P18-43 add the fight rules, P18-05 adds the Difficulty row to the other modes.
- What the code does today (base 794e348): five setup flows. Free Fight: fighter cards, then a cards-and-rules screen (opponent, Difficulty, Rounds, Round timer, "Players and teams"), then the level list. Versus: P1 card, P2 card, a rules screen, the level list. Brawl: one card screen, the lineup screen, the level list. Co-op Brawl: the join screen (device seats, fighter per seat, "Difficulty" button), the level list. The lineup screen holds Opponents (2 to 8 fighters), Teams (deal), a row per slot (fighter, control, team), "CPU level", Even teams and Clock. Three start paths: the plain 1v1 (`startFree`, `startVersus`: rounds, round timer, Versus score, byte-for-byte fingerprints), the lineup (`startLineup`, `cfg.slots`: one fight, no rounds, no waves, no friendly fire) and the co-op party (`startCoop`, `cfg.coop`: 1 to 4 people, 2 + people enemies up to 6 picked by the game, waves on the 6 of 8 levels that have a waves table, friendly fire read from Settings at every start). Nothing of the setup is saved: `Game.selection` and `Game.lineups` live in memory. Saved and keyed by mode: `Progress.d.grades.free / brawl / coop` (`GRADE_MODES`), `Settings.v.friendlyFire`. Versus has no grade; its session score is `Game.versus`.
- Decided: the mode list is five rows, in this order: Arcade, Survival, Training, Free Fight, Tournament (`MODE_ORDER`). `MODE_INFO` loses `versus`, `brawl` and `coop`. Free Fight's text: "Any fight you can set up: one on one, two players, a brawl with teams, or co-op against a crowd. Choose the fighters, level, difficulty and rules." The Play line on the main menu, the Unlocks hint ("all 5 game modes" follows `MODE_ORDER.length`), the controls table header "P2 keyboard (Versus)" > "P2 keyboard (two players)", the HUD mode line (all Free Fight lineups: FREE FIGHT; the crowd path: FREE FIGHT with its friendly-fire and wave tags), `SETUP_LABEL` (one entry, `free`) and every results subtitle say Free Fight or the fight type, never Versus, Brawl or Co-op Brawl. "Versus" stays only as the name of a fight type (below) and of the Versus score line.
- Fight types (the quick starts). The first row of the Free Fight screen is "Fight type" with four values that set the whole lineup at once, so the old modes are one tap away; the label is derived from the shape of the lineup (rule below), so an edit that keeps a preset's shape (another fighter, another level) keeps its name and an edit that changes the shape shows "Custom"; choosing a type again restores its preset (slots, deal, crowd) and keeps the fighter picks of the slots both have.

  | Fight type | Replaces | Lineup it sets | Path that plays | Rounds / clock (defaults on first use only; a later choice of a type does not reset them) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Duel (default) | Free Fight | slot 1 a person, slot 2 the CPU, free-for-all | plain 1v1 | Best of 3, 60 s per round |
  | Versus | Versus | slots 1 and 2 people, free-for-all | plain 1v1, Versus score | Best of 3, 60 s per round |
  | Brawl | Brawl | slot 1 a person on Team A, 3 CPU on Team B ("players vs CPU") | lineup | one fight, clock Off |
  | Co-op | Co-op Brawl | 2 people (1 to 4 on one team; 1 when the last device used was touch) against the game's crowd | crowd (party) | one fight, no clock |

  Label rule: crowd > "Co-op"; 2 slots with one person > "Duel"; 2 slots with two people > "Versus"; 3 or more slots with the "players vs CPU" deal > "Brawl"; anything else "Custom" (this shape rule is the only rule). The labels name presets, not engine paths: any lineup with a person, a CPU and teams plays, the label only helps.
- Decided: the crowd stays a flag of the lineup (`lineup.crowd`), not a pile of CPU slots. With it on, the Players and teams screen shows only the people (1 to 4, one team, no teams row, no Even teams row), the Opponents row says "Crowd: 2 + players, up to 6; waves on levels that have them" and the start builds the old party config (`cfg.coop`, `enemies: coopEnemyCount(people)`, `waves: !!ARENAS[arena].waves`). Because: folding the crowd and its waves into lineups would change the co-op engine (the co-op tests and fingerprints) for no player-visible gain; P18-42 / P18-43 may fold them later. The three start paths stay; the screens become one.
- First screen ("Free Fight", id `freefight`; replaces `char`, `setup`, `lineup` and `coop` for this mode; Back goes to the mode list; Training keeps `setup`). Five rows and two sticky buttons so it fits 390x844 and 844x390 without scrolling:

  | # | Row | Values | Default | Notes |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | Fight type | Duel, Versus, Brawl, Co-op (shows Custom when the shape is none of the four) | Duel; afterwards the last used | left / right or a tap cycles the four presets |
  | 2 | Players and teams | button, subtitle names the fighters first, then `lineupText` ("Kai vs Mara - 1 player, 1 CPU, one on one"; "Kai vs 3 CPU - 4 fighters, players vs CPU"; the crowd "Kai, Mara vs a crowd of 4 - 2 players") | | opens the grid screen |
  | 3 | Level | the 8 levels (`ARENA_ORDER`) | Warehouse | the one value row where a tap does not step it: left / right cycles, Enter or a tap opens the level list (`GS.ARENASEL`, as today) whose button reads "Use this level" and returns here; a second tap on the chosen level also returns (it no longer starts the fight) |
  | 4 | Difficulty | Easy, Normal, Hard, Expert | Easy (P18-05's name and place; replaces "CPU level", "Arcade difficulty" and the join screen button) | dimmed "Not used: no CPU fighter" when the lineup has none |
  | 5 | More options | button, subtitle lists what is not default ("Best of 5, clock 120 s, friendly fire on") | | opens `freemore` |

  Sticky buttons: "Back" and "Start fight" (the default focus; disabled with the reason line `Game.lineupProblem` gives). "Start fight" starts at once (no separate arena step).
- "More options" screen (`freemore`, Back returns to the first screen). Rows in this order; a row that does not apply is shown dimmed with its reason, out of the focus order (the existing `unusedRow`):

  | Row | Values | Default | Applies to |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Rounds | Best of 1, 3, 5 (`selection.rounds` 1, 2, 3) | Best of 3 | Duel and Versus only ("Not used: this lineup is one fight") |
  | Time limit | plain 1v1: Off, 30, 60, 90 s per round (`selection.timer`); lineup: Off, 60, 120, 180 s for the whole fight (`lineup.timer`, `LINEUP_TIMERS`) | 60 s per round; Off | the label of the value says "per round" or "whole fight"; the two values are stored apart so switching types loses neither; "Not used" for the crowd (it never had a clock) |
  | Friendly fire | Off, On | Off | lineups with a teammate of a person, and the crowd; "Not used" in a 1v1 |
  | Fight rules (P18-42) | its rows start here, "Rules preset" first, then area, ring-out, items, hazards, breakables, health, start side | P18-42 decides | P18-19 builds the screen with the rows above and no placeholder row; P18-42 appends its group after Friendly fire |

  Even teams stays where P4-08 put it, on the Players and teams screen next to the teams it is about (with its plain-words hint); it is not moved to More options. Clock moves to "Time limit" here because it is the same idea as the round timer.
- Players and teams screen (the existing lineup screen, id `lineup`, reached from row 2; Back returns to the first screen): rows Opponents (1 to 7, wrapping: the other fighters, people or CPU, as today, so the lineup has 2 to 8 fighters, 1 vs 7 CPU, a 4-people lineup and Versus all fit; with the crowd: Players, 1 to 4), Teams (the deals), the slot grid, Even teams (dimmed "Not used: the teams are equal" only for a free-for-all or equal team sizes, whoever is in them), then the devices hint and the problem line. It loses "CPU level" (now row 4 of the first screen) and "Clock" (now Time limit) and its "Choose arena" button (the first screen starts the fight; the screen's button is "Back"). Slot control cell: Player n or CPU; slot 1 is always a person (toast "Slot 1 is always a player"); at most 4 people.
- Picking a fighter: the fighter cell of a slot still cycles with left / right (people: unlocked roster plus customs; a CPU slot: the whole roster plus customs, as the old Free Fight opponent cards allowed locked fighters); Enter or a tap opens the existing card screen (`char`) for that slot, titled "Choose fighter: Player 1" / "CPU 2", where a locked card is refused for a person slot only, and Confirm or Back returns to the Players and teams screen. The cards of `opp:` and the second pick of Versus (`pickSide`, `player2`) are gone; `selection.player` and `selection.opponent` stay as the picks of slots 1 and 2 for the other modes and for the first launch (`lineupFromSelection` / `lineupToSelection` keep working for slot 1 and 2). To keep the common path short the Players and teams screen opens with the focus on slot 1's fighter cell (Enter opens its cards at once), and the results button "Change fighters" opens the card screen of slot 1 directly (the Versus type: slot 1, then slot 2), then Confirm returns to the first screen with the setup kept; Back from the cards returns to the screen they were opened from. Row 2 shows who fights, so the choice is visible without opening it. The creator's "+ CREATE" button on the card screen stays and its return writes the new fighter into the slot being picked (today it writes `Game.selection[UI.pickKey()]`).
- Decided: friendly fire becomes a Free Fight option (row above), default Off, and the Settings row "Friendly fire (co-op)" is removed (a setting that only one mode reads is an option of that mode). The rule in a lineup: with it on, a person can hurt any teammate (person or CPU); a CPU never hurts a teammate; it applies to every team, not only the lead's. In the crowd it is the old rule (the players hurt each other, enemies never each other). Off, nothing changes anywhere. In code: `canHurt` becomes `att !== def && (att.team !== def.team || (this.friendlyFire && (this.coop ? att.team === TEAM_PLAYERS : att.person)))` (the `att !== def` test stays first: the smoke cloud and the crate burst of the person's own item call `canHurt(owner, other)` and must never hit their owner), and `startLineup` passes `friendlyFire` (today it passes nothing, so P4-08 lineups have none; this lifts "teammates in a lineup never hurt each other" only when the row is On). The thrown-body rule of P18-25 uses `canHurt`, so it follows. Because: the owner called it "the players' setting" (P4-07); using `att.person` keeps P4-01 ("CPU enemies never hurt each other") and makes the row fair for a person on Team B.
- Where every option of the four old modes went (nothing a player could set is lost without a reason in the last column):

  | Old mode | Option today | New place | Reason when it moves or goes |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Free Fight | opponent card | slot 2's fighter cell / its cards | one grid for every slot |
  | Free Fight | Difficulty ("CPU level" in lineups) | first screen, row 4 | P18-05: one name, one place |
  | Free Fight | Rounds, Round timer | More options: Rounds, Time limit | same values, 1v1 only |
  | Free Fight | Players and teams, level | first screen rows 2 and 3 | unchanged |
  | Free Fight, Versus | Settings > Ring-out (1v1 on a level with a ring edge; Duel and Versus only, `beginMatch`) | stays in Settings, same meaning, read at every start; Duel and Versus only | P18-42 moves it into the fight rules (area, ring-out); until then nothing changes |
  | all | Settings > Items, Hazards | stay in Settings | P18-42 decides |
  | Versus | P1 pick, P2 pick on the same screen | slot 1 and slot 2 fighter cells; "Change fighters" runs slot 1 then 2 | one grid |
  | Versus | Rounds, Round timer, session score | More options; `Game.versus` unchanged | |
  | Versus | keyboard halves and pads dealt between two | the 2-people plan | unchanged |
  | Brawl | pick fighter, Opponents, Teams, slots, Even teams | slot cells, Players and teams screen | unchanged |
  | Brawl | CPU level | first screen Difficulty | P18-05 |
  | Brawl | Clock | More options: Time limit (whole fight) | same values |
  | Co-op Brawl | join with Punch / A / Start, leave with Defend / B, fighter per seat with left / right | Players slots and their fighter cells; devices dealt by people count | the auto deal covers the cases (see Input plan); the join screen stays for the Tournament |
  | Co-op Brawl | Difficulty button | first screen Difficulty | P18-05 |
  | Co-op Brawl | Friendly fire (Settings; read again at every start, so it could be changed between rematches in Settings) | More options: Friendly fire; the rematch repeats the setup as it stands, a change goes through Change fighters and More options | it is an option of this mode only; one more step between rematches is accepted |
  | Co-op Brawl | Start on a joined gamepad starts the brawl | Start on a gamepad starts the fight from the first screen | new input code (see Input plan) |
  | Co-op Brawl | touch not available | touch plays a party of 1 | improvement |
  | Co-op Brawl | enemies 2 + players, waves | crowd flag (Co-op type) | unchanged |

- Input plan (no join screen in Free Fight; the Tournament keeps the join screen, so `screen_coop` becomes Tournament-only and its "Co-op brawl" branch, `coopStart`'s non-Tournament code and `startCoop` are deleted):
  - 1 person: solo; keyboard 1, any gamepad, touch (the on-screen buttons show). The crowd with 1 person now allows touch too (the old co-op did not).
  - 2 people: the Versus plan; P1 keyboard 1 or the first of two gamepads, P2 keyboard 2 or a gamepad, a lone gamepad plays P2, touch is P1 only; either person can pause.
  - 3 or 4 people: seats are dealt without a screen by `Inputs.autoDevices`, changed in P18-19 (today it always gives seat 1 to keyboard 1). Decided: when the connected gamepads are at least as many as the people, deal gamepads only (P1 on the first pad, then the next pads in order), so 3 or 4 people on pads play together and no pad is left idle; otherwise keyboard 1 plus the pads, and keyboard 2 only when the pads are still short (as P4-08 decided). So 4 people need 4 gamepads, or 3 gamepads and keyboard 1, or 2 gamepads and both keyboards; 3 people need 3 gamepads, or 2 and keyboard 1, or 1 and both keyboards. Start is off with the reason line when the devices do not suffice ("4 players need 4 gamepads, or fewer gamepads and the keyboards"; the text is built from the count of connected pads); no touch. The keyboard still drives the menus. The old Co-op join screen allowed the pads-only party, so this is not a loss; `autoSeat` joins the dealt devices as before (a pad may take seat 1).
  - While the setup screens are open the plan is solo (all devices drive the menus as P1, as lineups do today); the plan by people count starts with the fight (`lineupPeople()` must also count the crowd's party, `cfg.coop.length`). Gamepad: up / down move between rows, left / right change the value, A opens a button row, B goes back, and Start on a gamepad starts the fight from the first screen (new input code: today only the join screen reads `COOP_START_BUTTON`, so the menu pad handler gets a Start case for this screen; it is how a co-op started from a gamepad before). Keyboard: arrows or WASD, Enter, Esc. Touch: a tap on a value row steps it forward (wrapping; the Level row is the exception, a tap opens its list), a tap on a button row opens it, Start fight is the bottom-right sticky button within the thumb's reach; no new on-screen buttons. P2 no longer picks "on their own device": one person drives the setup screens.
  - Decided: not offered any more: choosing the devices of a co-op by hand (for example only gamepads on seats 3 and 4 with keyboard 2 on seat 2, or a party without keyboard 1). With the pads-only deal above, the auto deal covers every case of 1 to 4 people on pads, keyboard 1 and keyboard 2 that the join screen allowed except the odd ones (a party without keyboard 1 next to keyboard 2, a pad on a chosen seat); the owner's question about a "Devices" row is settled by this and does not block P18-19.
- Saves and settings (no lost data):
  - New key `breakpoint.v1.setups` (JSON, `{ v: 1, freeFight: { type, crowd, slots: [{ id, human, team }], deal, handicap, clock, rounds, roundTimer, arena, difficulty, friendlyFire } }`), written when the first screen is left to start a fight or to go back. At start-up (after `Settings.init`) the file is only read raw with `readJson` and kept as it is: `Progress.init` runs before `CustomRoster.init`, so a custom fighter would look unknown and be replaced on every launch. The checking happens on first use (the first time Free Fight opens or a save is made, after `CustomRoster.init`), the way `Game.savedTournament` cleans its raw copy. Every field is checked then (`MatchLineup.fix`, known level, known difficulty, rounds 1 to 3, a person slot whose fighter is deleted or not unlocked gets the first unlocked fighter, a CPU slot whose fighter is deleted gets a known one); an unreadable, unknown-version or hostile entry falls back to the defaults field by field, never throws and never touches `Progress`. P18-05 adds `arcade`, `survival` and `tournament` entries (difficulty only) to the same object, so "saved per mode" is one place. Kept apart from `Progress` so a damaged setup can never cost a record.
  - Old saves: no `setups` key reads as the defaults above, except `freeFight.friendlyFire` which starts as the old Friendly fire value. That copy is done once at start-up, not at the first Free Fight visit: `Settings.save` writes only keys that are still in `SETTING_DEFS`, so the first change of any setting after the Settings row is removed would drop the old value. At start-up, when the `setups` key is missing, P18-19 reads `friendlyFire` raw from `breakpoint.v1.settings` and writes the new key at once (defaults plus that value); from then on the key exists and the old one may vanish from the settings file without loss (a downgrade then reads Friendly fire as off). `Progress.init` folds `grades.free`, `grades.brawl` and `grades.coop` into `grades.free` with `betterGrade` (the best letter survives) and `GRADE_MODES` becomes arcade, survival, free, tournament; `brawl`, `coop` and a stray `versus` key are dropped on the next save. Wins, losses, Arcade, Survival and Tournament records, unlocks and the "seen" flags are untouched (none is keyed by these modes). The mode list shows "Best grade A" under Free Fight (`modeRecord`).
  - Unlocks screen: the best-grade line lists `Free Fight A` only (today it would read `MODE_INFO.brawl.label` of a removed mode and throw: this is a trap, see below).
  - `Game.versus` (the Versus score): reset when Free Fight is entered from the mode list, kept across rematches and "Change fighters" while the fight is a two-people 1v1, shown in the HUD line and on the Versus results as today.
- Grades and unlocks: a Free Fight with at least one CPU fighter is graded as Free Fight when a person's team wins (Duel, Brawl and Custom lineups as `data.stats[0]`, the crowd with a grade per player and the best one saved, as today), saved in `grades.free`. A fight with no CPU (Versus, four people) has no grade, as the Versus has none. Free Fight credits no unlock counter (Arcade and Survival do), as before.
- Tournament and online reuse: the setup is plain data (`freeFight` above plus the level and difficulty) turned into a `cfg` by one function (P18-19 extracts it from `startFree` / `startVersus` / `startLineup` / `startCoop`); the Tournament bout and the P12 host build the same `cfg`. The Tournament keeps its join screen and bracket; it reuses the Difficulty row builder (same name and values, P18-05) and Entrants stays its own row. A bout is the plain 1v1 with the "Tournament bout" rules (P18-42's preset). Online (P12) adds a third control value for a slot (a remote person) next to Player and CPU and the same rows; nothing in the saved shape needs to change for it.
- Edge cases:

  | Case | Result |
  |---|---|
  | 1 person vs 5 CPU | Brawl type with Opponents 5: 6 slots, person on Team A against 5 on Team B; allowed. Even teams uses the ratio-4 factor (1 v 5 was never measured, P4-08); the clock is optional. |
  | 4 people, no CPU | a 4-way free-for-all (or 2v2 or 3 against 1 by the Teams row); Difficulty and Rounds dimmed; Even teams dimmed only for the free-for-all and for equal teams (with 3 against 1 it stays, `MatchLineup.plan` still gives the smaller team the Hard-column bonus with no CPU); no grade; Start off with the reason until 4 gamepads, or 3 gamepads and keyboard 1, or 2 gamepads and both keyboards are connected; no touch. |
  | All CPU (watch) | not offered: slot 1 is always a person (HUD, camera, pause, results all key off the lead person). Decided: a watch mode is a separate later task; `BREAKPOINT.simulate` already runs all-CPU lineups headless. |
  | Uneven teams | unchanged: Even teams On by default; the hint says in plain words what it gives; a person is never weakened. |
  | Everyone on one team | refused with "Everyone is on one team: nobody to fight" (as today); Start off. |
  | Two people on the same team in a 2-slot lineup | not a 1v1: plays as a lineup and is refused as above. |
  | Crowd, 1 to 4 people | enemies 3 to 6; a crowd of 1 starts exactly like the plain brawl (the old test); waves on a level that has them; Time limit and teams rows dimmed. |
  | Switching type after edits | the preset replaces slots, deal and crowd; kept: the fighters of slot 1 (and 2), Level, Difficulty, Rounds, both time limits, Friendly fire. |
  | Locked or deleted fighter in a saved setup | replaced on read as above; never starts with a missing fighter. |
  | Gamepad unplugged on the setup screen or in the fight | the existing `padsChanged` handling: the reason line updates, Start turns off; in the fight the seat keeps its pad id as before. |
  | Mobile one-hand (390x844, 844x390) | first screen: 5 rows + 2 sticky buttons, no horizontal scroll, each row at least the existing tap height; the heavy screens (grid, More options) scroll with the sticky bottom row; touch plays one person; a second person on a phone needs a keyboard or gamepad (the hint line says so; no blocking, the device cannot be told). |
  | Rematch / Change fighters / Main menu | "Play again" repeats the same `cfg` (lineup, crowd, level); "Change fighters" opens slot 1's card screen (Versus type: slot 1, then slot 2) and Confirm returns to the first screen with the setup kept (`GS.CHARSEL` shows `freefight` for this mode); the rematch repeats the setup as it stands (Friendly fire from its row), a change to the rules is made on More options after Change fighters; Main menu clears nothing saved. |
  | Rules that cannot go together (P18-42) | not decided here; P18-42 lists refused combinations and the rows dim with a reason, the same way. |

- Self-tests that change (names start as in `breakpoint-tests.js` at 794e348, the number is the line of the `test(` call; fix to the new flow, never weaken):
  - "Versus: two player controllers and no AI..." (2554, enters Versus by `chooseMode('versus')`).
  - "Fight grade (P10-07)" (2647, `GRADE_MODES` and the `coop` / `brawl` grade keys) and "Fight grade: a real match end grades a Free Fight, a menu Brawl or Free Fight lineup..." (2719, `grades.brawl / coop / versus`).
  - "Co-op setting: Friendly fire is off by default, is kept in the save..." (6411: now the Free Fight row, the setups key and the start-up seed from the old setting).
  - "Co-op flow: the mode is on the list..." (6430) and "Co-op screens and HUD" (6458; the join-screen half moves to the Tournament).
  - "Lineup: a lineup is made safe and checked" (6516: `check` loses its mode argument, "Versus needs two players" goes, "a crowd of 1 to 4 people" comes), "Lineup: the lineup screen shows a row per slot" (6768), "Lineup: the setup screens say that Rounds and Round timer are not used" (6809), "Lineup flow: Brawl is on the mode list..." (6890): one lineup `free`, the new screens.
  - The Tournament join-screen tests that set `selection.mode = 'coop'` or call `screen_coop`: "Tournament pick (P18-41): on the Tournament join screen a seat card..." (11119), "Tournament (P11-01): Continue plays the CPU fights..." (11177), "Tournament (P11-02): Continue tournament on a saved bracket of 3 people..." (11223), "Tournament (P11-02): a saved bracket counts only the people still in it..." (11335): use `'tournament'`.
  - Seven "Mode flows (P18-40)" tests: Free Fight (11677), Versus (11720), Brawl (the test at 11763), Co-op Brawl (11792), "Brawl for three people on a keyboard and two gamepads and Versus on two gamepads" (12108), "a lineup that cannot start" (12132), "on the Tournament and Co-op join screens the arrow keys..." (12216; its co-op half goes).
  - Mode flows that only pass through Free Fight: "Back and Esc from every screen of every mode..." (11979), "Controls with the Move list, Settings, Unlocks, About and the fighter creator open from ... the fighter screen" (12008: Free Fight's card screen and the creator return), and the seeded random walk "Mode flows (P18-40): seeded random walks through every screen..." (12388; it sets `Settings.v.friendlyFire` in its settings object, line 12357: use the Free Fight row, and it presses `mode:versus / brawl / coop`).
  - "Player-facing text (P17-05)" (12513): now also opens the new screens.
  - About eight tests start a plain Free Fight with `Game.chooseMode('free'); Game.startFromSelection()` (lines 2342 to 2523 and 2395). Once the last-used type is saved they could start a Versus or a Brawl. Decided: `chooseMode('free')` starts from the saved setup, and the tests start from none (their harness resets the setups key and `Game.lineups` first, as the lineup tests already do), so they still get the default Duel with the old config and the old fingerprints. P18-19 must grep every reference (`chooseMode(`, `lineupFor(`, `lineupPlain(`, `startCoop`, `startVersus`, `MODE_INFO.`, `SETUP_LABEL`, `selection.mode`, `Settings.v.friendlyFire`, `grades.`) in both files and fix each one; this list is a start, not the whole list.
- Self-tests P18-19 must add (names start `Free Fight merge (P18-19):`):
  1. the mode list is Arcade, Survival, Training, Free Fight, Tournament, and no screen, label or HUD line the tests can open says Versus, Brawl or Co-op Brawl except as a fight type name;
  2. each fight type sets the lineup of the old mode and `startFromSelection` builds the config the old start function built (mode, ids, slots, teams, rounds, timer, level, difficulty, crowd party and enemy count), and a seeded Duel, Versus and Co-op hash equals the old recorded one;
  3. the label follows the shape: a fighter swap keeps it, flipping a Duel's slot 2 to Player shows Versus, adding a slot to a Duel shows Custom or Brawl by its deal, the crowd flag shows Co-op; choosing a type again restores the preset and keeps the slot 1 and 2 picks, Level, Difficulty, Rounds and the time limits;
  4. the rows of the first screen and of More options, in order, with the dimmed "Not used" reasons (no CPU, a lineup, a 1v1, the crowd, equal teams), Even teams still active for 3 people against 1 with no CPU, at most 5 rows on the first screen, Start off with the reason line;
  5. the setups key: written, read back through a fresh `Store`, a damaged or hostile entry and an old version fall back field by field, a custom fighter in a slot survives a reload (read raw at start-up, cleaned only after `CustomRoster.init`), a deleted custom fighter and a locked fighter in a person slot are replaced, nothing throws;
  6. old saves: grades `free: B, brawl: A, coop: S` load as `free: S`; an old settings file with friendly fire true seeds the row On at start-up and the new key is written at once, also when any Setting is changed (and saved) before the first Free Fight visit; a save with neither loads the defaults; wins, losses, Arcade, Survival and Tournament numbers are equal before and after;
  7. friendly fire: Off nobody hurts a teammate; On a person hurts a person and a CPU teammate on Team A and on Team B, a CPU never hurts a teammate, the crowd keeps the players-only rule, a thrown body follows, a rematch follows the row, and a person never hurts themselves with their own smoke cloud or crate burst when it is On;
  8. the input plans: 1 person solo with touch (also in the crowd), 2 people the Versus plan, 3 and 4 people on pads only when there are enough pads (P1 on the first pad), keyboard 1 plus pads otherwise, keyboard 2 only when still short, the reason text when devices are missing, the plan is solo while the setup screens are open;
  9. grades: a Duel, a Brawl and a crowd win grade as Free Fight, a no-CPU fight has none, the Unlocks screen opens with the grades line and does not throw;
  10. the edge cases above: 1 vs 5 starts with 6 fighters, 4 people with no CPU is a free-for-all and is refused with too few pads, everyone on one team is refused, slot 1 cannot be a CPU, the crowd runs for 1 to 4 people;
  11. flows (replace the seven Mode flows listed above): Duel through Start, a clock result, Rematch, Change fighters (the setup is kept) and Main menu; Versus on two keyboards, score carried over; Brawl with three people on a keyboard and two gamepads; Co-op with a keyboard and a gamepad, paused and resumed with the pad, a win and a loss; the Level list opened from the first screen and left by Back and by a second tap without starting; Start on a gamepad starts from the first screen; Change fighters opens the cards of slot 1 (slot 1 then 2 in the Versus type) and returns to the first screen;
  12. the 390x844 and 844x390 layout check of the three new screens (no horizontal scroll, the sticky row visible), in the browser run.
- Where P18-19 will find it hard (read before starting): (a) `Game.lineups` is keyed by mode and tested by mode in several places; a single `free` lineup is the plan, so grep `lineupFor(`, `lineupPlain(`, `lineupProblem(` and `startLineup(`. (b) `UI.back()` and `Game.go(GS.CHARSEL)` choose the screen by `selection.mode` and `lineupPlain`; the new screens need their own branches (`char` returns to `lineup`, `lineup` and `freemore` to `freefight`, `arena` to `freefight`). (c) `Game.inputPlan()` and `syncVersusInput()` read `selection.mode` ('versus' / 'coop'); they must read the people count of the setup instead (and the crowd's party). (d) `MatchSim` builds the crowd from `cfg.coop` with seats `slot` 0 to 3 and `Inputs.forFighter` only maps them when `Inputs.coop` is on; with 1 or 2 people the plan is solo or Versus, so the fighter-to-person mapping for `cfg.coop` must also work there (fighters 0 and 1 to `Input` and `Input2`), or the crowd must be given `slots`-style persons. (e) `screen_unlocks` and `Progress.init` iterate `GRADE_MODES` and `MODE_INFO[mode].label`: remove the modes from both in the same change or the Unlocks screen throws. (f) The friendly-fire change touches `canHurt`, used by strikes, grabs, tackles, thrown bodies and the AI target choice; run the Group AI and the thrown-body tests. (g) The crowd is 4 + 6 = 10 fighters, above `LINEUP_MAX` 8; keep it on the party path, do not raise `LINEUP_MAX`. (h) `beginMatch` reads `Settings.v.friendlyFire` for `cfg.coop` and `restartMatch` deletes `items`, `hazards`, `ringOut`, `ring` to re-read Settings; the rematch must also re-read the Free Fight row, not the removed Settings key. (i) Size budget: the file is 1,184,374 bytes (LF, BOM) of 1,200,000; the merge removes a screen and two start functions and adds two screens: aim for a net decrease. (j) `Inputs.autoDevices` changes (pads-only deal) and its reason text with it; `UI.pickKey()`, `rivalId()` and the creator return (`CreatorUI` writes `Game.selection[UI.pickKey()]`) must address a slot. (k) The Free Fight setup file is read raw at start-up and cleaned after `CustomRoster.init`; the one-time friendly-fire copy runs at start-up.
- Rejected: keeping Versus, Brawl and Co-op Brawl as list rows that open Free Fight preset (it keeps eight rows and the owner asked for one mode); folding the crowd and waves into the lineup engine now (see above); a single flat screen with every row (a phone would scroll 14 rows before Start); one "Rules" screen shared by everything (P18-42 owns the rules); keeping the join screen for Free Fight (the auto deal already does Brawl and Versus); aliasing `chooseMode('versus' | 'brawl' | 'coop')` for the tests (dead code in the game; the tests use the fight type call instead); persisting the setup of every fight type apart (one setup, one label is simpler and what the owner asked for); a watch mode with no person (see edge cases).
- Decided, with the owner able to change each (none of these blocks P18-19): (1) a watch mode (all CPU) is a later task, slot 1 stays a person. (2) Devices: the join screen is gone from Free Fight; the pads-only deal above settles odd mixes, a "Devices" row can be asked for later. (3) Friendly fire in a lineup applies to people on every team, not only the first (because a team-mate fair-play option should not favour the lead's team); say if only the first team should have it. (4) The best grades of Free Fight, Brawl and Co-op are pooled into one best letter (because a Free Fight is now all of them). (5) The crowd keeps no clock (as the old Co-op); Co-op defaults to 1 person when the last device used was touch, else 2 (because a phone cannot seat a second person). No question is open that stops P18-19 from starting.

### Raised ground (P18-44) (2026-11-05)

- Owner, 2026-11-04 16:16: attacks, grabs and item use miss or cannot be used when fighters go up to a raised part of a level (steps and stairs to upper levels, scaffolding, the ring in the Gym). Decided by the task's delegate under the owner's standing go (rule: keep things balanced; the floor stays exactly as it was).
- Cause: `Combat.evaluate`, `Combat.tackle` and the CPU's view of its foe (`AIController.observe`) read the foe's and the attacker's HEIGHT ABOVE THE FLOOR (`y`), not above their own footing (`groundY`). On a deck, a stair step or the ring every fighter is at `y` = the footing, so a grab (needs `y` below 0.5 and 0.3), a Low strike (`y` below 0.35), a Mid strike (`y` below 0.9) and a tackle (`y` below 0.3 / 0.5) all failed, and the CPU never grabbed or kept to Low strikes. The same rows let a fighter on the floor hit one on a 2.5 m mezzanine (and the reverse), because nothing compared the two footings.
- Rule (one function, `Combat.evaluate`; `g` = the foe's footing minus the attacker's, in m; a floor fight has `g` = 0, so nothing on the floor changed):

  | Action | Reaches when |
  |---|---|
  | Any strike, a stomp on a downed foe, a weapon swing | `|g|` is at most 1.05 (`STRIKE_LEDGE`: a ring, stage or dock of 1.0 m counts, a tank, catwalk, scaffold tier or mezzanine of 1.5 m and more does not) |
  | Low strike (and the stomp) | the foe's feet are at most 0.35 above the attacker's footing (`LOW_REACH_UP`, the old limit now measured from the attacker's own footing; a foe a step up is reached, two steps up is not; a foe below always is) |
  | Mid strike | the foe's feet are at most 0.9 above the attacker's footing (`MID_REACH_UP`; a juggled foe excepted, as before) |
  | High strike | within the strike ledge only |
  | Grab (and its throw, the clinch strikes) | `|g|` is at most 0.55 (`GRAB_LEDGE`, two stair steps) and the foe is not more than 0.5 above its own footing (a jump), the attacker not more than 0.3 |
  | Tackle | the same as a grab |
  | Ground hold | unchanged: the two fighters within 0.6 of each other (`SUBMIT_LEVEL_GAP`) |
  | Pick up | unchanged (P17-06): from 0.6 below to 2.2 above the fighter's feet, all measured from its own footing |
  | Thrown item | unchanged: it flies and lands by the level's solids. The foe at the foot of a 2.5 m mezzanine is covered by its lip, a ring's ropes stop an item; on a stair, a stage, a dock and a scaffold tier on the same level it hits |

- The CPU sees its foe as `opp.y - f.groundY` (`AIController.observe`); `snap.y` keeps its two uses (a Low move is rated 0.1 above 0.4, a grab is considered below 0.3).
- Effects: a stomp, clinch, throw, escape, hold, tackle, KO, impact, wall slam and fall-damage effect were placed at a fixed height above the FLOOR (0.2 to 1.1 m), so on a deck they were drawn inside it; they now sit on the fighter (`def.y + height`). Event payload only; the simulation does not read it.
- Not covered: crates, tables and benches a fighter can jump onto (they break under the first strike, which then changes the footing mid-test); hazards (they stand on the floor and cover the floor only, `HAZARD_FLOOR_TOP`); the flight and landing of a THROWN body (other task).
- Self-tests (section "Raised ground (P18-44)", names start `Raised ground (P18-44):`): 11 tests over 99 runs (every deck, stair, scaffold tier and ring of the 8 levels, read from the built colliders, with both on it, one above and one below, and on two steps of every stair).

### P18-02 combat timing decided (2026-11-06)

- Owner (2026-11-01): "clearer combo breaks, animations and perfect block; timing consistent and intuitive". Decided by the coordinator's delegate under the owner's standing go; the owner can change any line ("Decided: X (because ...); owner can change"). This task changed no game code and no test. Everything measured here is from commit 794e348 and headless probes (the game script run in a stub browser: the real `MatchSim`, scripted fighters, and the P0-06 runner `SimRunner.run` with AI on both sides). The probes were temp files; each method is described so P18-03 can turn it into a self-test.
- Words used (all in simulation ticks, 60 a second; hit-stop freezes the whole fight and no fighter ages in it, so none of the counts below is touched by it):
  - Contact tick C: the tick a strike goes active on a foe and lands. Stun R: the ticks the foe is held (`reactDur`, or `blockStun`). Last stunned tick: C + R. Free tick F = C + R + 1: the first tick in which the foe's own input counts (a held Defend starts the guard on F, a Defend pressed up to 6 ticks before F is used on F).
  - Gap g of a follow-up: the tick N its strike goes active minus (C + R). g <= 0: it lands while the foe is still held (a TRUE link: nothing can stop it). g = 1: it lands on F itself. g = k: the foe has had k - 1 free ticks first.
  - Chain press offset k: the tick the next string step starts, counted from C (k = 1 is the earliest, the tick after contact). That step goes active k + its startup + 1 ticks after C.

**Step 1 - what the code does (measured, nothing changed yet)**

- The rules as the code has them:

  | Thing | Now |
  |---|---|
  | Hit stun | R = round(move hit-stun x the foe's Balance stun stat (0.75 to 1.2) x 1.3 when the foe was in its own startup or active frames (counter hit) x max(0.6, 1 - 0.07 x hits already landed in this combo)); a stagger move x 1.15 on top; knockdown and launch moves have no stun (the foe flies). The foe cannot block, sway, attack or move for R ticks, then is back to IDLE. The hit pose fades out over the same R ticks (`animate` reads `reactDur`), so the pose is back at guard on the free tick |
  | Block stun | round(move block-stun x the same stun stat). The blocker stays in the guard and cannot sway, attack, move or let go for those ticks, so a step that lands inside it cannot be avoided. Guard-break moves (all 8 strong moves, tier 3) and an empty tank break any guard that is not perfect: 80 ticks of STUN (a flat length), 30 % of the damage as chip, then the next hit lands at x 1.2 and replaces the STUN with a normal hit stun |
  | Frame advantage (Training panel, `frameAdvantage`) | stun - (active - 1 + recovery), after speed scaling: same tick of contact, so it is exactly how many ticks earlier the attacker is free (negative: the foe is) |
  | Chain cancel window | once a move connected (hit or block) the next buffered press cancels it from offset 1 up to offset (active - 1 + floor(0.6 x recovery)): jab 6, cross 10, roundhouse 15, strong moves 22. A press after that waits for the end of the whole move and the next step starts 5 to 9 ticks later than the last cancel would have. String rows (3 steps at most) ignore the tier rule; free chains (`canChain`) need an equal or higher tier, end on a strong move and stop after 4 chained presses |
  | Combo counter | counts per attacker on one foe (`gotHits`); ends 12 ticks after the foe was last in a hit reaction (`COMBO_RESET_TICKS`), or when another attacker hits the foe. It counts hits with free ticks between them (any gap up to 12), so a COMBO of 3 is not always a true combo. Damage x `COMBO_SCALE` from the 3rd hit (0.9 down to 0.5), stun x 0.93 per earlier hit down to x 0.6 |
  | Perfect block | a guard that has JUST STARTED (`blockFresh`: the fighter went from a non-guard state into the guard, not Block to Crouch-Block, and the previous guard started at least 30 ticks earlier: `PERFECT_BLOCK_COOLDOWN`) is perfect for strikes that land while it is at most 6 ticks old: 7 ticks, a Defend pressed on the contact tick itself counts (the documentation says 6). "Just started" does not need a press: the window counts from the tick the guard really starts, so a Defend that is already held when the fighter becomes free (end of a hit stun, a recovery, a get-up) starts a fresh guard on that first free tick, and a strike landing within 7 ticks of it is perfect (measured, the g table below) |
  | Perfect sway | the strike lands on tick 6 to 11 of the 12-tick dodge, i.e. the Defend was pressed 6 to 11 ticks BEFORE contact (a press 0 to 5 before is a plain dodge, 12 or more is a hit). A press while busy is kept 6 ticks (`SWAY_BUFFER_TICKS`); needs 10 stamina or the press does nothing |
  | What a perfect gives | block: the attacker staggers 26 ticks and is pushed back, the defender has no block stun, a counter window of 50 ticks for one counter move, +20 momentum, +6 stamina (Easy 8, Normal 7 for the people's side). Sway: counter window 50, +20 momentum, +0 stamina (Easy 5, Normal 3); the attacker is not staggered |
  | Not covered by the window | grabs (a sway does not dodge them, Block does not stop them: the clinch has its own 12 / 14-tick escape rules), the tackle (Block stops it for 10 ticks of block stun and the tackler is locked 24 ticks, never perfect; a sway or sidestep runs it past), thrown items (the same window for a Block, a perfect one costs nothing; a sway in its dodge ticks avoids them, never perfect), thrown bodies and hazards (no block, no sway) |

- Frame advantage of every strike move (base fighter: Speed 5, Balance 5; "hit / block"; the weapon rows are for the weapon classes):

  | Group | Moves (on hit / on block) |
  |---|---|
  | Tier 1 (6): on hit -1 to +4, on block -2 to -7 | jab +4 / -2, chain punch +4 / -2, body jab +3 / -3, low kick +1 / -5, front kick -1 / -7, teep -1 / -7 |
  | Tier 2 (13): -3 to +3, -4 to -12 | cross 0 / -7, trap strike +3 / -4, angle 2 +2 / -5, parry strike +1 / -6, elbow -1 / -8, angle 1 -1 / -8, overhand -1 / -8, spin heel -2 / -10, jump round kick -2 / -12, roundhouse -3 / -12, body kick -3 / -12, spin back kick -3 / -12, uppercut launch / -10 |
  | Tier 3 (8, all guard-break): -5 to +8, -6 to -20 | power jab +7 / -6, gut punch +8 / -7, hook 0 / -12, push kick +1 / -13, heavy body kick -5 / -20; knockdown or launch (no stun): sweep / -15, heavy roundhouse / -20, heavy uppercut / -17 |
  | Context moves | running lunge (tier 3, no guard break) +1 / -12, flying kick +5 / -3, air strike +5 / -2, counter strike +15 / 0, counter kick (knockdown) / -1 |
  | Weapons (`WEAPON_MOVES`) | light (tier 1 and 2 steps) +1 / -4, medium, heavy and two-handed steps -1 / -10, strong stagger steps 0 to +11 / -15, strong knockdown steps / -15; a thrown item stuns 16 ticks and a thrown body 24, both x Balance and flat (no combo scaling, no counter); a bottle adds 10 |

  Balance moves every number: a foe with Balance 10 (stun x 0.75) turns a jab from +4 into +1, a foe with Balance 1 (x 1.2) into +7; Speed 10 (attack speed x 0.875) adds 1 to 2 on hit and block, Speed 1 takes the same away. A tank under 25 % makes the attacker's startup and recovery x 1.1, an empty tank adds 3 startup and damage x 0.6, so links get looser (a brake, not a rule).

- The perfect window on every move (the probe: for each strike move find C against an idle foe, then press Defend d ticks before C; Block foe holds Defend, Crouch for low moves; Sway foe taps once). The same row came out for all 30 strike moves of `ATTACKS` (the two air strikes use the same line of code and were not probed), and for a foe with any Balance, Speed or style, because the window counts the defender's own ticks:

  | d (ticks before contact) | Block foe | Sway foe |
  |---|---|---|
  | -2, -1 (after the hit) | hit | hit |
  | 0 to 5 | PERFECT | plain sway (dodged, no counter) |
  | 6 | PERFECT | PERFECT |
  | 7 to 11 | plain block (guard break by a strong move) | PERFECT |
  | 12 and more | plain block | hit (the dodge is over) |

  So in the code as it is, the Block window is 7 ticks (0.117 s) and the Sway window 6 ticks (0.1 s), and they sit at opposite ends: Block rewards pressing LATE, Sway pressing EARLY; a press 2 ticks before the hit is perfect for a blocker and only a plain dodge for a swayer (the HIGH finding of the review; decided below). Taking a human reaction to the first tick of a wind-up as 12 to 15 ticks (0.2 to 0.25 s, an estimate), a perfect block can be got by reaction only against moves of startup 11 to 17 (at 12 ticks: hook, heavy uppercut, push kick, sweep, roundhouse, body kick, spin back kick, jump round kick) or 14 to 20 (at 15: push kick, heavy uppercut, heavy roundhouse, heavy body kick, jump round kick), and a perfect sway (press 6 to 11 early) against none at 15 ticks and only heavy uppercut, heavy roundhouse and heavy body kick at 12. The sway help text says "a hit dodged at the last moment", which reads as pressing late (that is a plain dodge). The counter of a perfect block lands 14 ticks after contact, the counter of a perfect sway 26 to 31 ticks after (16 to 21 sway ticks are still left), so a perfect sway punishes only moves that keep the attacker busy for 26+ ticks after contact (the strong ones).

- What the foe can do when a follow-up lands g ticks after the last stunned tick (the probe: jab lands, then a cross is started so it goes active at g; foe KAI-class; the same after any hit because g is counted from the stun):

  | g | Nothing | Block held through the stun | Sway tapped 6 to 1 ticks before F (each tick later shifts the column by one) | A jab pressed on the free tick |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | -2 to 0 | hit | hit (the guard cannot start) | hit | hit |
  | 1 to 4 | hit | PERFECT block | plain sway | the cross lands first (counter hit on the jab) |
  | 5 | hit | PERFECT block | plain sway | both land (trade) |
  | 6 | hit | PERFECT block | plain sway | the jab lands first (counter hit) |
  | 7 | hit | PERFECT block | PERFECT sway | the jab lands first |
  | 8 to 12 | hit | plain block (a strong step: guard break) | PERFECT sway | the jab lands first |
  | 13 and more | hit | plain block | hit (sway too early) | the jab lands first |

  In the code as it is, Block through the stun is therefore already a combo breaker for tight gaps (1 to 7) and Sway for loose ones (7 to 12), and the Block fighter who just holds Defend gets the perfect without pressing anything (the guard starts on F); a strong step with g 8 to 11 breaks the guard of a Block fighter and is answered only by a sway. Nothing in the game tells the player any of this. The aligned Sway column (decided below, checked on a patched copy of the extracted script in the temp folder, never in the game file): g 1 to 7 PERFECT sway, 8 to 12 plain dodge, 13 and more hit, i.e. the same 1 to 7 as the Block column.

- Links in the string tables (42 distinct rows over the 16 style tables; "hit n" = the n-th hit of the string; g at the earliest and the latest cancel press, base fighter): ALWAYS TRUE (g <= 0 at every press; 3 rows): chain punch > trap strike (Wing Chun, both as hit 1 and hit 2) and jab > parry strike (Krav Maga); jab > cross and jab > Kali angle 1 are true at every press against a foe with Balance 4 or less and to offset 5 of 6 against Balance 5. TRUE IF PRESSED PROMPTLY (29 rows): cross > hook (true only to offset 2 of 10), front kick > roundhouse (3 of 10), front kick > jab (9), roundhouse > jab (13 of 15), roundhouse > power jab (9), crouch body jab > uppercut (3 of 7), crouch low kick > body kick (2 of 8), crouch body kick > sweep (7 of 15), jab > hook for Wrestler / Judoka / Hapkido and parry strike > hook and trap strike > push kick (offset 1 only), cross > elbow (6), cross > overhand (4), teep > roundhouse (3), teep > jab (9), jump round kick > cross (11), roundhouse > spin back kick (6), angle 1 > angle 2 (6), a heavy body kick > any light step (18 to 21 of 22: a stagger of 28 ticks). NEVER TRUE (g >= 1 even at the earliest press, 10 rows): every step into a slow strong move or the jump round kick: jab > heavy body kick and jab > heavy uppercut (g 6 to 11), cross > push kick (1 to 10), roundhouse > heavy roundhouse (1 to 15), front kick > heavy roundhouse (5 to 14), jab > jump round kick (2 to 7), angle 1 / parry strike > push kick (1 to 11 / 2 to 10), chain punch > heavy body kick (6 to 11), and the crouch uppercut > gut punch (a juggle, not a stun). Over all 402 legal free-chain pairs after a first hit: 20 always true, 327 true if prompt, 55 never true (all into startup 14+ moves); after a third hit (stun x 0.86): 4 / 292 / 106, so a long chain loosens by itself.
- Chains with no way out (the P0-06 question for option A): AI against AI, 12 seeds a row, `evenDamage: false`, hits with no free tick between them on one foe, any attacker: 18 to 39 % of all hits (1v1 Normal 39, Hard 37, Expert 30; 1v3 Normal 21, Expert 20; 1v6 Hard 18). The longest unbroken run was 4 hits (Normal 1v1, 1v3, Expert 1v3, Hard 1v6), 5 (Expert 1v1) and 7 once (Hard 1v1, 34 hp); a typical 4-run is 16 to 31 hp, the worst 50 hp (a counter-hit heavy body kick, 26.7, then three more) of a 100 to 135 tank. A scripted attacker that presses as fast as the rules allow, on all 16 move styles with 7 press patterns and endless stamina and foe health, reached 4 locked hits at best (19.9 hp: Karateka, Capoeira and Taekwondo with kick, punch, punch pressed on and on) and 3 for most styles (up to 25.5 hp); the 3-step string cap, the chain cap of 4 presses, the strong move that ends a chain and the stun scaling (x 0.6 at the 7th hit) stop it. The air has its own cap: `JUGGLE_LIMIT` 3, one ground hit, a get-up that cannot be hit. Of the follow-ups that arrive after the foe had been free, 10 to 26 % arrive within 7 ticks of the free tick (the gaps a held Block turns into a perfect), 1 to 4 % at 8 to 12, the rest later than 12.
- Block and perfect counts in the same fights (block plus sway events, perfect among them): 1v1 Normal 36 / 2, Hard 117 / 47, Expert 185 / 135; 1v3 Normal 11 / 0, Expert 324 / 181; 1v6 Hard 187 / 69. A perfect inside an open combo (the foe had `gotHits` > 0): 2, 9, 8, 16 (1v3 Expert) and 12 (Sway 1v1 Hard) in those fights, and in none of them did a hit follow that was still counted into the same combo. A plain block inside an open combo is common (24 of 34 blocks at 1v1 Normal) and 17 of those 24 were followed by a hit that continued the count (5 to 30 such blocks per 12 fights in the other setups).

**Step 2 - decided**

- DECIDE (1) combo break. Decided: option B. No combo-break input is added; the ways out that exist are named and made readable. Because: (a) P1-03 accepts nothing that adds an input, and a touch layout has no spare button; (b) the P0-06 runner shows no chain without a way out: the longest run is 4 hits (7 once), 16 to 31 hp, and every link with g >= 1 is already answered by the foe's own Defend: a held Block or a tapped Sway is perfect for g 1 to 7 (Sway after the alignment below), a plain block or dodge above (see the table); (c) option A would need a stamina price, a window and an animation of its own, and would collide with the perfect block it is meant to feel like. Rejected: A (a defender input in hit stun that costs stamina), and a combo break that works inside the stun (it would turn every true link into a coin flip and wreck P14-01). Condition for reopening: the owner finds a chain that cannot be escaped on a phone. Owner can change.
- DECIDE (2) window and cooldown. Decided: ONE perfect window for both defences, 7 ticks: the strike lands on guard age 0 to 6 (Block) or on sway tick 0 to 6 (Sway), i.e. the defence started at most 6 ticks before the hit, by a press or by a Defend held into the free tick. Block and the 30-tick cooldown are unchanged; the Sway window changes (the only number of this log that moves): perfect on sway ticks 0 to 6, plain dodge on 7 to 11 (the 12-tick i-frames and the 15-tick recovery stay), one constant (`PERFECT_BLOCK_TICKS` 6, the highest age that counts, documented as 7 ticks everywhere it is written) read by `Combat.evaluate`, `itemBlock` and the sway test, and `SWAY_PERFECT_TICKS` goes. Because: (a) the owner asked for consistent, intuitive timing and P18-03 asks that the same press timing gives the same result everywhere; as the code stands a press 2 ticks early is perfect for a blocker and a plain dodge for a swayer, and a perfect sway cannot be got by reaction (measured above) while a perfect block can; (b) with the window aligned, "press Defend just before the hit" is the one sentence for both, the escape table has one perfect band (g 1 to 7) for both, and the reaction lists of the perfect block hold for the sway. Owner can change. The numbers are from a patched copy (the temp folder; 27 strike moves, identical rows): Sway perfect at press 0 to 6 before contact, plain at 7 to 11, hit from 12.
- DECIDE (2b) the counter after a perfect sway. Decided: a perfect sway ends the sway early, at sway tick 21 instead of 27 (a plain sway keeps its 27), a new constant `SWAY_PERFECT_FREE_TICK` 21, so the swayer may counter 6 ticks sooner. Because: without the cut the counter of a perfect sway comes at 31 to 37 ticks after contact (aligned window, measured: 37, 34, 31 at sway tick 0, 3, 6), 5 to 11 ticks later than today's 26 to 31, which would make it miss the medium moves it punishes now and tilt the Block vs Sway split towards Block; with the cut it lands at 31, 28, 25 (25 to 31, today's band) and the punish reach and the tuning of P3-02 are kept. A perfect block's counter stays at 14. The attacker is not staggered by a sway (unchanged). The i-frames end at tick 12 as before, so the swayer can be hit from tick 12 to 21 as it can today; the drawn lean must be upright by tick 21 on a perfect sway (cosmetic). Owner can change.
- DECIDE (2c) held Defend. Decided: a perfect block is "the guard starts, by a press or by a Defend already held when the fighter becomes free, and the strike lands within 7 ticks of the start"; a Block fighter who keeps Defend held through a hit stun therefore gets a perfect on a follow-up with g 1 to 7 (and a Sway fighter the same by tapping within the 6 ticks before F, the press being kept 6 ticks). Because it is what the code does for both people, it is what the escape table and the combo break rest on, and it is the cheapest way out on a touch screen; it is a feature and it is named in the help, Training reading and tests. Whether the CPU should do it: Decided: no CPU level holds Defend through a stun (it does not today: the AI gives no input in a hit reaction), because it would hand every Hard and Expert CPU a perfect on 10 to 26 % of the follow-ups it takes (the share within 7 ticks, measured above), move the whole P14-01 ladder and every fingerprint, and the CPU already has its own reaction block, `parry` and `swayAim` rolls; two people against each other have the same tool, so two-player fairness holds. If the owner wants the CPU to do it, it comes as one more difficulty field (`holdGuard`: Easy 0, Normal 0, Hard 0.25, Expert 0.5 as a start) with its own P14-01 run. Owner can change.
- The rules, measured value against decided value:

  | Rule | Measured now | Decided (the player's sentence) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Hit stun | as in the table above | Unchanged. "While you are hit you cannot act; your pose is back at guard on the tick you are free." |
  | Block stun | as above | Unchanged. "While you absorb a block you cannot let go, sway or attack." |
  | Advantage bands | hit tier 1 -1..+4, tier 2 -3..+3, tier 3 -5..+8; block tier 1 -7..-2, tier 2 -12..-4, tier 3 -20..-6 | Ratified as the bands for every present and new move: on block every move except the two counter moves (they exist only after a perfect) is -2 or lower at the base fighter and every strong move -6 or lower (the test pins -5, the jab startup); a new move stays in the band of its tier. |
  | True link | not defined; the combo counter allows 12 free ticks | Decided: a link is TRUE when the next strike goes active on or before the last stunned tick (g <= 0), and that is the only definition. Light and medium steps are meant to be true when pressed promptly; steps into startup 14+ moves are never true on purpose (they are the escapable steps and the guard-break threat). No move number changes to fix a link. |
  | Combo counter | counts through free gaps up to 12 ticks | Unchanged, plus: it ends at once at a combo break. "COMBO n" keeps meaning hits one attacker landed in a row on one foe; the Training panel (P18-04) says per link whether it was TRUE or how many free ticks the foe had. |
  | Chain cancel window | offset 1 to active - 1 + floor(0.6 x recovery); later waits for the end of the move | Unchanged. Said in the move list as "press the next button before the move is 60 % recovered to chain it; later it still comes, as a slow step". |
  | Chain escape | nothing says when | Decided: a chain can be escaped from the free tick on, with the foe's own defence; the three ways are named: (1) Block held through the stun (guard on F; perfect for g 1 to 7, a plain block above), (2) Sway tapped within 6 ticks before F (the press is kept 6 ticks; perfect for g 1 to 7, a plain dodge for 8 to 12, too early from 13; each tick later shifts the column by one), (3) a quick move on F wins when g >= 6 (a jab needs 5 startup ticks; g = 5 trades). No other input, no window inside the stun. |
  | Perfect block | 7 ticks (0 to 6 after the guard starts) | Unchanged window. Decided wording: the guard STARTS, by a press or by a Defend held into the free tick, and the strike lands within 7 ticks of the start ("press Defend up to 0.1 s before the hit, or keep it held through a combo and your guard starts the moment you are free"). Training reads PERFECT (held) when the guard started on a free tick with Defend already down, PERFECT when it started by a press. |
  | Perfect sway | 6 ticks, pressed 6 to 11 before contact | Decided: aligned with the block (DECIDE 2): perfect on sway ticks 0 to 6, plain dodge on 7 to 11; the CPU aims swap (below). Worded as the block: "tap Defend up to 0.1 s before the hit". The sway help text "a hit dodged at the last moment" is replaced. |
  | Counter after a perfect sway | lands 26 to 31 ticks after contact | Decided (2b): the sway ends at tick 21 after a perfect one, so it lands 25 to 31. |
  | Cooldown | 30 ticks between guard starts; a high to low guard switch is never perfect | Unchanged, said as "a guard you raised within the last half second cannot be perfect"; the Training reading shows "TOO SOON" instead of PERFECT. The same rule means a guard that started less than 30 ticks before the free tick (a hit just blocked, a block released and re-held) is not a combo break. |
  | Rewards | as above | Unchanged (block: stagger 26, counter window 50, +20 momentum, stamina; sway: counter window 50, +20 momentum, stamina by difficulty), plus the 21-tick end of a perfect sway. Why: counter window, momentum and stamina are what P14-01 and the Block vs Sway split were balanced on. |
  | Combo break | does not exist | Decided: it is a name and a cue, not an input. A perfect block or a perfect sway of a strike of `def.comboFoe` (the attacker whose combo it is: `claimCombo` keeps one attacker per foe) that lands while the defender is inside that open combo (`gotHits` > 0) is a COMBO BREAK: the engine emits `comboBreak { att, def, hits }` with `att` = the attacker whose strike was defended = `def.comboFoe`, ends the combo at once (`comboEnd` emitted, `gotHits`, `gotDamage`, `calmTicks` and `juggleCount` cleared) and the cues of the table below play. Several fighters: a break needs the strike to be the comboFoe's own. A perfect against a DIFFERENT attacker while somebody else's combo is open is a normal perfect (counter, momentum, stamina) and not a break: the other attacker's chain is not what was stopped, the open combo stays as it is and ends by the 12-tick rule or by that attacker's next hit; `claimCombo` (a landed hit from a second attacker) still ends the first attacker's combo with no break cue, as today. BROKEN shows on the counter of the attacker whose chain was broken (`att.comboShow`), nobody else's. Cost: none beyond the defence itself (a Sway costs its 10 stamina, a Block nothing). Input: the defence itself (Defend held or tapped). Window: g 1 to 7 for both defences (for Block, a guard whose previous start was at least 30 ticks earlier: the cooldown; a sway has no cooldown, only its 10 stamina). Reward: nothing new (the counter window, momentum and stamina of a perfect). Because: it is what a perfect already is when it lands mid-chain; naming it makes the owner's "clearer combo breaks" real without a number; tying it to `comboFoe` keeps the reset and the cue attached to one attacker in a crowd (Free Fight is now the one mode). |
  | A plain block or a sway inside a combo | the count goes on | Unchanged, owner can change (5 to 30 such blocks followed by a counted hit per 12 AI fights; ending the count would raise damage in many fights and move fingerprints; the defender stopped one step, not the string). |
  | Early and late press | not recorded | The Fighter keeps the age of its last fresh Defend press (read-only data) and whether Defend was already down when its guard or sway started. Training shows: PERFECT or PERFECT (held) (defence started n ticks before the hit), EARLY n (plain block or plain dodge, n ticks more than 6), TOO SOON (inside the 30-tick cooldown), LATE n (Defend pressed n ticks after the hit, up to 12), SWAY EARLY n (dodge over before the hit). In normal play LATE is the only text this adds; the free-tick ring and sound also play (cue table). |

- Which moves change: none. No hit stun, block stun, startup, active or recovery number of any move moves; no new move. Rule changes: the Sway perfect window (0 to 6 instead of 6 to 11) and the 21-tick end of a perfect sway, the CPU Sway aims (below). The code changes are only these: (0) `Combat.evaluate` marks a sway perfect when `def.stateTicks <= PERFECT_BLOCK_TICKS` (`SWAY_PERFECT_TICKS` removed), `swayHit` on a perfect sets the fighter's sway end to `SWAY_PERFECT_FREE_TICK` 21 (`updateSway` reads it, `startSway` clears it), the sway pose returns upright by then, and the Sway CPU: `AI_SWAY_AIM_PERFECT` 8 to 3, `AI_SWAY_AIM` 3 to 9, and a CPU that rolled the plain aim only answers a strike it sees at least 8 ticks ahead (`eta` >= 8; with 6 or 7 it could not delay enough and would land inside the perfect ticks by accident, so it does not sway, as it does not below `AI_SWAY_MIN_ETA` 6 today); (1) `Combat.blockHit` / `swayHit` call a new `Combat.comboBreak(att, def)` when the perfect lands with `def.gotHits > 0` and `att === def.comboFoe` (the reset and the events); (2) `Fighter` stores the age of its last fresh Defend press and the age it was last hit, and emits `freeTick { f }` when a HIT, STAGGER or STUN ends; (3) HUD, FX and Sound listeners and the Training panel read them (cosmetic, no sim state). The sim change of (1) was checked against the AI fights above: in 60 seeded fights no hit followed a perfect inside a combo, so it changes no result there.
- How the timing is made readable (P18-04); every outcome different by sight alone and by sound alone, and no cue may lead or lag the sim by more than one tick:

  | Outcome | Sight | Sound | Text |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Plain block | white sparks, small flash (as now), no ring | the existing thump (triangle 520 to 360 Hz) | BLOCK (as now) |
  | Perfect block | the big flash (as now) plus a cyan ring that grows from the contact point in 8 ticks | thump plus the two sine notes 1320 and 1980 Hz (as now) | PERFECT BLOCK! (cyan) |
  | Perfect sway | white flash under the swayer (as now) plus the same cyan ring | the same two notes without the thump (and the perfect block's "held" reading is the same) | PERFECT SWAY! |
  | Plain sway | nothing at the body (as now) | none (as now) | SWAY |
  | Combo break | a gold ring (not cyan) plus the attacker's combo counter turns to BROKEN for 0.6 s | three rising notes 880, 1320, 1760 Hz, 60 ms apart (not the perfect pair, not the counter ting) | COMBO BREAK! (gold) |
  | Guard break | orange sparks and shake (as now) | the sawtooth drop (as now) | GUARD BREAK! |
  | Late (Defend pressed 1 to 12 ticks after a hit; only to the player who pressed) | the hit as it is plus a small grey pop on the defender | a short low click (square 220 Hz, 30 ms), not a chime | LATE (Training: LATE n) |
  | Free tick (the stun ended while the foe is still attacking; humans and the Training dummy) | a short white ring at the feet | a soft tick (sine 1000 Hz, 40 ms, low gain) | none (so LATE is the only new text; the ring and the tick play as well) |

  The counter hit's ting (triangle 1760 and 2640 Hz) is too close to the perfect pair and moves to another pair in P18-04 (a test below proves no two outcomes share their notes). Animations: attack and reaction poses already follow the tick counts (`mStartup`, `mActive`, `mRecovery`, `reactDur`); the rule is that no pose may stay after its stun or recovery ended nor show READY before it. The Training panel keeps its frame-data lines and adds the LINK line: "LINK TRUE (3 ticks to spare)" or "foe free 5 ticks" for the last chain step, and the DEFEND line above. The help and the move list (plain words, nothing from this log): "While you are hit you cannot act. A chain is a series of hits; if the next one comes while you are still stunned you cannot stop it. When you are free, your guard or sway is ready: hold Defend through a combo and your guard starts the moment you are free, and a hit right then is a perfect block that breaks the combo. A perfect block or sway: press Defend up to 0.1 s before the hit."
- Self-tests P18-03 / P18-04 must add (section "Timing (P18-03)" and "Cues (P18-04)"; none may skip, weaken or delete an older test):
