  1. `Timing (P18-03): the Block window is the same for every strike move` - for every strike move of `ATTACKS` (air strikes with an airborne attacker, weapon moves with a weapon in hand), for foes of Balance 1, 5, 10 and attackers of Speed 1, 5, 10, press Defend at d = -2 to 12 before contact: hit below 0, perfect 0 to 6, plain above; low moves with Crouch; plus the held case: Defend already down when the fighter becomes free reads perfect (held). Proves one window everywhere; exceptions are the ones listed below.
  2. `Timing (P18-03): the Sway window is the Block window` - the same moves and foes, d = -2 to 13: hit, perfect 0 to 6 (the same d range as test 1), plain 7 to 11, hit from 12; a perfect sway ends at sway tick 21 and the counter after it lands 25 to 31 ticks after contact, a plain one keeps tick 27; a test that sets the Block rows and the Sway rows side by side and fails if their perfect ranges differ.
  3. `Timing (P18-03): the CPU levels use the same window` - AI foes of Easy, Normal, Hard, Expert (and `noParry` set): every perfect they produce has guard age or sway tick <= 6; a reaction block with `noParry` is never perfect; Easy (react 20) misses what Expert (react 8) catches; a Sway CPU that rolled the plain aim never lands inside tick 0 to 6 (no accidental perfect), one that rolled the perfect aim lands on tick 3.
  4. `Timing (P18-03): cooldown and guard switch` - a second guard start within 30 ticks of the first, and a high to low switch, are never perfect; at 30 it is.
  5. `Timing (P18-03): stun and block stun follow the formula` - for every strike move the measured `reactDur` and `blockStun` equal the table (Balance 1, 5, 10; hits 1 to 7; counter hit; stagger; block) and the Training advantage equals the measured free-tick difference.
  6. `Timing (P18-03): links` - for every string row and every legal free-chain pair, g at the earliest and latest cancel press equals the model (the lists above are the expected values; the three classes are asserted, not just printed), and the late-press path (after the cancel window) is the move end plus startup.
  7. `Timing (P18-03): the escape table` - the grid of the g table above is reproduced with the aligned Sway column: Block held, Sway tapped in the last 6 ticks and a jab on F, for g = -2 to 14 (perfect 1 to 7, plain above for Block to any g and for Sway to 12, hit from 13); and the cooldown case: the same grid with the guard started 29 ticks before F (never perfect) and 30 ticks before F (perfect), so no combo break from a recent guard.
  8. `Timing (P18-03): combo break` - a perfect block and a perfect sway of the comboFoe's strike inside its open combo emit `comboBreak` once (att = the comboFoe) and end the combo; outside a combo, a plain block, a sway dodge and a perfect against a thrown item emit none; a hit after the break counts as hit 1 (full stun, full damage). With three fighters (Free Fight): A chains the foe, B's strike is perfect-blocked while A's combo is open: a normal perfect, no `comboBreak`, A's combo goes on; A's own step perfect-blocked: `comboBreak` with att A, A's `comboShow` shows BROKEN and B's counter is untouched; a landed hit of B on the foe ends A's combo with no break cue.
  9. `Timing (P18-03): the free tick` - `freeTick` is emitted on the tick a HIT, STAGGER or STUN ends and on no other.
  10. `Cues (P18-04): every outcome has its own sight and sound` - the table above as data: no two outcomes share a colour-and-ring pair or a note set, each plays on the tick of the event.
  11. `Cues (P18-04): the Training reading` - presses at d = -3 to 14 read PERFECT / PERFECT (held) / EARLY n / TOO SOON / LATE n correctly, for a Block and a Sway dummy, on touch, gamepad and keyboard intents.
  12. `Cues (P18-04): help text` - the new sentences pass the P17-05 scan (no design notes), the sway sentence no longer says "last moment", and the help says that holding Defend through a combo starts the guard when you are free.
  Then the P3-02 and P0-06 runs and the fingerprint steps of the next bullet.
- Exceptions to the one window (the P18-03 test lists them; each is a rule here): (a) a Block to Crouch-Block switch is a new guard that is never perfect; (b) a guard started within 30 ticks of the previous start is never perfect, so a guard that started less than 30 ticks before the free tick (a hit just blocked, a block released and held again) gives no perfect and no combo break there; (c) a CPU reaction block flagged `noParry` is never perfect; (d) the tackle: Block stops it (10 ticks of stun), never perfect, no counter; (e) grabs, clinch strikes, throws and holds are outside (their own escape windows); (f) a thrown item meets the same Block window but a sway dodges it without a perfect; thrown bodies and hazards cannot be blocked or swayed; (g) a strike from outside the front half-plane of the guard cannot be blocked at all, whatever the timing; (h) after a get-up the strikes are ignored for 6 ticks (`WAKEUP_GRACE_TICKS`), so a guard held from the first free tick can only be perfect against strikes that land at guard age 5 and 6 (2 ticks); (i) a Sway press below 10 stamina does nothing (needs a cue: the stamina bar flashes and the LOW STAMINA tick plays on the press); (j) an air strike uses the same line but is not yet probed.
- Effect on P14-01 and the fingerprints: no number of any move, style, difficulty or stamina rule changes. What does change is everything a Sway CPU does with a perfect sway: the window (0 to 6 instead of 6 to 11), the end of a perfect sway (tick 21), the CPU aims (`AI_SWAY_AIM_PERFECT` 3, `AI_SWAY_AIM` 9, plain aim only from `eta` 8). So P3-02 (Block vs Sway, 45 to 55 % at every difficulty) and the Sway rows of P14-01 must be re-measured, and the fingerprints that include a Sway CPU change. Sway CPUs are 16 of the 32 presets (MARA, YUKI, FEMKE, AMARA, SORA, DIEGO, TIAGO, ELISE, REID, CAMILA, VIKRAM, LUCIA, MARLON, NIA, TESSA, ROXY) and every custom fighter with defense sway; MARA is also the default second fighter of the scripted tests, so any seeded run with MARA or another Sway fighter on either side is affected, a run with Block fighters only is not. The new combo reset is silent in 60 seeded AI fights and the new events and fields are read-only for the sim, so determinism and online replay are untouched. Steps for P18-03, in this order (named so nothing is re-recorded by habit):
  1. Land the window, the tick-21 end, the combo break and the events behind one temporary local switch that restores the old Sway rule and the old AI aims (not committed). With the switch off, run the whole suite and every recorded hash: all must match as recorded (shows the rest of the change is silent). Also run the pinned 1v1 hashes of the Block-only pairs with the switch on: they must match too.
  2. Switch on and list which recorded hashes and counts fail (candidates: the pinned Free Fight hashes, the old-layout hashes, the hazards-and-items hashes, the N-fighter hashes, the Group AI and item-throw tests, the headless runner test with its default MARA, the Levels, Hazards, Intro-emote, Story-scene and Arcade-story runs; tests that compare two runs of the same code with and without a flag are unaffected). For each failing one show the cause is the Sway rule alone: run it again with every Sway fighter swapped for a Block fighter of the same style and show the hash is the old one, or show that the first differing tick of the run is a sway event.
  3. Re-tune P3-02: run `defenseSplit` (60 seeds, both seats, Easy, Normal, Hard, Expert) before and after; the aim is 45 to 55 % for both defences at every difficulty. Knobs in this order: `swayAim` (0.01, 0.05, 0.7, 1), then `swayRead`, then the plain-aim threshold; `parry` (0.25, 0.3) only if the Block side moved. Record before and after in this log.
  4. Re-run P0-06 (the rows of the P18-33 table: Press, Mash, AI at Easy, Normal, Hard, Expert for 1v1 to 1v6 and waves) and the P14-01 ladders for the Sway fighters; they must stay inside the P14-01 targets, otherwise change the knobs of step 3 and not a move number.
  5. Only now re-record the failing hashes, in a separate commit that names each test and says "Sway rule aligned (P18-02)". If any hash of a Block-only run fails, stop: that is a bug, not a re-record.
- Edge cases, each a rule:
  - CPU difficulty: stun and windows do not depend on difficulty. A CPU sees a wind-up `react` ticks late (Easy 20, Normal 14, Hard 10, Expert 8) and then guards, so Easy cannot react in time to any move (the longest startup is 18), and a reaction block can only be perfect against a startup between react - 1 and react + 5 (Expert: 7 to 13, Normal: 13 to 19); `parry` (0.01, 0.05, 0.25, 0.3) is only the chance a reaction block is timed (`noParry` otherwise); `swayAim` (0.01, 0.05, 0.7, 1) the chance a Sway CPU aims at the perfect ticks (after the alignment: tick 3 of 0 to 6; the plain aim is tick 9 of 7 to 11). A CPU does not hold Defend through a stun (DECIDE 2c): it gives no input in a hit reaction, so a CPU defender never makes a combo break by itself; it can still be broken against by a person.
  - Several fighters: a combo is per attacker and per foe (`def.comboFoe`); a hit by a second attacker ends the first attacker's combo (no break cue); a combo break needs the defended strike to be the comboFoe's own (see the rule table); the BROKEN text shows on that attacker's counter only. Windows are the defender's own and the front-half-plane rule decides which attacker can be blocked; a sway dodges all sides. The free-tick cue shows only for the foe that is being chained.
  - Stamina: a guard with an empty tank breaks at the first non-perfect hit; a perfect still works and gives stamina back; a Sway below 10 stamina is refused (cue (i)); a low tank slows the attacker (x 1.1), an empty tank adds 3 startup, so links loosen; none of it moves the defender's window.
  - Items: a held weapon, bin lid or crate changes costs only (a weapon strike costs the blocker x 1.2, the lid takes the cost, a carried crate allows Kick but no punch), never the window; a weapon move's stun is in the table above.
  - Raised ground and walls (after P18-44, commit 2edb05b, not yet in 794e348; reach is measured from the attacker's own footing, not from the floor, `rise` = the foe's feet above it): whether a strike can reach depends on the foe's feet above the attacker's footing (a low strike misses above its low reach, a mid strike above its mid reach) and on the footing gap (a strike needs the foe's footing within the strike ledge 1.05 m, a grab, a throw or a tackle within the grab ledge 0.55 m); the window is the same at any height and on any footing, because it counts the defender's own ticks from the contact. The numbers and the ledge test are those of P18-44; the P18-03 test (1) runs one case on a raised footing (a foe 0.5 m above and 0.5 m below) and one beyond the strike ledge (no contact, so no window). A sway at a wall or on a ledge has the same i-frames (only the drawn lean is shortened).
  - Wake-up, juggles, knockdowns: (h) above; a juggle allows 3 hits then the body falls; one ground hit per knockdown; a thrown body or item stagger / hit stun (24 / 16 x Balance) is flat and counts into the combo (`gotHits`) but does not scale.
  - Hold the guard through the stun: allowed and intended (the first free tick starts the guard at stateTicks 0); a fighter who holds Defend all fight gets the same plain blocks as before, perfects only by that tick and never twice within 30 ticks.
  - Auto-assist and touch: the string window x 2 (96 ticks) and press-without-contact only change when the attacker may start a step; the defender's window is the same on touch, gamepad and keyboard because Defend is one press.
- Open (for the owner; each one is decided above and the owner can change it): (1) The Sway window is aligned with the Block window, with the 21-tick end of a perfect sway (DECIDE 2 and 2b); the cost is the re-tune and the re-recorded Sway hashes of the steps above. (2) A plain block or sway inside a combo does not end it (17 to 30 events per 12 AI fights, would raise damage). (3) The CPU does not hold Defend through a stun (DECIDE 2c); the option is a `holdGuard` difficulty field. (4) The free-tick cue is on for humans and for the Training dummy only.
- Checks: probes run only (extract of `breakpoint.html` at 794e348, stub-browser harness, `SimRunner.run` with 12 seeds per row, scripted `MatchSim` trials; the aligned-Sway numbers come from a patched copy of the extracted script in the temp folder, 27 strike moves with identical rows, and the escape grid g -2 to 14 run again on it); no game file, test file or fingerprint was touched, so no self-test run was needed. Still open for P18-03: the air-strike window and the full suite after the code lands; for P18-04: the browser look and sound check of the cue table, and the owner's phone feel check (for the P16-03 list; the coordinator adds it): press Defend in Training against a dummy and read PERFECT / EARLY / LATE, and break a three-hit chain with a held Block.

### P18-42 Free Fight fight rules decided (2026-11-06)

- Owner (2026-11-04 05:40, verbatim): "Any kind of fight can be configured there incl if the level is open or constrained to the square ring also an option for ring out existence of weapons and breakables and overall settings to recreate any kind of fight". Decided by the coordinator's delegate under the owner's standing go ("balanced"); every "Decided" line can be changed by the owner. It fills the "Fight rules (P18-42)" group that P18-18 reserved on the "More options" screen (after Rounds, Time limit, Friendly fire); P18-43 builds it. Nothing here changes the game code.
- What the code does today (base 78a6e17):
  - Area: every level has a `ring` row `[x, z, hx, hz, ringOut]`. `Arena.setArea(ring, ringOut)` already supports a ring without ring-out (walls on the ring edge) and with it (walls 1.5 m beyond the edge, `RING_APRON`, and `stepRingOut` knocks out a fighter standing outside the ring). `MatchSim` ignores the ring for `mode: 'brawl'` (lineups and the crowd), and honours `ring[4]` (1 on the Gym, Rooftop, Construction Site and Night Club only: the other four rings cut across a cage, a track, a ramp or a mezzanine, P8-01). `Game.beginMatch` sets `ring` and `ringOut` only for Free Fight and Versus, from `Settings.v.ringOut`, and only on a level with `ring[4]`; so the ring without ring-out is not reachable from any screen, and the ring is never drawn (the four ring-out levels have it painted on the floor).
  - Items and hazards: `cfg.items` and `cfg.hazards` (`itemsOn`, `hazardsOn`), filled by `beginMatch` from `Settings.v.items` and `Settings.v.hazards` at every start (so a rematch re-reads them; the Tournament passes `false` for both). A ring keeps out the items and hazards that lie outside it (`Arena.inRing`).
  - Breakables: every level object has hit points (`Arena.damageObject`, `breakObject`); weak decks collapse under weight (`stepLoad`), fire heats flammable objects (`stepFire`), a glass wall is also a hazard that triggers when it breaks. Nothing switches this off.
  - Health: the lineup multiplies a fighter's `stats.maxHp` and `stats.damage` by the Even-teams handicap (`slot.hp`, `slot.dmg`); nothing else scales health. Survival carries health between bouts (`cfg.playerHp`), Training has infinite-health toggles for the dummy.
  - Start side: a 1v1 (not Training) draws the first fighter's side from the seed in round 1 and swaps it every round (`MatchSim.sideFor`, `cfg.sides`); lineups and the crowd always put the lead team on the left.
  - The match config `cfg` is already the whole truth of a fight: `MatchSim` reads only `cfg` (the Settings are read in `Game.beginMatch`, `Story.simOpts` and `Tournament.simulate`, never inside the sim), so replays, `SimRunner` and online host/client stay in step as long as every rule is a `cfg` field.
- Decided: the rules are seven rows plus a preset row, kept as one table (`FIGHT_RULES`) that drives the screen, the validation, the presets, the summary line and the `cfg` fields (one source, so a row cannot be added in one place only). They sit on `freemore` after Friendly fire under a small heading "Fight rules"; a row that does not apply is shown dimmed with its reason and is out of the focus order (the existing `unusedRow`); its stored value is kept, so switching the level or the fight type back restores it.

  | # | Row | Values | Default | Becomes | Dimmed ("Not used: ...") when |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | Rules preset | Standard, Tournament bout, Survival style, Ring match (shows Custom when the rows are none of the four); a tap cycles the four | Standard | sets rows 2 to 8 and Rounds and Time limit above | never |
  | 2 | Area | Whole level, Square ring | Whole level | `cfg.ring` | the crowd: "the crowd walks in from the level's doors" |
  | 3 | Ring-out | Off, On | Off | `cfg.ringOut` | Area is Whole level: "the whole level is open; choose Square ring"; the level has no ring edge (`ring[4]` is 0): "this level has no ring edge (Gym, Rooftop, Construction Site, Night Club have one)"; the crowd |
  | 4 | Weapons and items | On, Off | On | `cfg.items` | never |
  | 5 | Hazards | On, Off | On | `cfg.hazards` | never |
  | 6 | Breakables | On, Off | On | `cfg.breakables` (false = solid scenery) | never |
  | 7 | Health | Half, Normal, One and a half, Double (x0.5, 1, 1.5, 2) | Normal | `cfg.hpScale` | never |
  | 8 | Player 1 starts | Automatic, Left, Right | Automatic | `cfg.startSide` (0, 1, -1) | the crowd ("the doors decide") and a lineup of 3 or more teams or a free-for-all ("everyone starts in a circle") |

  Defaults equal today's Free Fight (whole room, ring-out off, items and hazards on, scenery breakable, normal health, drawn side), so with the defaults a fight is byte-for-byte what it is now and no fingerprint changes.
- Row notes (what each value does, on every level):
  - Area, Square ring: the fighting area is the level's `ring` rectangle (centre, half sizes) cut to the level bounds; the walls stand on its edge, the fighters start around its centre (`cfg.ring: true`, `ringOut` false), items and hazards outside it are not placed, scenery outside it stays as backdrop nobody can reach or break. This works on all eight levels, including those whose ring has stairs, decks or a track at its edge: the ring is a cut, so a stair or deck that runs past the edge is walkable up to the wall only (Warehouse: the mezzanine and its stair top lie outside; Subway: the near track and the train are inside). Because the walls are otherwise invisible (the owner met this in P17-08), the ring is drawn: four thin floor strips on the ring edge (white; orange when ring-out is on), see Build notes. Decided: the ring is each level's existing P8-05 rectangle, not a new size (because the 8 rectangles are already checked against the levels' items, hazards and spawns); owner can change.
  - Ring-out On: as today (Settings hint text reused): a fighter standing outside the ring on the floor or on anything has lost the round (a fighter in the air has not landed yet); the walls stand 1.5 m beyond the edge so a push or throw can carry someone out; the CPU does not walk, run, sidestep or jump out by itself. Now it also works in lineups: the fighter outside is knocked out (credited to `lastFoe` when that fighter may hurt it), the fight goes on with the others, the round ends with RING OUT when a ring-out happened in it. Decided: ring-out stays limited to the four levels with `ring[4]` (because the other four rings cross a cage, a track, a ramp and a mezzanine, so a stair or a fall would lose a round by accident); on those four the row is dimmed and the ring plays without ring-out. A level's flag is data, so allowing more levels later is a one-number change per level.
  - Weapons and items: Off places no item, so there is nothing to pick up, throw or break out of a wall (a brick wall leaves no brick); fighters are bare-handed. Electric panels only start from a thrown metal item (`metalOnly`), so with items Off they stay quiet; the Hazards hint says so. No "more weapons" value: new item content is not a rule.
  - Hazards: Off places none (including the open drops and the train) and no glass patch from a bottle; as today.
  - Breakables Off: the scenery is solid: a hit still wobbles the object and makes its sound but does no damage, weak decks never collapse, nothing burns down, the scaffold bays never drop, and a glass wall hazard (it triggers only by breaking) is not placed. Fire barrels, panels, the load, steam and drops still work (they do not need a breaking object). It is a flag on `Arena` (`Arena.solid`, read in `damageObject`, `stepLoad`, the fire heat loop and `spawnHazards`), not `fixed` on every object: `fixed` objects skip the level-of-detail pass (`updateLod`), so they would all be drawn in full detail. `Arena.build` is also called without the new argument for the menu backdrop and the scenes behind the menus (`breakpoint.html` lines 11344, 11476 and 11606 at 78a6e17): a missing value means breakables on and `build` always assigns it, so a solid fight never carries over to a menu level; `Arena.reset()` (Training's object reset) rebuilds with the match's own stored value, as it already does for the ring.
  - Health: multiplies every fighter's `stats.maxHp` after the Even-teams handicap, rounded, for people, CPU, the crowd and enemies that walk in later (`enterEnemy`); damage, stamina and hazards stay as they are, so a Half-health fight is shorter and the hazards matter more. Applies in every path, lineups and the crowd included. Grades: the time part of a grade compares the fight's length with a par (`GRADE_PAR_ROUND`, `GRADE_PAR_ENEMY`), and a Half-health fight ends sooner, so Health would hand out easy grades (and Double hard ones) into the pooled Free Fight best grade. Decided: `MatchSim.results` carries `hpScale` and `gradeInputs` multiplies the par by it (Half: half the par, Double: twice), because it keeps every fight gradeable and comparable with one multiplication; the damage part already scales (`takenMax` uses the scaled `maxHp`). Owner can change (the alternative is no grade when Health is not Normal).
  - Player 1 starts: Left or Right fixes the side in round 1 (the first fighter's lead team in a lineup of two teams: the whole line-up is mirrored about the centre of the fighting area); later rounds still swap sides (the P9 seat-balance rule), so Left does not mean Left every round. Automatic is today's rule (a 1v1 draws and swaps; a lineup puts the lead team on the left).
- Rejected rows: Damage scale and Stamina (the Difficulty row already moves the CPU's damage and the people's stamina edge, hazards do fixed damage, so a damage factor is health in the other direction; infinite stamina and health belong to Training's dummy); a separate "Round time" (Time limit above has it); a third item value "More weapons"; a Devices row; a Clock-style "sudden death" (a time-out is already decided by health share).
- Rules presets (the Rounds and Time limit rows of P18-18 are part of a fight, so a preset sets them too; it never touches the lineup, the level, Difficulty or Friendly fire). A preset equals the mode it copies; the copied modes whose rules are the Standard rules need no row of their own:

  | Preset | Copies | Area | Ring-out | Items | Hazards | Breakables | Health | Starts | Rounds | Time per round | Time whole fight |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Standard | Free Fight today, Arcade duel (2 wins, 60 s, whole room), Versus, Brawl (one fight, no clock) | Whole | Off | On | On | On | Normal | Auto | Best of 3 | 60 s | Off |
  | Tournament bout | a bracket fight (`beginTournamentMatch`: items and hazards false, 1 win, 60 s; the final is first to 2) | Whole | Off | Off | Off | On | Normal | Auto | Best of 1 | 60 s | Off |
  | Survival style | the rules of one Survival fight (1 win, items and hazards on, whole room). Not exact, and named "style" for that reason: Survival's clock starts at 50 s and shrinks to 30 s, the Time limit list (Off, 30, 60, 90) has no 50, and the CPU level climbs with the run, so the preset uses 60 s and leaves Difficulty to the first screen | Whole | Off | On | On | On | Normal | Auto | Best of 1 | 60 s | Off |
  | Ring match | new: a ring fight in the Budokai style | Ring | On | Off | Off | On | Normal | Auto | Best of 3 | 60 s | Off |

  - Label rule (the only rule, as for the fight type): the label is the first preset whose values equal the current rows; rows that do not apply to this lineup (Rounds in a lineup, a dimmed row) are ignored in the comparison; anything else shows "Custom". Choosing a preset sets its values at once and keeps everything else.
  - Decided: no separate rows for "Arcade duel" and "Brawl" (because their rules are exactly the Standard rules, and two identical presets would show only the first name); the Fight type row already makes a Duel or a Brawl, so Arcade duel = Duel + Standard, Arcade brawl rules = Brawl or Co-op + Standard (the waves come with the Co-op crowd on a level that has them), Tournament bout = Duel + Tournament bout, Survival style = Duel + Survival style with the CPU level chosen by hand. Not reproducible by design: the Arcade boss tuning and story cast, Survival's carried health and changing levels, the Tournament's level order.
- Where the Settings switches go (P18-18 left them in place until this task):

  | Switch | Decided | Because |
  |---|---|---|
  | Settings > Ring-out | removed (`SETTING_DEFS.ringOut` goes; the Settings screen builds its rows from the table, so the row goes with it). Free Fight: rows Area and Ring-out. Tournament: a new row "Ring-out" (Off, On) next to Entrants and Difficulty on the Tournament screen, stored in the bracket | only Free Fight, Versus and the Tournament read it, and it now has per-fight homes |
  | Settings > Items, Hazards | stay in Settings, relabelled in their hints ("Arcade, Survival and Training. Free Fight sets its own under More options.") | Arcade, Survival and Training still read them and give no other place to set them; Free Fight and the Tournament no longer read them |
  | Free Fight rows Items, Hazards | seeded once from the old switches (below), then independent | a player who had weapons off keeps a bare-handed Free Fight |

- What the other modes use:

  | Mode | Rules |
  |---|---|
  | Arcade | fixed: whole room, no ring, no ring-out, breakables on, normal health, drawn side; items and hazards from the two Settings switches (as now) |
  | Survival | the same as Arcade |
  | Training | the same, no ring; its dummy toggles stay in the pause menu |
  | Tournament | bout rules of the preset above; the bracket's own `ringOut` row decides the ring (Off: whole room; On: the ring with ring-out on its four levels, which are the only levels `TOURNAMENT_STAGES` uses); stored in the bracket when it is drawn so a continued bracket and `Tournament.simulate` always agree (today they read the global setting at every step) |
  | Free Fight | the group above, saved with the setup |

- Save format: inside P18-18's `breakpoint.v1.setups`, `v` stays 1 (a new field with a default is not a new version): `freeFight.rules = { area, ringOut, items, hazards, breakables, health, side }`, every field a small whole number (area 0 or 1, ringOut 0 or 1, items 0 or 1, hazards 0 or 1, breakables 0 or 1, health 0 to 3 for the four scales, side 0 to 2 for Automatic, Left, Right) and `tournament.ringOut` a boolean. `FIGHT_RULES` holds [key, max, default] so one loop validates: read raw at start-up and cleaned on first use after `CustomRoster.init`, as P18-18 says; a missing `rules`, a non-object, a non-integer or out-of-range field falls back to that field's default, never throws and never touches `Progress`. An older build that reads a newer file ignores `rules`. Rounds, Time limit and Friendly fire stay where P18-18 put them (they are not copied into `rules`).
- Migration (no lost choice): at start-up, immediately after `Settings.init` and before anything can call `Settings.save` (which writes only keys still in `SETTING_DEFS` and would drop `ringOut`), when `setups.freeFight.rules` is missing, read `ringOut`, `items` and `hazards` raw from `breakpoint.v1.settings` and write `rules` at once, by merging only `freeFight.rules` and `tournament.ringOut` into the raw stored `setups` object and leaving every other field exactly as stored (no cleaning, no `MatchLineup.fix`, no slot check: at start-up `CustomRoster.init` has not run, so a cleaned setup would replace a custom fighter in a slot with a known one, the trap P18-18 avoided; its cleaning stays on first use). A `setups` entry that is missing, unreadable or not an object gets a fresh `{ v: 1 }` with just these two fields; an entry with an unknown `v` is left untouched and the rules read as defaults. The values written are: `area` = Square ring and `ringOut` On when the old switch was true (else Whole level and Off), `items` and `hazards` Off only when the old switch was `false`; and `setups.tournament.ringOut` = the old switch. The test is "`rules` missing", not "`setups` missing", because P18-19 writes the `setups` key at its own start-up (for friendly fire) before this task ships. Known small change: a player who had Ring-out On now also plays the four levels without a ring edge inside their ring (before, those levels had the whole room); ring-out still applies only on the four edge levels. A saved bracket from before has no `ringOut`: it reads the seeded `tournament.ringOut` once and keeps it in the bracket from then on (`Game.savedTournament` cleaning). Downgrade: an older build reads Ring-out from the settings file, where it is missing, and plays with it off.
- Determinism, replay and online:
  - Every rule is a `cfg` field filled once in `Game.beginMatch`/the one `cfg` builder P18-19 extracts (`ring`, `ringOut`, `items`, `hazards`, `breakables`, `hpScale`, `startSide`); `MatchSim` reads nothing else, so a host sends the `cfg` and the clients play the same fight, and a test can run `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(cfg))` and get the same hash. Missing fields mean the defaults, so old configs and every recorded fingerprint are unchanged. The effective values go in `cfg` (a ring-out On on a level with no edge is stored as Off, the crowd stores no ring), so the stored `cfg` shows what was played.
  - New sim behaviour is deterministic: no random numbers (the side comes from the seed or the row, health is an integer scale, the solid flag only skips damage). `SimRunner.run` must pass the new fields through (it passes `ring`, `ringOut`, `items`, `hazards`, `sides` by name today), so `BREAKPOINT.simulate` can balance-test any rule set; `Story.simOpts` and `Tournament.simulate` keep passing their own values.
  - Rematch: "Play again" repeats the same `cfg`, so the rules are kept (`restartMatch` today deletes `items`, `hazards`, `ring`, `ringOut` to re-read Settings: a Free Fight `cfg` carries `rulesSet: true` and keeps them; Training still re-reads). Change fighters and More options keep the saved rules; Main menu clears nothing.
- Lineups and the crowd (what is new in the sim):
  - A lineup (2 to 8 fighters, any teams) may fight in the ring: `MatchSim` takes the ring when `mode !== 'brawl'` or the match is a lineup (not the crowd). `placeLineup` starts the teams around the ring centre (not 0, 0), with the spawn distance and the radial radius cut to fit inside (`min(spawn, 0.6 hx)`, radial radius at most `hx - 0.7` and `(hz - 0.7) / 0.8`; the smallest rings are the Gym at 8 x 7 m and the Pit and the Night Club at 9 x 7 m, and 8 fighters in a circle there are about 2.3 m apart), and clamps each start to the ring minus the body radius. A start that `Arena.blockedAt` reports as blocked (the Warehouse and Pit rings cut stairs and decks, other rings hold crates, lockers or a bar) is moved along z in 0.4 m steps, alternating sides, to the nearest free spot still inside the ring and at least a body width from every other start; the order is fixed, so it is deterministic. The step runs only for ring starts (whole-level lineups keep their starts, so their fingerprints do not move); when no spot on that z line is free it tries the same steps along x, then keeps the clamped spot. `CrowdDirector.placeSlot` clamps the CPU's waiting spots to the ring itself, `Arena.sx0..sz1` (the playable area without the apron), minus `RING_WALL_MARGIN`, instead of `def.bounds`. Not `Arena.ax0..az1`: with Ring-out On those include the 1.5 m apron, so a spot could lie about 0.8 m outside the ring, `holdAtRing` would park the CPU at the edge and a push would carry it out. Without a ring `sx0..sz1` equal the bounds, so the old spots are unchanged.
  - The crowd (Co-op type, waves) never uses the ring: its enemies walk in from doors that lie outside every ring; its rows are dimmed. Health, items, hazards and breakables apply to it.
  - Camera: unchanged (it follows the fighters and keeps the eye inside the room); the ring is smaller than the room, so no new limit.
- Edge cases:

  | Case | Result |
  |---|---|
  | Ring-out On, Area Whole level | not possible to start with it: the row is dimmed and `cfg.ringOut` is stored false; the saved value comes back when Area is Square ring again |
  | Ring-out On, Square ring, Warehouse, Pit, Subway or Garage | the ring plays without ring-out (row dimmed with the reason) |
  | Square ring on a level where the ring cuts a stair or deck | allowed; the part outside is unreachable; items, hazards and breakable scenery outside are simply not part of the fight |
  | Square ring and no item inside the ring | allowed (the ring may hold none); nothing is added |
  | Items Off and Hazards On | allowed; electric panels never start (they need a thrown metal item); the Hazards hint says so |
  | Breakables Off and Hazards On | allowed; the glass wall hazard is not placed (it needs a breaking wall), the rest work |
  | An item thrown or a body thrown out of the ring | the walls hold bodies; an item that lands outside the ring cannot be reached and counts toward `ITEM_MAX` until it is the oldest (accepted) |
  | Crowd (Co-op type) with a ring or ring-out stored | ignored, rows dimmed, stored values kept for the next Duel |
  | Lineup of 3 or more teams or a free-for-all, Start side Left or Right | the row is dimmed; the circle start is the same as now |
  | Health Double with a time limit | a time-out is decided by health share, as now |
  | 8 CPU in the smallest ring | must finish and nobody ever ends up outside (the self-test below) |
  | Tournament screen with a saved bracket | the Ring-out row is not shown (a saved bracket keeps its size, CPU level and ring-out) |
  | Old save without `rules` | the seed above; a damaged one falls back to the defaults field by field |
  | Training and Arcade with the ring stored | never read |
  | A phone | the screen scrolls with its sticky bottom row as the other heavy screens; each row keeps the existing tap height; a tap steps the value (Level is the only exception); no new on-screen buttons |

- Self-tests P18-43 must add (names start `Fight rules (P18-43):`):
  1. the table and the defaults: `FIGHT_RULES` defaults give a Free Fight `cfg` equal to the old one for the plain 1v1, a lineup and the crowd, and the recorded fingerprints (the three seeded 1v1s, the three with the ring, the lineup and co-op ones) are unchanged;
  2. Area and Ring-out in a real start on all eight levels: the ring row, walls on the ring edge (and 1.5 m beyond with ring-out), starts inside, items and hazards outside not placed, no fighter outside after a CPU fight, ring-out knocking out the fighter outside on the four edge levels and only there, the dimmed reasons for the whole level, the four other levels and the crowd;
  3. Weapons and items Off: no item placed, no brick from a wall, nothing picked up, an electric panel stays idle; Hazards Off: none placed, no bottle glass; as the existing Settings tests do, through the rows;
  4. Breakables Off: every strike, throw, fall, fire and load leaves all objects at full hit points, objects still wobble, a weak deck holds two heavy fighters for 10 s, no scaffold drops, the glass wall hazard is not placed, the other hazards still run; Breakables On is unchanged (`statusCounts`); after a solid fight `Arena.build(def)` with no argument (the menu backdrop) is breakable again, and `Arena.reset()` during a solid match stays solid;
  5. Health: `maxHp` of every fighter at each scale in a 1v1, a lineup with the handicap, the crowd and an enemy who walks in later, the revive in the crowd, the bars show full at the start, scale Normal changes nothing, and the grade par: the same fight length at Half, Normal and Double gives `par` x 0.5, 1 and 2 in `gradeInputs`;
  6. Player 1 starts: Left and Right in round 1 of a 1v1 and the swap in round 2, a two-team lineup mirrored, the crowd and the free-for-all ignoring it, Automatic equal to the old rule;
  7. a lineup in the ring: 2 teams and a free-for-all of 8, on every level, start inside and none blocked (`Arena.blockedAt` false for every start) on all 8 levels, including the Warehouse and Pit rings that cut stairs and decks and the Gym, Pit and Night Club rings for 8 fighters, CPU-only fights finish with nobody outside, a ring-out in a lineup knocks out one fighter and the rest fight on, the crowd ignores the ring, and with Ring-out On every `placeSlot` waiting spot of every CPU lies inside the ring (not in the apron), checked each tick of an 8-fighter ring-out fight on the four edge levels, with no CPU standing within the ring-out look-ahead of the edge for more than a short moment;
  8. the presets: each sets the rows and the two time rows of its table and the label follows, editing one row shows Custom, the label ignores dimmed rows, Standard builds the old Free Fight `cfg`, Tournament bout builds the `cfg` `beginTournamentMatch` builds (items, hazards, rounds, timer), Survival style the `nextSurvivalMatch` one (apart from the 60 s and the CPU level);
  9. the screen: row order, values, the dimmed reasons, the first-screen More options subtitle lists non-default rules, taps and gamepad steps change the value, the Level change dims and restores Ring-out;
  10. saves and migration: the `rules` field written, read back through a fresh `Store`, damaged, hostile and missing entries fall back field by field, old settings with Ring-out true / Items false / Hazards false seed `rules` and `tournament.ringOut` once at start-up, also when a Setting is changed and saved first and also when P18-19's `setups` key already exists without `rules`; the seed merges and cleans nothing else: a `setups` written by P18-19 with a custom fighter in slot 2 and a deleted-fighter slot still holds exactly those ids after the seed and before `CustomRoster.init`, and is cleaned only on first use afterwards; an unreadable `setups` entry becomes `{ v: 1, freeFight: { rules }, tournament: { ringOut } }` and nothing throws; `SETTING_DEFS.ringOut` is gone and the Settings screen has no Ring-out row, Items and Hazards stay with the new hints;
  11. other modes: Arcade, Survival and Training start with the whole room and read the Settings switches for items and hazards, whatever the Free Fight rules say; a rematch keeps the Free Fight rules, Training re-reads Settings;
  12. the Tournament: the Ring-out row is on the screen and hidden with a saved bracket, the bracket saves and validates `ringOut`, a live bout and `Tournament.simulate` use it (and nothing reads `Settings.v.ringOut`), an old bracket loads with the seeded value;
  13. determinism: the same rule set run twice with `SimRunner` gives the same result, a `cfg` through `JSON.stringify` / `JSON.parse` gives the same hash, and changing `Settings.v.items` and `Settings.v.hazards` while a Free Fight `cfg` with them On runs changes nothing;
  14. the 390x844 and 844x390 layout check of the More options screen with the new rows and of the Tournament screen (no horizontal scroll, the sticky row visible), in the browser run.
  - Existing tests that change (names start as in `breakpoint-tests.js` at 78a6e17; fix to the new flow, never weaken): "Arena bounds (P17-08): a plain 1v1 ... has the whole room; the Ring-out setting fences Free Fight and Versus ..." (9412, the setting is replaced by the rule rows), "Arena bounds (P17-08): a Tournament bout has the whole room ... with the ring only under the Ring-out setting ..." (10753) and "Tournament (P11-02): two people play the CPU as Free Fight and each other as Versus ... (the Ring-out setting is off) ..." (10724, `cfg.ring` / `cfg.ringOut` now come from the bracket), the ring tests that check `SETTING_DEFS.ringOut` (8923), the seeded random walk "Mode flows (P18-40): seeded random walks ..." (12357: `ringOut` leaves its settings object; it should also press the new rows), and the Tournament-screen flow tests that count rows. The Hazards tests that use the Hazards setting stay (the switch stays).
- Build notes for P18-43 (read before starting):
  - Order of work: (a) `FIGHT_RULES` table and `FightRules` helper (defaults, `fix`, `preset`, `label`, `apply`, `effective(rules, arenaId, lineup)` giving the `cfg` fields, `summary`, dimmed reasons); (b) the sim: `Arena.build(def, ring, ringOut, solid)` and `Arena.solid`, the `MatchSim` ring for lineups, `hpScale` (one loop after the fighters are built, plus `enterEnemy`, and in `MatchSim.results` for the grade par in `gradeInputs`), `startSide`, `placeLineup` and `placeSlot`; (c) `beginMatch` and the one `cfg` builder of P18-19 fill the fields from the saved rules and `rulesSet`; `restartMatch` keeps them; `SimRunner` passes them; (d) the screen rows and the Tournament row; (e) save, seed and migration; (f) the drawn ring; (g) tests.
  - The drawn ring: four thin strips (`hput`, `LAYER.ADD` or `SOLID`, 0.06 m wide, 0.02 m high) on the ring edge, in `drawHazards`' neighbour (the match draw), white, orange when `Arena.ringOut`; not drawn without a ring. Check it on the four painted levels (a double line is acceptable, the strip sits on the paint).
  - The `fixed` trap (above): do not implement Breakables Off by setting `fixed` on the level objects.
  - `Arena.setArea` already bumps `navVersion`; a ring changes the CPU's walking map, so the first-tick cost is the same as a level load.
  - Size: the file was 1,187,837 bytes (LF, BOM) of 1,200,000 at 78a6e17; main after P18-44 is about 1,189,500, so about 10,500 bytes remain (P18-19 is meant to lower it). Estimate for P18-43, in bytes: `FIGHT_RULES` and helper 1,600; sim changes (solid, hpScale, startSide, lineup ring, `placeSlot`) 1,000; drawn ring 350; `beginMatch`, `restartMatch`, `SimRunner` 400; More options rows and dimmed reasons 1,300; presets 450; Tournament row and bracket field 500; save, seed and migration 700; text changes (Settings hints, `MODE_INFO.tournament`) 100; plus the start-clear step in `placeLineup` and the grade par 120; minus the removed Ring-out definition about 350. About 6,200 to 7,600 bytes, which leaves roughly 3,000 bytes of the 10,500 for the tasks after it. P18-43 measures the size before it commits (the node one-liner of its validation list) and stops at 7,600. Reuse: one table drives validation, rows, presets and summary; reuse `optRow`, `unusedRow`, `Store`, `readJson`, `MatchLineup.fix`'s pattern; no new screen. Real fallbacks if the measured size is over 7,600 bytes, in this order, with what each saves: (1) shorter dimmed-reason texts, 3 to 5 words each, about 250 bytes; (2) Left and Right only for the plain 1v1, not the mirrored lineup (the row is dimmed in a lineup), about 250 bytes; (3) the ring drawn as one loop over the four edges with one colour (no orange for ring-out), about 100 bytes; (4) drop the Survival style preset, about 80 bytes, then the Ring match preset, about 70 bytes. Together about 750 bytes. If that is still not enough, ask the owner which row to drop (the rows are the owner's request), do not shrink anything else.
  - Hard parts: (1) the migration order (after `Settings.init`, before any `Settings.save`, and keyed on `rules` missing); (2) `restartMatch` must keep the Free Fight rules but still re-read Settings for Training; (3) lineups in a ring: starts and `placeSlot` must use the ring itself (`Arena.sx0..sz1`, never the apron), starts must not be blocked, and 8 fighters in the Gym, Pit or Night Club ring need the radial radius cut; (4) the Tournament bracket field and `Tournament.simulate` / `beginTournamentMatch`, which read `Settings.v.ringOut` today; (5) `MatchSim` re-checks `ring[4]` itself, so the stored `cfg.ringOut` must already be the effective value or tests compare wrong numbers; (6) the effects of Breakables Off are four small checks, not an object property.
- Decided, with the owner able to change each (none blocks P18-43): (1) ring-out only on the four levels with a ring edge. (2) The ring is each level's existing rectangle; a size row is not added. (3) Items and Hazards stay in Settings for Arcade, Survival and Training; Free Fight has its own, seeded once. (4) Health is the only scale row; Damage and Stamina are not offered. (5) Presets: Standard, Tournament bout, Survival style (60 s, not Survival's 50 s, hence "style"), Ring match; Arcade duel and Brawl are the Standard rules. (8) Health scales the grade par, so grades stay comparable. (9) The seed merges into the raw setups object and cleans nothing else. (6) The crowd never uses the ring. (7) The Tournament gets a Ring-out row stored in the bracket. No owner-only question is open that stops P18-43 from starting.

### P18-20 + P18-37 Arcade and Survival brawls, goons and allies decided (2026-11-06)

- Owner (2026-11-01, 2026-11-03 19:56 and 2026-11-04 00:30, verbatim): "Arcade and Survival can have brawls vs cpus or with ally cpus (configurable,balanced according to difficulty)"; "In Arcade the brawls use themed goons that utilize the character creator.They are themed according to the character you fight in the end (one of the existing playable characters). Goons have several variants and due to them being fought at groups they are weaker (balance things out). The main characters used as bosses can be at max 3 and depending on the story you might get cpu allies too."; "each stage's boss bring their own goons!". Decided by the coordinator's delegate under the owner's standing go ("balanced"); every "Decided" line can be changed by the owner. P18-21 builds it; P18-35 (story text) follows its cast and ally rules; P14-02 adds the goon variants to its list. Nothing here changes the game code.
- What the code does today (base 775c3aa):
  - A run has 7 stages: brawls 1, 3, 5, 6 and duels 2, 4, 7 (`Story.cfg`, `Story.arena`). Main-fighter opponents: rival A (stage 2, and the last-wave leader of stage 6 after "Walk away"), rival B (leader of stage 3's last wave, beat B7), the boss (stage 4) and the champion (stage 7), so 4. Rival B is the tone's first rival that is neither rival A nor the player (`STORY_TONES` rivals), always a preset with role `rival`.
  - The "grunts" of a brawl are not a separate cast: `MatchSim.waveProfiles` deals the roster in ladder order (`ROSTER` minus the party, KAI first, ZERO sixth, then the 26 presets), so a stage-1 wave already holds ZERO and the bosses at full strength. `row[3]` (the 4th number of a wave row) marks the leader row (the last row of each level); only `cfg.rival` fills the leader slot, and only stages 3 and 6 set it. `waveKeep` 3 (stage 1) cuts the leader row away. `waveCut` 1 (stage 6 after "Offer a hand") is the only reward for that choice.
  - A wave has `row[0] + partySize - 1` enemies (3 to 6; 6 at most on screen, `BRAWL_MAX_ENEMIES`); level rows: Warehouse, Rooftop, Garage 3-4-5-6 (18); Subway, Construction Site, Night Club 3-4-5-6-6 (24). Stage 1 plays the first 3 rows (12). A brawl is lost when `TEAM_PLAYERS` has no survivor (`checkRoundEnd`, `endRound`), so a second fighter on the players' team (co-op) keeps the fight alive.
  - CPU fighters of a brawl get `DIFFICULTY[level].dmg` (Easy .75, Normal .85, Hard 1, Expert 1) on their damage and nothing else (`MatchSim` constructor, `enterEnemy`); the people's side gets the stamina edge of P18-33 (`giveStaminaEdge`, for `k < partySize`: this would also hand it to an ally CPU).
  - Survival (`Game.startSurvival`, `nextSurvivalMatch`, `onMatchEnd`): a chain of 1v1 fights against the shuffled roster (customs included), CPU level by wins (Easy under 2, Normal under 4, Hard under 7, Expert from 7), clock 50 s shrinking by 2 s per win past 7 down to 30 s, health carried over and healed by 25 percent per win (down to 10 percent). Records: `Progress.d.survivalKills` (wins in a row) and `survivalSeconds`; the unlock counter `streak` reads `survivalKills` (REID 3, CAMILA 5, NIA 8). A Survival win grades as one round.
  - Customs: a creator record is `{ name, title, arch, tier, moveStyle, defense, attrs, look, ...storyFields }`, checked by `validateCustom` (name A-Z0-9 up to 10, attributes 2 to 9 inside the point budget 44, look options from `LOOK_CHOICES`); `customProfile(record, slot)` turns it into the sim profile (`id`, `arch`, `moveStyle`, `defense`, `attrs`, `range`, `style` from `ARCHETYPES`, `look`). `deriveStats` gives health 85 + 5 x attribute, stamina 60 + 10 x attribute, damage 0.75 + 0.05 x strength. The sim only needs the profile, so a goon does not have to live in a custom slot (the 8 slots belong to the player).
  - The match config `cfg` is plain data and `MatchSim` reads only `cfg` (see P18-42), so a goon theme, an ally list and a group size are `cfg` fields and replays, `SimRunner` and online stay in step.
- Measured for this decision (headless `SimRunner.run` on the 775c3aa code with a throw-away scale hook on the enemies' health, damage and stamina; not committed; reference player KAI as an AI, `evenDamage`; n = 12 to 24 seeds a cell, so each rate wobbles by about +-15 points; P18-21 and P14-04 repeat everything with n = 50 to 100. These runs predate P18-03 (combat timing) and P18-01 (+20 percent movement speed), which land first, so every number below is a starting value to re-measure after them):
  - Three main fighters as one group (a 1v3 on the Warehouse floor, no waves), the Normal AI player: Easy 85, Normal 0, Hard 0, Expert 0 percent wins. This is why the group needs a rule at all; today's Brawl type is lost by the AI at Normal and above.
  - The same group with health x0.4 and damage x0.7 (damage then times the level's own `dmg`): Easy 100, Normal 67 (54 at x0.5 / x0.8), Hard 38 (25), Expert 13 (4). That fits the P14-01 "groups" ladder (Easy 85 or more, Normal 50-70, Hard 25-45, Expert 10-25).
  - 2 / 4 / 5 enemies with health x0.57 / 0.31 / 0.25 and damage x0.82 / 0.61 / 0.54 (the first formula tried): n2 100 / 63 / 19 / 0, n4 100 / 100 / 44 / 19, n5 100 / 100 / 44 / 25 at Easy / Normal / Hard / Expert: the damage factor has to stay 0.7 for every size and only the health shrinks with the count (table below).
  - With one ally (SORA, the AI, at full strength) and one enemy more (the coop rule): Normal 94 to 100, Hard 19 to 56 percent against 44 for the same group without an ally: an ally is worth about one person, so "one more enemy per ally" keeps the fight level (noise is large).
  - Whole levels with waves (Warehouse, 18 enemies in four waves, no health between waves): the 3-enemy factors (health x0.4 / damage x0.7) are far too strong for a crowd that arrives one wave after another: the Normal AI player clears Easy 75, Normal 0, Hard 0 percent (Night Club, 24 enemies: Easy 17). With the Hard AI player (the P14-01 reference) against Normal enemies, health x0.3 / damage x0.55 clears 25 percent, x0.2 / x0.4 and x0.15 / x0.35 clear 92. Health x0.25 / damage x0.5: Easy 100, Normal 58, Hard 50, Expert 8 (Warehouse); the first three waves of the Garage (stage 1): Normal 100, Hard 92; the Night Club at Normal 33. Health x0.3 / damage x0.45: Normal 67, Hard 42, Expert 0. Health x0.35 / damage x0.4: Normal 75, Hard 17, Expert 0. A clear takes 85 to 125 s (one goon down every 5 to 7 s) against 2.5 to 5 min and 6 to 15 s in P14-01, so that pace band is replaced for goon stages (below).
- Decided: Arcade brawls are fought against goons (a new cast of creator characters, never roster presets), one crew per brawl, wearing the colours and fighting in the style of a main fighter; the crews have three variants and a leader; allies come from the story in Arcade (rival A in stage 6) and from a setting in Survival (a "Survival type" row); Survival brawls use main fighters from the roster, scaled by the size of the group; a run has 3 main-fighter bosses. The table of answers to the task's five questions:

  | # | Question | Decided |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 | Arcade allies | from the story, never a player setting: rival A fights beside you in stage 6 after "Offer a hand" and against you after "Walk away"; no other ally in a run. Survival allies: a player setting (the Survival type row) |
  | 2 | Which boss each brawl uses | stage 1 rival A, stage 3 the stage-4 boss, stages 5 and 6 the champion, stage 6 rival A's own crew after "Walk away" (table below) |
  | 3 | Boss cap | 3 main-fighter bosses: rival A, the boss, the champion. Rival B stops being a main fighter and becomes the leader goon of stage 3's last wave |
  | 4 | Goon variants | 3 variants (Bruiser, Scrapper, Runner) plus a leader per crew, built from the boss's data, so 11 crews cost no extra data |
  | 5 | Survival | 4 types (Duels, Brawls, Brawls + 1 ally, Brawls + 2 allies), group size by wins, one more enemy per ally, 4 apart record sets, ally runs give no unlock credit and save no grade |

#### Goons

- A goon is a creator record built in code from a theme fighter (any `ROSTER` id) and a variant, passed to `customProfile`-like code: `goonProfile(themeId, variant)` returns the sim profile of the record `{ name, title, arch, tier: 'standard', moveStyle, defense, attrs, look, ...storyFields() }`. Nothing is saved: no custom slot, no share code, no `CustomRoster` entry, no `ROSTER` / `ROSTER_BY_ID` entry (so no card, no unlock, no Survival or Tournament pick, no ladder, no `Progress` field), and an id of the form `g:<variant>` that cannot clash with a roster id or `c1`..`c8`. A self-test runs every goon through `validateCustom` so the claim "made with the character creator" stays true.
- Theme: the colours of the theme fighter (`top`, `shorts`, `glove`, `shoe`, `accent`; a missing or non-hex value, and the `top` of a bare-top theme, fall back to the neutral grey `#808894`) and its move style and defense type. Not copied: hair style and mask (ZERO's legacy `mask` hair style is not an option of the creator), skin and hair, face, items: those come from the variant. A goon sets every key `validateCustom` requires, because it rejects a record with a missing one: all of `LOOK_CHOICE_KEYS` (hairStyle from the variant; eyes `normal`, brows `thin`, beard `none`, headband `none` except the Runner and the leader `band`, mask `none`, topStyle `tee` for every goon, never `bare`, so the grey top shows even for a bare-top theme, legs `shorts` except the Scrapper `pants`, hands `gloves`, belt `none`, feet `shoes`, glow `off`), all of `LOOK_COLOR_KEYS` (the theme's five colours plus skin and hair from the variant), `scale` and `build`; muscle, weight, age and gender take their defaults. So every one of the 32 preset fighters is a possible theme with no per-theme data.
- Variants (the whole table; attribute order health, stamina, strength, speed, reach, defense, balance, recovery; goon point budget `GOON_BUDGET` 36, a main fighter costs 44 to 46; every attribute 3 to 7, speed never below 4):

  | Variant | Name | Arch (AI personality, range) | Attributes (total) | Style and defense | Look | Role |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0 | BRUISER | brawler | 6 4 6 4 3 4 5 3 (35) | the theme's style and defense | height 1.06, build 1.2, hair style buzz | slow, heavy hits, the front line |
  | 1 | SCRAPPER | striker | 4 5 5 5 4 3 3 5 (34) | the theme's style and defense | height 1.0, build 0.95, hair style short; top and shorts colours swapped | fast hands, average |
  | 2 | RUNNER | kicker | 3 5 3 6 6 3 4 5 (35) | `ARCHETYPE_MOVE_STYLE.kicker` and its default defense (a street kicker whatever the theme) | height 0.96, build 0.85, hair style short with the headband | long reach, light, fragile |
  | L | the leader | brawler | 7 5 7 4 3 4 5 3 (38) | as the Bruiser | the Bruiser's look plus the headband and a name from `NAME_IDEAS` | the strongest goon of the crew |

  Skin and hair colour of the three come from a short list of 3 skin tones and 3 hair colours picked by variant, so a crew is not a clone army; the data is a few dozen bytes. Titles are `The Bruiser`, `The Challenger`, `The Rookie`, `The Closer` (existing `TITLES` indexes; no new titles). Names are the variant names (BRUISER, SCRAPPER, RUNNER), valid under `NAME_PATTERN`; a crew has one leader and the name is per crew: from `NAME_IDEAS` without GRANITE (7 letters; the story text test caps the `{rival2}` token at 6 and every other name has 6 or fewer), index `(run.seed + 5 x crew) % 28` with crew 0 the boss's and crew 1 the champion's, so the two leaders of a run differ (the run's seed does not change on a retry, so the names are stable for the run and for the cutscene). Rival A's crew has no leader goon (stage 1 cuts the leader row; after the walk rival A leads it himself).
- Strength (what makes a goon weaker than a main fighter, three layers): (1) the lower point budget above (health 3 to 6 against 4 to 7: about 5 percent less health and damage on average); (2) a crew factor applied to the derived stats when the goon is built (the same place the Even-teams handicap sets `slot.hp`: `stats.maxHp`, `maxHp`, `hp`, `stats.damage`, `stats.maxStam`, `maxStam`, `stam`); (3) the CPU level's own AI profile and `dmg` as for any enemy. Table (starting values fitted to the stage ladder; the leader has 2x the health factor and 1.3x the damage factor of the crew, so about 0.6 of a main fighter's health at Normal and still weaker than any main fighter):

  | CPU level | Goon health x | Goon damage x (before the level's `dmg`) | Goon stamina x | Reference result (Hard AI player, Warehouse, 18 goons) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Easy | 0.30 | 0.50 | 0.8 | clears 90 percent or more |
  | Normal | 0.30 | 0.45 | 0.8 | clears 50 to 80 percent (the P14-01 band) |
  | Hard | 0.27 | 0.45 | 0.8 | clears 40 to 60 percent |
  | Expert | 0.22 | 0.40 | 0.8 | clears 20 to 40 percent |

  The measured points behind the table: 0.25 / 0.5 gave 100 / 58 / 50 / 8, 0.3 / 0.45 gave 67 at Normal, 42 at Hard and 0 at Expert (the Expert AI alone does it), so Expert needs the lowest factors; the Easy row is interpolated and the Expert row extrapolated beyond the measured range, neither is measured. That is about 30 hit points per goon at Normal (a main fighter has 105 to 135), so a goon goes down in about 5 hits. Stamina x0.8 was not measured apart; P18-21 checks it moves no clear rate by more than 10 points. Stage targets with the same reference (the table's levels, n = 50 each): stage 1 (the first 3 rows, 12 goons) 85 percent or more at Normal and Hard; stages 3 and 5 as the table; stage 6 (24 goons) 40 to 70 percent at Normal (it measured 33 at 0.25 / 0.5; if it stays under 40, stage 6 gets its own factor 0.9 on the goons' health, one number). A clear takes 80 to 160 s and a goon falls every 4 to 9 s (replaces the P14-01 "Brawl stage" pace band of 2.5 to 5 min and 6 to 15 s, which was written for main fighters; the clear-rate band stays). Arcade length: the four brawls take about 60 + 100 + 100 + 120 s of fighting for the Hard AI player, the three duels 50 to 110 s each (P14-01 "Time to KO"), plus 1 minute of Short scenes (the P14-01 rule), so a run is expected at about 11 to 13 minutes, under the P14-01 band of 15 to 25 minutes, which was set when the brawl stages were fought against main fighters. Re-banded: 10 to 16 minutes of simulated fight time plus the scenes (people play slower than the AI, so 13 to 20 minutes in play). The old target is not chased by making goons tougher, which would undo the stage ladder; a longer run would be a content question (more waves), for the owner.
- Waves (what the enemies of a wave are): `waveProfiles` fills a wave with goons when `cfg.goons` is set: goons are dealt in turn by the running counter `waveCursor` that the roster cycle uses today (`waveCursor++ % 3` over the whole match, so the mix does not depend on wave sizes; no random number, the same crew every try); the leader row (`row[3]`) puts the crew's leader in slot 0, or the main fighter `cfg.rival` when one leads. Wave sizes stay as the level data gives them (`row[0] + partySize - 1`, so an ally adds one enemy to every wave, 6 enemies on screen at most) and are not changed by the difficulty: the level rows were set in P8 and the table above already moves the clear rates, and a count shift on top would double-count. Non-Arcade brawls (Free Fight Brawl and Co-op, `BREAKPOINT.brawl`) keep the roster cycle, so their fingerprints and tests do not move.
- Boss cap and the brawl-to-boss map (the cap is 3 main-fighter bosses per run: rival A, the boss, the champion; the player's own fighter is never one of them, with the existing "next in the list" rule; when the player is ZERO the champion is MARA, P10-02, and the theme follows):

  | Stage | Fight | Level | Goon crew (theme) | Last-wave leader | Ally | Why |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | brawl, first 3 wave rows | the region's home level | rival A | none (the leader row is cut, as today) | none | the gym's crew that the invitation (B3) comes from; the stage before the first duel |
  | 3 | brawl, all waves | pool level 1 | the boss (HAO, MARLON or YOAV) | the crew's leader goon (this was rival B); beat B7 stays | none | the crew that waits in front of the stage-4 duel (B8, B9) |
  | 5 | brawl, all waves | pool level 2 | the champion (ZERO, or MARA when the player is ZERO) | the crew's leader goon | none | "the champion's crew" (B11) |
  | 6 | brawl, all waves | the Night Club | after "Offer a hand" the champion; after "Walk away" rival A (his own crew, the same crew as stage 1) | after "Offer a hand" the champion crew's leader goon; after "Walk away" rival A himself (a main fighter, unscaled, as today) | after "Offer a hand" rival A | the champion's club night (B12): rival A either helps, or brings his own crew to settle it |

  Stages 5 and 6 (after the hand) share the champion's crew; they differ in the level, the 24-goon row set and the leader. After the walk, stage 6 is rival A's crew under rival A, so every boss still brings their own goons and a run has three crews and three main-fighter bosses, never four. The three duels keep their opponents (rival A, boss, champion). `Story.start` stops choosing rival B (the `rivals` column of `STORY_TONES` goes; the tone still picks the lines); `run.rivalB` and `Story.idOf('R2')` give way to the leader (below). Old saves: `Progress.d.arcadeRun.rivalB` is read and ignored (`Story.clean` keeps accepting a `word`, `Story.restore` stops using it, `Story.pack` stops writing it); a run saved on stage 3 or later continues under the new rules from its stage start. Preset unlocks lose nothing: the six rival presets are unlocked as rival A (every motive has one), so rival B's exit removes no way to open anyone, but a run now opens one rival (rival A) instead of up to two, so unlocking the six rivals takes more runs; the owner can change this (for example another ally for another motive).
- Cutscenes: the leader is a goon profile, so a scene that puts `R2` on screen (B7) builds its fighter from the profile, not from `ROSTER_BY_ID[id]` (P18-21 changes `Story.idOf` / the scene cast to accept a profile, `Story.name` returns the leader's name, the token `{rival2}` stays and means the leader). The goon crews are never in a scene.
- For P18-35 (the story text): the cast of a run is the player, rival A, the boss and the champion (the three main-fighter bosses), the named leader goon (speaks in B7 as `R2`, token `{rival2}`), and rival A as the ally of stage 6 after the hand; the crews never speak. B12's hand line must now say that rival A fights beside the player (today it only opens "the back door"); `Story.beaten` for stage 3 returns nobody (no rival B to open). The rows for B7 keep their tone keys.
- Stage info and the story screen: "Brawl - <level> led by <leader name>" for stages 3, 5 and 6 (as stage 3 shows rival B today); stage 6 shows "with <rival A>" after the hand and "led by <rival A>" after the walk. The "one enemy fewer" text goes.

#### Allies

- Rules (all modes; the same code for Arcade and Survival): an ally is a CPU fighter of the players' team (`TEAM_PLAYERS`) added after the people in the party, so `partySize` counts it (waves and the HUD row already scale with it) and `AIController` steers it at the run's CPU level; it gets the level's `dmg` factor like an enemy and its own full health; it takes no orders (P1-03 rejected orders and tag switching) and nobody can swap into it. It never takes the stamina edge (only people do: `giveStaminaEdge` must skip allies). The person can never hurt an ally and the ally can never hurt a person (friendly fire is a Free Fight option and is off in Arcade and Survival; a CPU never hurts a teammate anyway). Lock-on, soft facing and the targets of enemies already use the team test, so an ally is never locked and enemies may choose it as a target (that is the point of an ally). An ally always starts a fight at full health and stamina: `placeFighters` gives every party member the carried health (`hpFrac`, `breakpoint.html:10855`), so P18-21 resets allies with 1 and only people get the carry; in Survival the carried health, its 30 percent floor and the heal belong to the person alone. A knocked-out ally stays out until the fight ends and is back at full health in the next fight (Survival draws a new ally each fight).
- Loss rule: a brawl with allies is lost when every person is down, even if an ally still stands, and the clock beats the people as today (`peopleDown()`, used by `checkRoundEnd` and `endRound` instead of `!teamAlive(TEAM_PLAYERS)` when `cfg.allies` is set; without allies nothing changes). Because: otherwise a knocked-out player could be carried to a win by the CPU, and in Survival would then be healed from 30 percent and played on. The fight is won when every enemy is down, whoever landed the blows. A co-op party (people only) is unchanged.
- Enemy count: one more enemy per ally (the coop rule that gives 3 enemies for 1 person and 4 for 2): in waves through `partySize` as today, in Survival through the group table below; the enemies fielded are capped at 6 (allies are not in the cap, so up to 9 fighters can be on screen: 1 person, 2 allies, 6 enemies). Measured as a fair swap (see above). Damage and health of those enemies come from the same table as without an ally.
- Rewards: Arcade is unchanged (a won stage counts 1 for `wins`, `clears` and `best`; the stage grade is computed from the person's stats against the group par, which grows with the extra enemy; no ally penalty). Survival with an ally: records apart, no unlock credit, no saved grade (below).
- HUD: the ally has its own tag in the party row (the same tag and health bar a co-op partner gets, in the players' tint; `TEAM_TINTS` is only a count of 8 tints and no distinct ally tint is added), the "UP" count and the defeat check count people only, the camera does not need the ally in view. Ally dialogue is P18-35's.
- Where allies come from in Arcade (data, one rule): `Story.cfg(run, 6)` sets `cfg.allies = [run.rivalA]` unless `run.choice === 'walk'` (an unset choice reads as `hand`, as the Story scenes: Off default already does, so the ally is there with scenes off too). `waveCut` is dropped (the "one enemy fewer" is replaced by an ally, who brings one more enemy per wave: wave sizes 4-5-6-6-6 = 27 against 24; with the ally the stage is expected to clear at about the same rate, P18-21 measures it, n = 50, within 10 points of stage 6 without an ally at the same level). Rival A as ally is a main fighter at normal strength (no goon factor), because it is a named character who chose to help. A later story row can add allies (`goal:friends` is the obvious next one); the data hook is `cfg.allies`, a list of roster ids, at most 2.

#### Survival

- The setting: one new row on Survival's first screen, "Survival type", under the Difficulty row P18-05 adds (and above Start): Duels (default), Brawls, Brawls + 1 ally, Brawls + 2 allies; a tap steps the four and wraps, left and right step too, nothing else is added (no new on-screen buttons). The label is the whole state, saved as `setups.survival.type` (0 to 3, an integer, anything else falls back to 0) next to P18-05's `difficulty` in P18-19's `breakpoint.v1.setups` key (no version change: a new field with a default). The row is always shown (Survival always has a CPU). Survival has a Difficulty row (P18-05: difficulty in every mode with a CPU, owner 2026-11-01), and this is exactly what it does: today's ramp has four rungs, Easy for fewer than 2 wins, Normal for fewer than 4, Hard for fewer than 7, Expert from 7; Difficulty D is 0 for Easy, 1 Normal, 2 Hard, 3 Expert; the CPU level of fight k is rung min(3, rung(k) + D). Easy (the default) is today's ramp exactly; Expert is Expert from the first fight. The enemy count of a Survival brawl follows the CPU level the fight is played at, not the number of wins (table below), so a higher Difficulty also means a bigger first group (Hard: 3 enemies from fight 1; Expert: 4). The clock and the heal still follow the wins.
- Duels (type 0) at Difficulty Easy is today's Survival byte for byte (the same fights, clock, healing, record fields and unlocks, the same fingerprints; a higher Difficulty only shifts the CPU level as above); the other three types play every fight as a brawl.
- Brawls: the enemies and the ally are main fighters drawn in turn from the shuffled lap (`r.order`, customs included, never the player), so a fight never repeats a fighter and each lap meets everyone; the ally takes the draw after the enemies. No repeat inside a fight, also at the end of a lap: a fight needs up to 8 distinct fighters (6 enemies + 2 allies); it takes them from the front of `r.order`, and when fewer remain than it needs it takes those, reshuffles the pool without the fighters already taken for this fight (`shuffled(r.pool, r.rng)`) and takes the rest from the new order (the pool is 31 fighters or more, so it is always enough). The fight is a plain brawl on the run's level (`ARENA_ORDER` by wins, as today; levels without waves are fine: all enemies start together, in a line, as in Free Fight Brawl), no waves, no goons (goons are the Arcade cast; the Survival cast is the roster, as it is today), `cfg.mode: 'brawl'` (the sim's brawl path) with `cfg.survival: true` (for the HUD line and the grade; the run itself stays `Game.run.mode === 'survival'` and the carried health is `cfg.playerHp`, which `placeFighters` already uses in round 1), `cfg.enemies` a list of ids and `cfg.allies` a list of ids. Group rule, by the CPU level the fight is played at (above) and, for the last row, the wins `k` (fights won in this run):

  | CPU level of the fight | Enemies, no ally | with 1 ally | with 2 allies |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Easy | 2 | 3 | 4 |
  | Normal | 3 | 4 | 5 |
  | Hard | 3 | 4 | 5 |
  | Expert | 4 | 5 | 6 |
  | Expert, 9 wins or more | 5 | 6 | 6 (7 by the rule, capped) |

  Formulas, so the table is not a second source: `enemies = min(6, base(level, k) + allies)` with `BRAWL_MAX_ENEMIES` 6 as the cap on enemies only (the allies are not in it, so up to 9 fighters are on screen: 1 person, 2 allies, 6 enemies; at Expert from 9 wins the 2-ally type is one enemy short of its rule, accepted); `clock = SURVIVAL_TIMER + 8 x (enemies - 1) - 2 x extra(k)` with the floor `SURVIVAL_TIMER_MIN + 8 x (enemies - 1)` (3 enemies 66 s, 6 enemies 90 s; floors 46 and 70 s); `heal = max(0.1, 0.25 + 0.04 x (enemies - 1) - 0.02 x extra(k))` (3 enemies 33 percent, 6 enemies 45); `extra(k)` is today's `survivalExtra`; the CPU level is today's ramp shifted by the Difficulty row (above). Time up in a brawl is a loss, as in every brawl. The loss rule above applies (every person down).
- Group strength (the balance rule for main fighters in a group; the enemies' health shrinks with the count, damage does not): enemy health x0.50 (2 enemies), 0.40 (3), 0.33 (4), 0.28 (5), 0.24 (6); enemy damage x0.70 for every count (times the level's `dmg`); stamina x1; the count is the number of enemies fielded, so an ally's extra enemy is already in it. The ally is a full-strength main fighter (health and stamina x1, damage times the level's `dmg`). Fitted so that the reference player (the Normal AI) wins a 3-enemy group at Easy 100 / Normal 67 / Hard 38 / Expert 13 percent (the P14-01 "groups" ladder); the sizes 2, 4 and 5 are by interpolation of those and the first run above, P18-21 fits each (n = 100). One table, `SURVIVAL_GROUP`, holds the six health factors.
- Records: four apart sets, by type only, not by Difficulty (a higher Difficulty never makes a record easier to reach, so one record per type cannot be gamed; owner-changeable). Type 0 keeps `survivalKills` and `survivalSeconds` exactly as saved today (no migration, old saves read as they are). Types 1 to 3 use `Progress.d.survivalRec`, an array of three `[wins, seconds]` pairs (whole numbers: wins 0 to 9999, seconds 0 to 999999, the clamps `Progress.init` uses for the old fields; a missing, non-array, short or hostile entry is zeros, field by field, never throws). A record is "fights won in a row" and "longest run in seconds" of the chosen type, saved after every win and when a run ends or is quit (`saveSurvivalRecord` writes the bucket of the run's type). The mode list and the results screen show the best of the type that is selected / played ("Best: 5 wins - longest run 6:12"). Because: a run with an ally is easier and a group is harder than a duel, so a shared number would mean nothing (the task's recommended rule); with four buckets the player sees the one they are playing.
- Unlocks: the counter `streak` (REID 3, CAMILA 5, NIA 8) reads the larger of the Duels and the Brawls (no ally) records; ally types never count (they are easier). `UNLOCK_COUNTERS` is a map of counter names to `Progress.d` field names, read by field in two places (`unlockProgressText`, `breakpoint.html:858`, and `Progress.checkUnlocks`, `:933`), so P18-21 adds one helper `Progress.counter(name)` and changes both readers to call it (the other counters return their field). The Progress screen (`breakpoint.html:14227`, "Best survival streak") keeps showing the Duels record; the per-type best is on the mode list and the results screen.
- Grades: a Survival fight is graded as before (one round, or the group par `20 s x enemies` in a brawl); the best Survival grade (`grades.survival`) is saved from the Duels and Brawls types only; an ally fight shows its grade but does not save it.
- Results: the Survival results line keeps "Opponents defeated" for Duels and says "Fights won" for the brawl types; the HUD line reads `SURVIVAL - FIGHT n - <level>` for brawls and keeps `DEFEATED n` for Duels.

#### Save and data shape

- Saved: `Progress.d.survivalRec` (new, array of three pairs, defaults zeros) and `setups.survival.type` (new, 0 to 3, default 0). Changed: `arcadeRun` drops `rivalB` (read and ignored on old saves). Not saved: goons, crews, leaders, allies (all derived).
- `cfg` fields (plain data, read once at the start of a match, missing means off, so every old config and fingerprint is unchanged): `goons` (the theme fighter's id, a string), `goonLeader` (the leader's name, a string, only with `goons`), `allies` (a list of up to 2 roster ids), `rival` (stays: the main fighter who leads the last wave), `survival` (true for a Survival brawl; the group factors are read from the length of `cfg.enemies`). `waveCut` stays in the engine (tests use it) but no story stage sets it.
- Determinism: no new random number anywhere in the sim: variants come from the wave counters, the leader's name from the run seed, the Survival draw from the existing run generator (`makeRng(Date.now())`, as today; a Survival run is not replayable by design, but each fight's `cfg` is). `SimRunner.run` passes the new fields through `matchCfg` (it already takes a free-form `matchCfg`), so `BREAKPOINT.simulate` can balance-test a stage or a Survival group. A goon fight and an ally fight twice with the same `cfg` give the same hash, and the same through `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(cfg))`.

#### Edge cases

  | Case | Result |
  |---|---|
  | The player is a rival, the boss or the champion (the fighter list order picks the next one) | the theme of each stage is whoever fills that role in the run, so the crews follow the run; the player's own fighter is never a theme or an ally |
  | The player is ZERO | the champion is MARA, stages 5 and 6 use MARA's crew |
  | A theme fighter with a bare top (BRUTUS, DMITRI, CRUZ) or the mask hair style (ZERO) | the crew wears the neutral grey top; the mask is not copied |
  | Stage 6 with scenes Off and no choice | `hand`: rival A is the ally |
  | A saved run on stage 3 or later from before this change | continues; stage 3's leader is a goon, not rival B; nothing is lost |
  | Retry of a lost stage | same crew, same leader, same ally; a new seed only changes the fight |
  | Ally and the last enemy both attack | whoever lands the last blow, the win is the team's; credit (combo, kill count) goes to that fighter; the person's grade uses the person's own stats |
  | Person down, ally and enemies still up | the fight ends in defeat after the usual knock-out delay |
  | Ally knocked out, person still up | the fight goes on without it |
  | Survival Brawls on a level with no `waves` row | all enemies start together in a line (as Free Fight Brawl); the cap is 6 enemies, the allies come on top |
  | Survival Brawls + 2 allies at Expert, 9 wins or more | 5 + 2 enemies would be 7, capped at 6: that fight is one enemy easier than the rule, 9 fighters are on screen |
  | Survival run quit in the middle of a fight | the record of the run's type is saved as today; no other record changes |
  | Old Survival record in a damaged save | zeros per field; type 0 reads `survivalKills` as today |
  | Gamepad / touch / keyboard | the Survival type row is a value row like the others (tap steps it, left / right change it); no new button; the ally has no controls |
  | A phone (390x844, 844x390) | the Survival screen has one more row; no horizontal scroll; the sticky row stays visible; the ally tag fits next to the person's in the party row |

#### What P18-21 must build and test

- Order of work: (a) `GOON_VARIANTS`, `GOON_SCALE` and `goonProfile`; (b) the sim: `cfg.goons` / `goonLeader` in `waveProfiles`, the crew factors in the constructor and `enterEnemy`, `cfg.allies` (party, controllers, `partySize`, `giveStaminaEdge` for people only, `peopleDown`), `survival` group health factors; (c) `Story`: theme map, `cfg` fields, leader name, rival B removed (`start`, `pack`, `restore`, `idOf`, `stageInfo`, `STORY_TONES` rivals), the ally on stage 6, the cutscene cast accepts a profile; (d) Survival: the type row, `nextSurvivalMatch`'s brawl branch, group table, records and unlock counter, results and HUD words; (e) saves (`survivalRec`, `setups.survival.type`); (f) HUD ally tag; (g) tests.
- Self-tests (names start `Arcade goons (P18-21):`, `Allies (P18-21):`, `Survival brawls (P18-21):`):
