  1. every crew: for each of the 32 preset ids as theme, the three variants and the leader pass `validateCustom`, cost at most `GOON_BUDGET` 36 (leader 38), carry the theme's colours, style and defense (variants 0, 1, leader), are not in `ROSTER` / `ROSTER_BY_ID`, are in no card list, `opponentChoices`, Tournament pool or ladder, and the ids are the four `g:0`, `g:1`, `g:2`, `g:L` that cannot clash with a roster id or `c1`..`c8` (goons of one wave share the id of their variant, by design: the test plays a stage-3 wave with two goons of one variant up at once and checks that nothing confuses them);
  2. strength: the derived `maxHp`, `damage` and `maxStam` of each variant at each CPU level equal the formula (budget layer x table), every goon is below every main fighter in health and damage, the leader is below a main fighter and above its crew; the Easy / Normal / Hard / Expert rows are the table;
  3. the map: for every preset as the player and for all ten regions, stage 1's crew is rival A's, stage 3's the boss's, stages 5 and 6 the champion's, the main-fighter opponents of a run are at most 3 (rival A, boss, champion), `rivalB` is gone from `Story.start` and `Story.pack`, stage 3's last wave has the leader goon in slot 0 and B7 fires once on it, stage 1 has no leader, stage 6 has the champion crew and its leader goon after the hand and rival A's crew led by rival A after the walk, and that rival A after the walk is the unscaled roster profile (health, damage and stamina equal `deriveStats` of his attributes, times the level's `dmg` only);
  4. waves: goons are dealt in turn by `waveCursor` (variant n % 3 for the n-th goon dealt), counts equal the level rows plus `partySize - 1`, a Free Fight Brawl / Co-op / `BREAKPOINT.brawl` keeps the roster cycle and its recorded fingerprints, and two runs with the same `cfg` (also through `JSON`) give the same hash;
  5. old saves: an `arcadeRun` with `rivalB` loads and continues, a saved stage 3 and 6 start under the new rules, a hostile `rivalB` and a missing one are ignored, `Story.pack` no longer writes it;
  6. allies: Arcade stage 6 has an ally after the hand (also with scenes Off) and none after the walk; the ally is on the players' team, steered by the AI, does not hurt a person and is not hurt by one, is not locked or auto-targeted, takes no stamina edge, may be targeted by enemies; one more enemy per wave; an ally starts at full health and stamina; a knocked-out ally stays down and the fight goes on; the person down with an ally standing is a defeat; the time limit beats the people; a co-op party without allies is unchanged;
  7. Survival: the four types and the saved `setups.survival.type` (damaged values fall back to 0); Duels is bit-identical to today (same `cfg`, same fingerprints, same records); Difficulty shifts the CPU level and so the enemy count as the formula says (Easy is today's ramp byte for byte); an ally starts at full health when the person carries 30 percent, and the heal reads the person only; Brawls: enemies by CPU level and ally count exactly as the table, the draw never repeats a fighter inside a fight, also across the end of a lap, capped at 6, never the player, no repeats in a lap, the ally takes the draw after the enemies, group health factors, damage x0.7, the clock and heal formulas, time up is a loss, the loss rule; the four record sets are independent, a damaged or short `survivalRec` reads zeros, type 0 keeps `survivalKills`, `streak` reads Duels and Brawls (no ally) only, an ally fight saves no grade, a quit saves the run's bucket;
  8. flows: a headless AI run of stages 1 and 3 at every CPU level that ends (no hang, no time cap) with never more than 6 enemies up, a headless Arcade run through all 7 stages with goons on the new `cfg`s (a win and a loss, a retry, a save and continue at stage 6 after the hand), Survival for each type to the second fight, the Survival type row by tap, key and pad, the 390x844 and 844x390 layout check in the browser run, the stage-6 HUD with an ally.
  9. the most fighters: a Survival brawl with 2 allies and 6 enemies (9 fighters) and stage 6 with an ally run for 20 s of game time in the headless runner without an error, never more than 6 enemies up, and the P0-04 low-spec check (viewport 640x360, CPU throttle 4, quality low) in the browser run is not worse than the phase baseline; 10. a headless Survival brawl at the first rung of each CPU level ends inside the fight-length band (25 to 60 s median over 50 seeds, a record in Appendix C, not a pass/fail test).
  - Balance runs P18-21 records in Appendix C (n = 50 to 100, `BREAKPOINT.simulate`, seeds 1 to n): the goon table against the stage targets above; the group table against the P14-01 groups ladder; stage 6 with and without an ally within 10 points; the Normal AI player against Survival Brawls at 0, 1 and 2 allies for the first rung of each CPU level within 15 points of each other; Arcade length (P14-01 row, the 7 stages at Normal, scenes Short, 20 runs) 10 to 16 minutes; Survival brawl fights median 25 to 60 s at the first rung of each CPU level; and the P0-04 performance check at the most fighters on screen (Survival with 2 allies and 6 enemies: 9 fighters; stage 6 with an ally) not worse than the phase baseline.
  - Existing tests that change (fix to the new flow, never weaken): the story and cast tests that read `rivalB`, tone `rivals` or `waveCut` (the unlock test at 4499 that opens "rival B", "Story (P10-03)" at 9623, 9675, 9686, 9705 to 9709, 9761 (the allowed `cfg` keys gain `goons`, `goonLeader`, `allies`), 9780, the B7 tests from 10257 to 10440, and the saved-run test at 10600), the Survival tests that count record fields or the HUD line, and the Group AI tests that count `partySize` (an ally is counted).
- Build notes (read before starting):
  - Size: the file measures 1,189,162 bytes (LF, BOM) of 1,200,000, so 10,838 are free today. Every task that lands before P18-21 draws on that: P17-02 (animations: little), P17-17 (a comment-only trim, scheduled before P17-10 and before P18-43; about 218 KB of the file is comments, so it is the one task that gives bytes back), P17-10 (the new style animations, the reason P17-17 exists), P18-03 + P18-04 (timing made readable: flashes, sounds, a Training readout, help text), P18-01 (a speed constant), P18-19 (meant to lower the size), P18-05 (the Difficulty row on more screens, about 800 by estimate) and P18-43 (up to 7,600). Only P18-43 and P18-05 have an estimate, so the free bytes when P18-21 starts are not known today. The size gate is a scheduled step, not a hope: the first step of P18-21 measures the free bytes; if fewer than 5,300 remain, a second comment-only trim (the P17-17 method, comments only, run alone) is done before any P18-21 code, and only then the cuts below. Estimate for P18-21, in bytes: goon variants, builder and the table 1,000; crew factors 450; wave and leader code 450; Story (theme map, `cfg`, leader, profile cast, stage info) 750 less 250 for the removed tone rivals; allies in the sim (party, loss rule, stamina-edge guard, tag) 900; Survival type row and `setups` field 450; the brawl branch with the group table, clock and heal 600; records, clamps, unlock counter and results words 900; total about 5,250 (range 4,800 to 5,800). If fewer than 5,300 bytes remain even after the second trim, P18-21 applies these cuts in order, each with what it saves: (1) leader as the Bruiser with a name and the factor, no separate record, about 200; (2) flat clock and heal, no per-enemy term, about 150; (3) no "Fights won" / "FIGHT n" wording (the Duels words for every type), about 60; (4) no 2-ally type (three types), about 150; (5) one shared `SURVIVAL_GROUP` row instead of five health factors (health x0.4 for every size), about 80. Together about 640. If that is not enough, do not shrink anything else: ask the coordinator for the owner's choice between the Survival types (about 1,950 bytes in all: the type row, the brawl branch and the records) and one more comment-only trim; splitting the file is allowed only at the wall (owner rule 2026-11-02).
  - Reuse, do not rebuild: `customProfile` / `validateCustom` shape for goons, `ARCHETYPES`, `NAME_IDEAS`, `TITLES`, `waveCursor` and the leader row, `partySize`, the co-op enemy rule, `optRow`, `Store` / `readJson` and the clamps of `Progress.init`, the `setups` key of P18-19.
  - Hard parts: (1) `Fighter.id` and the id lookups: grep `ROSTER_BY_ID[` on fighters and profiles inside a match (alternate looks, results, HUD names, credits, `Story.beaten`) because a goon id is not in the table; (2) `giveStaminaEdge` runs for `k < partySize` and would hand an ally the edge; (3) `partySize` is used as "the players' side" in the HUD, the camera, the results and the revive code: with allies it must still mean people for the defeat rule and the "UP" count; (4) the cutscene cast for the leader (a profile, not an id); (5) `Story.cfg` and the tests that list its keys; (6) the Survival record writes (`saveSurvivalRecord`, the quit path) must use the run's type bucket; (7) CrowdDirector with AI on both sides: lineups already run it with CPU teams, but check an ally does not starve the enemies of attack tokens (the enemy count rule is the safety); (8) `Progress.init` clamps and the unlock counter readers (two places, below); (9) `placeFighters` carries the person's health to every party member: allies must reset with 1; (10) goons of one wave share the id of their variant, so grep every place that tells fighters apart by `id` (the alternate-look test `other.id === e.profile.id`, results, stats, credits) and key them by index.
  - The hazards the sim already has (items, hazards, breakables) are untouched; Arcade and Survival rules stay as P18-42 lists them.
- Rejected: goons for Survival (the owner asked for Arcade; the roster is the Survival cast and the group factors balance it); hand-written goon records (11 crews x 3 variants about 400 bytes each is about 13,000 bytes against about 1,000 for the builder); goons stored in custom slots or share codes; any fourth main-fighter boss; keeping rival B as a main fighter that only leads the wave (the cap is 3); allies with orders or tag switching (P1-03); an Arcade ally setting for the player (the newer owner text makes Arcade allies a story matter); allies that carry the fight when the person is down; strong allies in a group fight without the extra enemy; changing wave sizes by CPU level (double-counting); one shared Survival record for every type; a mixed Survival type (a duel, a duel, a brawl): it makes the record ambiguous; counting ally runs for unlocks or grades; goon health from the creator attributes alone (the attribute floor is 2, so health stays 95 or more, which is no weaker than a main fighter by enough).
- Decided, with the owner able to change each (none blocks P18-21): (1) Arcade allies come only from the story, one rule: rival A on stage 6 after "Offer a hand"; more can be added as data. (2) The map brawl to boss: stage 1 rival A, stage 3 the boss, stages 5 and 6 the champion, and stage 6 rival A's own crew after "Walk away". (3) Rival B becomes the leader goon of the boss crew in stage 3; the cap is 3. (4) 3 variants plus a leader per crew; every preset fighter can be a theme. (5) Goon strength by the table (about a quarter to a third of a main fighter's health, 40 to 50 percent of its damage, 80 percent of its stamina). (6) Survival gets four types, groups by the CPU level of the fight, one more enemy per ally, records apart, no unlock or grade from ally runs. (7) Survival brawls use roster fighters with group factors, not goons. (8) The pace band of the P14-01 brawl stage changes for goons (80 to 160 s, one goon down every 4 to 9 s), and the Arcade length band becomes 10 to 16 minutes. (9) "Made with the character creator" means built in code in the creator's record format and checked by `validateCustom`, not records the owner can open and edit in the creator: hand-made records for 11 crews x 3 variants would cost about 13,000 bytes; the owner can ask for editable ones at that cost. (10) A run opens one rival (rival A) instead of up to two, so unlocking the six rivals is slower. (11) Survival's Difficulty row shifts the whole ramp (Expert starts at Expert and with 4 enemies). No owner-only question stops P18-21 from starting; the one thing to watch is the size budget: the size gate in the build note is a scheduled first step of P18-21, not an option.

### P17-18 touch crouch by stick-down decided (2026-11-06)

- Decided by the coordinator's delegate under the owner's standing go ("balanced", no new button, keep it tiny); the owner can change any row. Source: P17-01 audit F12 (Medium). No game code was written; the implementing task is "new task after P17-18" (the coordinator creates it).
- What the code does today (base d751426, read, not run): touch crouch is the held DUCK button (`TOUCH_BUTTONS`), `touchHeld.crouch` > `held.crouch` > `it.crouch` in `fillIntent`. `it.crouch` is what makes a Punch / Kick low (`applyAttackPress`: `it.height = it.crouch || touchAttackHeight(...)`; `touchAttackHeight` only aims at a foe that is already crouched or high-guarding), what makes Defend a low guard (`updateFree`: `BLOCK_L`), the low grip of Grapple and the crouch walk (`CROUCH_FACTOR` 0.45). DUCK sits next to the stick, so a person who walks and wants a low move needs the left hand on two controls and the right thumb on a third. The gamepad has the stick gesture "tap down, then hold down" (`padCrouch`; tap at most `PAD_CROUCH_TAP_MS` 200, second press within `PAD_CROUCH_REARM_MS` 250). Touch has a fixed stick (`buildTouch`: radial dead zone 0.15, `touchStick.x/y` in -1..1 of the radius, y positive = down) and no stick crouch. A double flick of the touch stick is already a sidestep or a run (`touchDirections`, `touchRunTicks` > `TOUCH_RUN_TICKS` 27), and the stick has no jump (JUMP is a button), so down is the one stick direction with no gesture on it. Movement is on `Settings.v.movement` "screen" by default: stick down = toward the camera, which is across the fighter's line to a foe that stands left or right, and along it only when the foe is between the fighter and the camera.
- The forks and the pick:

  | Fork | Options | Pick | Why |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | In addition to DUCK, or instead | add / replace | add: DUCK stays | DUCK is the only crouch that works with a stick pointing anywhere (crouch walk sideways, low guard while backing off), costs nothing now, needs no layout change (the P2-04 overlap test and P17-06 USE test stay as they are) and is the fallback for a thumb that cannot make the gesture. Removing it frees one button but changes the layout the owner has seen |
  | The gesture | (a) pull straight down and hold, (b) tap, then hold, like the pad, (c) pull past the rim, (d) a toggle | (a) | (b) on glass is the touch double flick, which already sidesteps (a sidestep fires at the second press, and it costs stamina), and a failed double flick that lingers would crouch; (c) has no room: at 844x390 the stick centre is about 85 px above the bottom edge with a radius of 65 px, so a pull to 1.3 radii lands on the edge of the screen (portrait 390x844: within about 13 px); (d) a stuck crouch is worse than a held stick (P17-06 rejected it) |
  | What the crouch does while the stick is down | crouch walk toward the camera / crouch in place | crouch walk (the same state as DUCK) | Smallest code (no movement edit), same as DUCK and the pad, and a slow creep is a milder surprise than a fighter that stops dead |
  | Cone, pull and hold | see the numbers | below | measured against the cases in the next bullet |
  | Cue | knob colour, DUCK lit | none | the fighter's own crouch pose is the cue; the pose shows 0.3 s after the pull and the DUCK button state cannot be lit without a second writer to its `.on` class |

- The numbers (`touchStickDuck`, called from `fillIntent` once per tick like `touchRunTicks`, so it counts ticks and not frames):
  - Arm: `touchStick.active`, `y >= 0.8` of the stick radius (down) and `|x| <= 0.4 * y` (a cone of 21.8 degrees each side of straight down; the pull along the line is 0.8 straight down and up to 0.86 at the cone edge, about 52 to 56 px at 844x390), for `TOUCH_DUCK_TICKS` 18 ticks in a row (0.30 s, longer than the 0.28 s double-tap window and the 0.20 s pad tap, shorter than the 27-tick run). Leaving the cone for one tick resets the count.
  - Keep: once armed, the crouch stays while `y >= 0.5` and `|x| <= 0.7 * y` (35 degrees each side). So a thumb that drifts while holding does not stand the fighter up, and walking on from the crouch needs a real turn.
  - Release: a tick outside the keep cone, a finger lift, `releaseAll` or `inputReleased` stands the fighter up at once, and arming needs the full 18 ticks again.
  - Also, and why:
    1. It arms only while `|it.fwd| <= 0.5` (checked after the screen / opponent conversion, on arming only). `it.fwd` above 0.5 is a push toward the foe, which is the run; `it.fwd` below -0.5 is backing off, and a crouch there would turn a held Defend of a Block fighter into a low guard (`BLOCK_L`, `updateFree` 5991) after 0.3 s, so a mid or high strike would hit (`BLOCK_H` guards only H and M, `BLOCK_L` only L: 6886-6887). A crouch that already armed is not stood up by a later change of `it.fwd` (a deliberate crouch is not dropped because the foe moves round); only a fresh one is refused. Plainly: in "opponent" movement stick-down is always backing off (`it.fwd` -1), so there the stick crouch is off and DUCK is the only touch crouch; in "screen" movement (the default) it works whenever the foe is within 60 degrees of sideways on the screen (foe 60 to 120 degrees from screen-down). Between `it.fwd` 0.5 and 0.92 in size (foe roughly below or above on the screen) stick-down neither crouches nor runs (the run needs more than 0.92 for 27 ticks), so the gesture is inert there; DUCK works as ever. The Controls row says "Stick down (hold = crouch)", true for the default setting; a note for "opponent" is not worth the bytes.
    2. A JUMP press wins, and that needs one clause in the sim. The jump check in `updateFree` (5987) refuses a jump from `S.CROUCH`, and the state it reads is the one the previous tick's `this.to(target)` (5997) left; `readIntents` (11013) fills every intent before the fighters update. So clearing `it.crouch` on the jump tick is not enough: the fighter is still in CROUCH and the press (already taken from the latch) is lost. Dropping the state from the input layer is rejected (a replay or an online peer that feeds only intents would not reproduce it). Pick: a new intent flag `unduck`, set by `fillIntent` as `it.unduck = it.jump && this.touchDuck` (the stick crouch was on the tick before), cleared in `makeIntent` / `clearIntent`, and the jump guard reads `(this.state !== S.CROUCH || it.unduck)`. CROUCH to JUMP is a legal transition (`TRANSITIONS` 4198). The stick crouch also drops on that tick (count 0). Only touch ever sets the flag, so keyboard, gamepad and CPU intents read the guard as before and no fingerprint changes; DUCK + JUMP keeps the old rule (both cancel at 1263, so `it.jump` is false) and the one new rule is that the stick crouch never eats a jump. The flag is part of the intent, so the P12 online intent format must carry it.
    3. DUCK held and the stick crouch together are one crouch (`||`).
- Accidental crouches while walking, by case (hand-worked from the numbers; the test below proves them):

  | Walk | Stick | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | Diagonal, toward the camera and sideways | 45 degrees from down (x equal to y) | never arms; to arm at 22 degrees the thumb has to be nearly straight down |
  | Stick swept round the rim, one turn in 140 ticks (2.3 s) or faster | passes the 43.6 degree arm cone in 17 ticks or fewer | no tick of crouch (a slower sweep that rests in the cone for 18 ticks does crouch: that is the pull-and-hold) |
  | Backing off with Defend held (Block fighter) | "opponent" movement: any stick-down; "screen": foe above, `it.fwd` -1 | never arms (`it.fwd` below -0.5), so the guard stays `BLOCK_H` |
  | Running at a foe who is below on the screen | `it.fwd` above 0.92 | never arms, the run wins as today |
  | Foe roughly below or above, `it.fwd` between 0.5 and 0.92 in size | stick straight down | neither crouch nor run (inert); DUCK still works |
  | A thumb that wobbles +-10 degrees around a 35 degree walk | 25 to 45 degrees | outside the arm cone, so never arms |
  | Straight toward the camera, shorter than 0.30 s | cone, 17 ticks or fewer | no crouch (a step) |
  | Straight toward the camera with the foe to the side, 0.30 s or more | cone, 18 ticks | crouches (the one accepted cost: a person who walks straight down the screen for more than 0.3 s gets a crouch walk; they stay standing by keeping the thumb more than 22 degrees off straight down, and once crouched they stand up again by going past 35) |
  | Double flick down (sidestep) | two short presses | sidesteps as today, never crouches (each press is far under 18 ticks) |
  | Already crouched, thumb relaxes to 0.6 | y >= 0.5, inside 35 degrees | stays crouched |

- Interactions:
  - Sway direction: unchanged. `startSway` reads `it.side` / `it.fwd` and not `it.crouch`, so a Sway fighter that presses Defend with the stick down sways the way the stick points, crouched or not (a Sway fighter has no hold-guard, so a crouch does not turn its Defend into a low guard; that is today's rule for DUCK too).
  - Jump, run, backing off, sidestep: above.
  - Items and buttons: USE, LOCK and the throw / set-down timing are untouched (P17-06: crouch has no item job, so a stick crouch cannot change a pick-up, throw or set-down). GRAB while the stick crouch is on is the low grip, exactly as with DUCK. PUNCH, KICK and DEFEND become low with the stick, with no DUCK finger: that is the point of the task. Fight screens only: menus never call `fillIntent`.
  - Other devices: keyboard, gamepad and co-op seats do not call this path (their `touchStick` is never active), so their crouch is as before. The one sim line (the `unduck` clause in the jump guard) reads a flag only touch sets, so no fingerprint, `BREAKPOINT.simulate` run or CPU behaviour changes: the crouch and the flag enter only as intent.
  - Text: `TOUCH_LABELS.down` becomes "Stick down (hold = crouch)" so the Controls screen and the Move list say it; `defend` keeps "DEFEND (with DUCK: low)" and the aria labels keep "with Duck" (both stay true). No tip or tutorial text names DUCK today (only the labels do), so no tip is added (tips still show once only).
- Rejected: replacing DUCK; the pad's tap-then-hold on touch; a deep pull past the stick rim; a crouch toggle; crouching in place (zeroing the stick while crouched: a freeze is a worse surprise than a slow creep, and it is more code); a hold shorter than 15 ticks (a straight walk toward the camera would crouch within a step) or longer than 24 (a pull then Punch within 0.4 s would still be a standing punch); a wider arm cone (a thumb at 30 degrees is a walk); a floating stick that appears under the thumb (F17, a layout change, and it would drop the 20 px Safari swipe-back margin); stick up as a jump (the pad does it with a firm quick tap; on touch the up flick is the run double-flick and the JUMP button works).
- Self-tests the implementation needs (breakpoint-tests.js, group 'Touch stick crouch (P17-18)', not counted in the file size; each fails on the old code):
  1. Cone table (a foe to the side, "screen" movement; the test sets `touchStick` directly as x = m sin(a), y = m cos(a) for pull m and angle a from straight down, so every row is a real stick position inside the circle): crouches at tick 18 and not at tick 17 for (m, a) = (1.0, 0), (1.0, +-15), (1.0, +-21), (0.86, 0), (0.86, +-15), (0.81, 0); never crouches in 120 ticks for (1.0, +-23), (1.0, +-30), (1.0, +-45), (1.0, 90), (1.0, 180), (0.81, +-15) (y 0.78), (0.79, 0), (0.6, 0).
  2. Diagonal and sweeping walks: (a) 600 ticks at pull 1.0 and at 0.6 at angle 45 degrees from straight down on either side, with a +-10 degree wobble; (b) the stick swept round the rim at one turn in 120 ticks with the same wobble and at one turn in 140 ticks without it (at 140 the stick rests about 17 of the 18 arming ticks in the cone, so a wobble could arm it), in both directions, for 600 ticks: zero crouch ticks and `intent.crouch` never true, in "screen" movement with the foe to the side. A sweep slower than one turn in about 148 ticks rests in the arm cone for 18 ticks by design and is not part of this test.
  3. Hold time and release: exactly 17 ticks no, 18 ticks yes; lifting the finger stands up on the next tick; a flick of 12 ticks never; the existing 'Touch: double flicks sidestep' test still passes unchanged and the second flick of a double flick does not crouch.
  4. Keep cone: armed, slide to 30 degrees at pull 0.6 stays crouched for 60 ticks; to 40 degrees or pull 0.45 drops at once and needs 18 ticks to arm again.
  5. Low moves with the stick alone (no DUCK): PUNCH and KICK intents have `height` true and play the crouch string rows (reuse the string-row helper of 'Touch: every string row'), DEFEND gives `defend && crouch` and a Block fighter reaches `BLOCK_L`, GRAB gives the low grip, the fighter walks at the crouch speed (0.45) toward screen-down; DUCK held at the same time is one crouch, and releasing the stick keeps the DUCK crouch.
  6. JUMP wins, through the real fighter update (not only the intent): stick crouch armed and the fighter in `S.CROUCH`, then a JUMP press: that tick has `jump` true, `unduck` true and `crouch` false, and after the fighter update its state is JUMP (it fails if the guard still reads only `state !== S.CROUCH`); DUCK + JUMP still cancels both (the old rule, unchanged); a CPU or keyboard intent with `jump` and a CROUCH state is still refused (the guard changed only for `unduck`).
  7. Run wins and backing off: "screen" movement, foe on the camera line below the fighter (stick down is toward the foe): 40 ticks of full stick down give `run` true and no crouch; with the foe to the side the same input crouches at tick 18 and does not run; with the foe above (stick down = backing off) and with "opponent" movement, 60 ticks of stick down never crouch.
  7b. Backing off while guarding: a Block fighter holding Defend with the stick straight down for 60 ticks, foe above on the screen, and the same in "opponent" movement: the state stays `BLOCK_H` and a mid strike is blocked, never `BLOCK_L`. Foe angle sweep in "screen" movement (stick straight down, 40 ticks): the fighter crouches only for foe angles 60 to 120 degrees from screen-down; at 45 and 135 degrees (`it.fwd` 0.71) it neither crouches nor runs.
  8. Sway: a Sway fighter with the stick crouch on and Defend pressed starts a sway with the same `swayDir` as the same input with the crouch forced off.
  9. Items: with the stick crouch on, USE picks up / throws / (held 30 ticks) sets down exactly as in 'Use (P17-06)'; LOCK works; no `setDown` without the hold.
  10. Reset and other devices: `releaseAll`, `inputReleased` and a stick lift clear `touchDuck` and the count; keyboard and gamepad players never get it; `TOUCH_BUTTONS` still has DUCK and `TOUCH_LAYOUT` is unchanged (the P2-04 and P17-06 overlap tests pass as they are); `TOUCH_LABELS.down` says crouch.
  11. Sim unchanged for everyone else: the headless fingerprints and the P0-06 runner results are unchanged (`unduck` is never set outside touch).
- Size (a draft was written to a temp file and counted): about 990 bytes of the 10,838 free (1,189,162 of 1,200,000 LF today): the two constants about 250, the two fields about 95, the method about 480 (the `Math.abs` on `it.fwd` and the `unduck` line included), the `||` in the crouch line about 25, the `unduck` field in `makeIntent` / `clearIntent` and the jump guard about 55, the label text 16 (about 740 in all if the comments are cut). Hard cap for the task 1,100; if it runs over, cut the comments first. The self-tests are in `breakpoint-tests.js`, which is not counted. Every task before P18-21 draws on the same bytes, so the P18-21 size gate (build note above) starts with about 990 fewer: P18-21's first step measures it, as written. (The first draft said about 900; the jump flag and the backing-off guard added about 90.)
- Owner device checks (added to P16-03 by the coordinator; a phone is needed, none was available here): (1) walk a diagonal circle round a foe for 30 s on a small phone (360 wide) and a large one in portrait and landscape: the fighter never ducks; (2) pull the stick straight down and hold: the fighter ducks in about 0.3 s, and Punch, Kick and Defend are low with the right thumb only; (3) walk straight toward the camera for a second: the crouch comes after 0.3 s, and a thumb more than 22 degrees to a side keeps the walk standing; say if that ever feels like an accident; (4) JUMP while the thumb is down (crouch showing) jumps at once, on the first press; (4b) with a Block fighter, hold DEFEND and back away with the stick for 2 s in each Movement directions setting: the guard stays high and a punch to the head is blocked, the fighter never drops to a low guard; in the "opponent" setting the stick never ducks (only DUCK does); (5) a double flick down still sidesteps and a flick up and hold toward a foe still runs; (6) DUCK still works alone and with the stick; (7) the thumb reaches 0.8 of the stick radius straight down at 844x390 without leaving the screen (about 33 px clear of the bottom edge there).
- Found, not fixed (not touch, out of scope): by reading the code, the pad's tap-then-hold can also latch a double-tap on Down (`press` sets `dtap` if two presses come within 0.28 s), which in screen movement is a sidestep before the crouch; a slow enough gesture avoids it. Nothing was run on a pad; a pad check can go in P16-03 if the owner wants it settled.
- Implementing task: new task after P17-18 (the coordinator creates it): `touchStickDuck` (with the `|it.fwd| <= 0.5` arming test), the two constants, the two fields, the `unduck` intent flag (`makeIntent`, `clearIntent`, set in `fillIntent`) and the `unduck` clause in the jump guard of `updateFree` (5987), the `fillIntent` order change (jump first, then crouch), the label text, the self-tests above, the REFERENCE entry for the finished numbers. Depends on nothing; no other task changes `fillIntent`.

### P17-09 distinct styles decided (2026-11-06)

- Owner (playtest of 2026-10-31 and the brief of this task): "Capoeira has no acrobatic moves", "the styles look very similar", and styles share moves only where the real arts do ("kickboxing and boxing punches"). Decided by the coordinator's delegate under the owner's standing go; the owner can change any line ("Decided: X (because ...); owner can change"). This task changed no game code and no test. Base: `main` at 2895a01 (P17-01 audit in); revised after the Opus review of the first version (24 findings, all taken). Everything measured here comes from probes run on that commit's tables (temp scripts, not kept; each is described so P17-10 can turn it into a self-test): (a) the 16 guard poses rebuilt from `GUARD_ORTHO` and `applyStyleGuard` (all 120 pairs), (b) the 21 new move rows against the damage-per-tick and advantage bands of the existing style-move test, (c) the 16 string tables expanded to move identities (row plus animation) and checked for heights, positions, prefixes, overlap and animations. Not measured (no game code may run in this task): the real hit-check distances, the plant of inverted poses, the CPU's use of the new moves and every win rate; P17-10 measures those first (below).
- This entry replaces the P1-04 rule "a family shares one animation set" (the Families table above): the unit that may be shared is now a single technique, and only where the real arts share it (the share list below). It does not change the 16 styles, their ids, their families as labels, their default defenses or the 32 preset fighters.
- Order of work (the program has moved since the audit): P18-03/04 (combat timing, being built) and P18-01 (movement speed) land **before** P17-10, so P17-10 builds on their timing numbers, their re-recorded hashes and their links test, and updates the links test and the fingerprints **once**, after them (a separate commit, below). P17-02 (hit check) and P17-17 (comment trim) also come first.

**Step 1 - what the audit found (F04 to F07, F14 of the P17-01 audit)**

| Fact | Now | Target of this decision |
|---|---|---|
| Moves only one style plays | 11 of 32 (Wing Chun 2, Muay Thai 2, Taekwondo 2, Kali 2, Hapkido 1, Krav Maga 1, MMA 1); the 7 original styles 0 | every style at least 3 strike moves nobody else plays (5 counting throws, hold, counters, clinch), listed in the Move list |
| Identical string tables | 3 pairs (Kickboxing and Brawler, Wrestling and Sambo, Judo and BJJ) | none; no two styles share a table, and shared moves are on the share list |
| Capoeira | 13 moves, none acrobatic | Armada, Au Batido (cartwheel kick), Bananeira (handstand kick), Flip Kick (air), Rasteira sweeps, Cabecada |
| Guard poses | closest pair 0.133 rad (Capoeira, BJJ) by pose distance, largest single joint 0.08; 38 of 120 pairs under 0.25 on the largest joint | pose distance at least 0.38 for all 120 pairs (the retune below gives 0.399) |
| Throws and holds | 3 throw sets for 14 styles, 2 style throws; 4 hold poses for 6 hold styles (Wrestling and Judo share the pin, BJJ and MMA the armbar) | own throws in the cells each style favours (7 strikers one each, 8 grapplers two each, 17 new poses; the cells nobody favours stay the generic game throws), 6 hold poses, one per hold style |
| Size | about 4,564 bytes left of 1,200,000 (the html is about 1,195,436 LF bytes since P18-19; the audit had 10,838) | estimate below: about 20 KB; a gate of about 30 KB free, measured when P17-10 starts |

  "Pose distance" (used everywhere below) = the square root of the sum of the squared differences of the 26 joints between two guard poses, in radians. It is not a root-mean-square: the true RMS is that number divided by the square root of 26 (0.133 is an RMS of 0.026, 0.399 one of 0.078). The thresholds are stated on this distance.

**Step 2 - decided**

- DECIDE (1) what a move is, and what may be shared. Decided: a **move is a frame row plus an animation builder** (identity `row_id@anim`, for example `heavy_body_kick@liver`; the vocabulary below always writes the real row ids). The frame row owns every number that affects a fight (startup, active, recovery, damage, stamina, stun, push, step, kind, height, tier); the animation owns how it looks. Two styles play the same move only if row and animation are both equal, and then both styles must stand on the share list (a subset test, see the self-tests). A style may play a shared frame row with its own animation and name (a **skin**); that is a different move. Because: it is the one definition the test can check, it keeps the frame rows the P18-02 numbers describe and lets the Karate Cross and the Boxing Cross be two different moves that cost 30 bytes, not 330. It does **not** keep the P18-02 link and escape tables: those depend on which row follows which, and the new tables change that (DECIDE 3, and the proof under the balance targets). Owner can change.
- DECIDE (2) how it is built (data-driven, no new per-move code). Decided: (a) **skins**: `SKINS[style][row] = [name, anim, limb?, reach?, width?, cat?]`, resolved once per style and row into a cached clone of the ATTACKS row (same `id`, so events, hashes and the Training panel keep reading `mv.id`); a skin may change only these six fields, never a number of the table above (self-test). (b) **new rows**: `variant(baseId, id, name, anim, over)` copies a frame row and changes the fields it names (about 140 bytes a row with its name, tier and flags). (c) **animations as data**: `ANIM_SPECS[name] = [baseBuilder, spec]` builds `STRIKE_BUILDERS[name] = (p, s) => { STRIKE_BUILDERS[base](p, s); applyTech(p, spec, s); }`, and `isKickAnim` follows the base; the spec language is the one `STYLE_TECH` already uses (`arm`, `off`, `sw`, `leg`, `base`, `front`, `rear`, `lean`, `twist`, `roll`, `head`, `fwd`, `drop`) plus five keys: `pitch`, `brol` (body roll), `yaw`, `rdrop` (root drop) and `pre` (a wind-up spec that replaces the kick chamber, for the acrobatic moves). **An animation name that is used on rows of different heights names one base per height**: `[{ H, M, L }, spec]` (a Mid row such as a body jab must not get the pose of a High punch; F03 is exactly that mismatch); 10 names need it (`collar`, `stompK`, `haymaker`, `thrust`, `ap`, `boot`, `guard`, `palm`, `thrustK`, `kaliKick`), a few bytes each. An animation costs one line of 4 to 6 numbers, about 72 bytes. (d) **string tables as positions**: each style row gets `strings` = 18 tokens `P1 K1 PP2 PK2 KP2 KK2 PPP3 PPK3 PKP3 KPP3 KKP3 KKK3 cP1 cK1 cPP2 cKK2 cPPP3 cKKK3` (the step of that press in that position; the row of the string table is the prefix steps, so rows are always prefix-consistent), parsed into the `stand`/`crouch` tables by `stringsFor`; the token `-` means no step: that row does not exist and the press starts a new string (only Wrestling and Judo use it, DECIDE 5); `STYLE_STRINGS`, `rowsFrom` and the neighbour chaining go. Because: the existing pieces (`STYLE_TECH`/`applyTech`, `weaponAttackPose` key poses, `STRIKE_SPIN`) already do this job; the only new interpretive code is the skin cache, the position parser, the five spec keys, the roll and the height bases. Owner can change.
- DECIDE (3) strong moves, positions and heights. Decided: the **8 strong frame rows stay the only guard-breakers**; no ninth strong row (the Running Lunge is a tier-3 context move and is not one of the 8). A style plays 3 to 7 of them, each as its own skin (a style whose real art has no such technique does not play it: Boxing has no leg finisher, Capoeira no punch finisher). The strong rows stay in the positions P18-02 analysed (`PPP3 PPK3 PK2 KPP3 KKP3 KKK3 cPPP3 cKKK3`); the one exception is decided in DECIDE 5 (Wrestling and Judo at step 2). Tier 2 rows may stand in a strong position (Taekwondo already has the Jump Round Kick at PK2). **Crouch positions (`c...`) hold only Mid or Low rows** (the crouch press is the low line: `touchAttackHeight` and the crouch rows of every style assume it, and a High strike whiffs on a crouched foe); a High row never stands in a crouch position (test 3). Because: the 8 rows carry the guard-break numbers and moving a row changes which step follows which, so the links change anyway and are recomputed (below). Owner can change.
- DECIDE (4) Boxing has **no leg moves**. Decided: the Kick press of Boxing plays body punches (Bob and Weave Hook, Body Hook, Liver Shot, Clinch Shove); its air Kick is a punch (Leaping Cross, the `jump_kick` row with an arm skin and its own animation `airCross`, a body turned into the punch, so that it does not look like the shared Air Strike on the Punch button; RA, reach 1.25); its clinch Kick strike is a body shot (new clinch set `boxing`: a short hook to the ribs where the other sets have a knee; the set carries its own strike names, "Clinch Body Shot" for the knee slot, because the Move list reads the names from the clinch move table). The buttons keep their captions, the Move list names the moves. The one exception, G4 below: while a Boxing fighter holds an item or carries a crate, its Kick steps fall back to the default kick rows (the item takes the punching hand). Because: the owner's own example is that boxing has punches and kickboxing has punches plus kicks; a boxer with a roundhouse is the thing he does not want. Alternative (rename the buttons per style) costs a second caption table on every layout for 16 styles; rejected, device check added. Owner can change.
- DECIDE (5) the grappling arts get strikes that are grips, and Wrestling and Judo keep their short strings. Decided: Wrestling, Judo, BJJ (and Sambo's boot work) throw grip-fighting moves in the P and K slots (Snap Down, Shoulder Bump, Collar Pull, Foot Sweep, Guard Kick), hit-volume strikes with small damage and large stun like every other light row; they are not damage dealers, their weight is in the throws and holds. **Wrestling and Judo keep the two-step shape P1-04 and P3-10 gave them** ("a stagger, a knockdown ... within grab range and holdable", at most 2 steps, the strong step second): all 3-step positions are `-`, and their strong rows stand at step 2 (`PP2 PK2 KP2 KK2 cPP2 cKK2`); the other 14 styles lose the few `null` rows they had (Boxing KKK, Karate PPP and KPP, Capoeira PPP and KPP) because their finishers are re-assigned by this table. Because: those arts have no strikes in their rules, so inventing punches would be the same copying the owner objects to, and the short string is what P14-01 balanced them on. Owner can change.
- DECIDE (6) Capoeira and the `duck` flag. Decided: three acrobatic rows (Armada, Au Batido, Bananeira), one acrobatic air move (Flip Kick), low sweeps (Rasteira, Rasteira Takedown) and Cabecada; its hand strike is the open-hand Tapa; no punch finisher. Rasteira is the crouch **Punch** opener (cP1, a Low row; the Capoeira hands are for the floor), Bananeira the crouch Kick opener (cK1), so the CPU's first-move choice and the touch auto-height reach both: Rasteira against a blocking foe does not wait for a blocked Bananeira. Two rows carry a new row flag `duck` (Bob and Weave Hook of Boxing and Bananeira, which is a Mid row): **while the fighter is in the startup ticks of a `duck` move, High strikes treat it as crouched** (one more term in `Combat.evaluate`'s crouch test; Mid and Low strikes, grabs and throws still hit; thrown items and hazards take their own head-hit path and ignore `duck`, which is stated here and not changed). **Both stand at an opener position** (Bob and Weave Hook at Boxing `K1`, Bananeira at Capoeira `cK1`), because the CPU only continues a string after a connect and `pickMove` sees only the four openers. Because: dodging low and attacking from there is what the Boxing slip and bob and the Capoeira esquiva are, and one flag on two rows gives both styles a way to win a high-low exchange that does not touch any shared number. **If a P14-01 run puts either style out of band the `duck` flag comes off first, before any frame number, AI weight or animation moves** (one order, also in the balance section). The inverted and rolling poses use the P18-38 plant (`plantFloor` with `planted` true while the move runs) with a faster plant speed while a plant move runs (see the hit check, point 5): the standard 1.5 m/s moves the root only 2.5 cm a tick, 35 cm in the 14 startup ticks of Bananeira, which is not enough for an inversion. Owner can change.
- DECIDE (7) context moves (air, run, counter). Decided: the **counter strike and counter kick of every style are skins** (table below); the **air kick/strike** is its own move for Boxing (Leaping Cross), Capoeira (Flip Kick), Taekwondo (Tornado Kick) and Flying Knee for Kickboxing, Muay Thai and MMA; the other 10 styles, and the running lunge of all 16, keep the one game-level jump attack and lunge (G1, G2): they are reaction mechanics of the game, not techniques of an art. Because: counters are the part of a style a player sees most after a perfect block or sway, they cost a name and an existing animation each, while 16 jump poses would cost 1.2 KB for moves that are rare.
- DECIDE (8) grabs, clinch, throws, holds. Decided: (a) **clinch strike poses**: 8 sets instead of 5 (`default` Karate, Taekwondo; `thai` Muay Thai, Kickboxing, MMA, Krav Maga; `collar` Wrestling, Judo, Sambo, BJJ, Hapkido; `boxer` Wing Chun only; `brawler`; new `boxing`, `capoeira` and `kali`). (b) **throws**: the three grip sets stay the stat source (damage, ticks, flight); a style replaces **the cells (grip and direction) it favours** with its own name and pose; a throw skin is `{ name, pose, carry }` and changes only those three: `carry` is the key into `CARRY_PATHS` and **equals the carry path of the cell it replaces**, because `sampleCarry` falls back to the hip-toss path for an unknown name and would fly the foe the wrong way. The 7 striking styles with no throwing art (Boxing, Karate, Taekwondo, Wing Chun, Kali, Capoeira, Brawler) replace **one cell each**, Kickboxing uses Muay Thai's Clinch Sweep, and the 8 grapplers (Wrestling, Judo, Sambo, BJJ, Hapkido, MMA, Muay Thai, Krav Maga) replace **two cells each**, each grappler with at least one throw of its own (the table below); every other cell (a grip the style does not favour, the mirrored side throws) stays the generic game throw, G3 of the share list, which the share test does not check. Because: a throw is chosen by where the foe stands, not by the player, so every style needs a sensible throw in every cell, and 12 cells times 16 styles is not affordable. (c) **holds**: 6 hold poses, one per hold style: `pin` Wrestling, new `kesa` Judo (lying across the foe's chest), `leg` Sambo, `arm` BJJ, `wrist` Hapkido, new `mount` MMA (sitting astride, one fist up; ground-and-pound). Because: F06 found 14 of 16 styles on three shared sets; the stat rows stay the balance anchor and only the look and name change.
- DECIDE (9) stance. Decided: the guard stays eight numbers per style (`guardHands` ... `guardTuck`), retuned inside art-plausible boxes so that all 120 pairs are at least 0.38 rad apart by pose distance and at least 0.14 on the largest joint (baseline 0.133 / 0.08); the values below come from a search over those boxes (minimum 0.399 / 0.150), no field moves by more than 0.10. Rhythm keys (`bounceHz`, `sway`, `swayHz`, `stride`, `armSwing`) stay as they are: they already separate the styles (Boxing 2.3 Hz and a 0.09 head bob, Muay Thai 0.9 Hz and planted, Capoeira the 0.075 m ginga at 0.55 Hz with the 0.16 arm swing, Wrestling 0.75 stride). The guard of a fighter varies by up to +-8 percent (`MOTION_JITTER`) and a hand bias of +-0.06; the test therefore uses the style row without jitter and demands the margin above.
- DECIDE (10) AI use. Decided: the CPU reads the new moves through what it already reads, and three small things change so that it actually plays them: (a) a style-row field `aiw`, a string `row@anim:weight` list (default weight 1) of the style's signature strikes (3 to 6 per style, about 1 KB in all), read **in both places that pick a move**: `pickMove` as `sc *= weight`, and `thinkChain` as a weighted pick (its uniform random pick becomes one roll against the cumulative weights, the same single `simRng` call, so the random stream keeps its shape); signature weights start at **1.3**, slow ones (startup 12 or more) at 1.0 because `1.25 - startup/30` already cuts them; (b) the `duck` rows get x1.8 in `pickMove` while the foe is in the startup of a High strike (`snap.state` ATTACK, `mv.h` H, ticks to active at least 4); both `duck` rows are openers (DECIDE 6), so `pickMove` sees them, which is how a Boxing or Capoeira CPU "slips" without a new defense; (c) nothing else: no new input, no new AI plan, no change to `decide`'s grab, guard, circle or sidestep scores, and the CPU levels (react, parry, swayAim, mistakes) are untouched. The CPU holds no new move back by difficulty. Measured first (P17-10, before the test is written): how often the Boxing and Capoeira CPUs start a `duck` row against a High wind-up in 12 seeds against three foes; the duck clause of test 10 is kept only if the measure shows at least one use per fighter, otherwise it is dropped from the test and reported (the unit test of the rule, test 8, stays). Frequency target (test 10), measured first like the duck clause: every identity in a style's `aiw` list is started in the P0-06 runs, and openers and second steps make 3 to 45 percent of that style's strike starts, together at least 20 percent; **third-step identities (Liver Shot, Au Batido, Heavy Shin Kick and the like) are only required to start at least once**, because a step three is reached after two connects and may fall under 3 percent whatever its weight; if the measure shows the bands cannot hold for a style, the band is set from the measure and written into the test with the figure, never dropped silently; no string above 35 percent of the style's damage (the P14-01 Strings row). "Own moves as a share of all starts" is not used: four styles (Brawler, Capoeira, Wing Chun, Kali) play only their own identities, so that share is 100 percent by construction.
- DECIDE (11) size gate and order. Decided: P17-10 costs about **20 KB** (table below). It starts only when the headroom is at least **30 KB** after BP-P17-17: 20 KB for P17-10, 2 KB slack, and the 7.6 KB that P18-43 may take, which comes after P17-10. The headroom today is about **4.6 KB** (4,564 bytes; P18-19 grew the file to about 1,195,436 LF bytes), so P17-17 must free **at least about 26 KB** just to meet the gate, and its target is **40 KB of comments** (about 18 percent of the 218 KB that were left at the audit), which leaves about 17 KB for the rest of the program after P17-10 and P18-43. If it frees only 20 KB the headroom is about 24.6 KB: the gate is **missed**, the optimization list below starts, and P17-10 plus P18-43 would not even fit (3 KB over). The gate is measured at the start of P17-10, on the file as it is then. If the headroom is below 30 KB, the owner's rule (2026-11-02) applies in this order and only then: **optimizing must be exhausted before any split**. The coordinator first lists in Appendix D, one line each, what is left and marks each done or not possible: (1) comment text beyond P17-17 (whole-line and trailing); (2) dead code and unused constants (a wired-one-end-hunter pass); (3) data compaction of what exists: up to 14 of the 27 `STRIKE_BUILDERS` as derived specs (the format of this decision; a saving of 1.5 to 3.5 KB, to be measured, only for poses that really are small offsets of another), the `STYLE_STRINGS` block (already counted), repeated pose literals in `THROW_POSES` and `VICTORY_POSES`; (4) the P17-10 design itself in its smallest form (package A only is not allowed, below). Shortening names to save bytes is not allowed (the project rule is full-word readable names). Only when that list is reported exhausted does the coordinator ask the owner for the split into several files. Build order inside P17-10, each with its tests green before the next: (1) engine pieces and the share-list test with the old tables; (2) package A: rows, animations, skins, position tables, signatures, stance, AI data (about 13.8 KB); (3) package B: counters, air moves, grabs, clinch sets, holds (about 3.2 KB); (4) package C: throws (about 3.0 KB). One update of the links test and one re-record of fingerprints at the end (below). Owner can change.

**The 21 new frame rows** (all tier 1 or 2, none strong, each inside the damage-per-tick and advantage bands of the base rows of its category that the existing self-test uses: punches 0.263 to 0.294 per tick, +3 to -1 on hit, -3 to -9 on block; kicks 0.222 to 0.324, 0 to -2, -6 to -11; the numbers are the raw row values, the arithmetic of the old test (`frameAdvantage` on the row: stun - (active - 1 + recovery)); P18-02's frame table is after the base fighter's speed scaling and reads about one tick different. A row is **tier 1 only if its startup is 9 or less and its raw advantage on block is -8 or better** (the existing tier-1 rows run from -3 for the jab to -8 for the front kick and teep; P18-02 reads -2 to -7 after scaling), otherwise tier 2; the tier sets the hit-stop, the counter-hit multiplier and the free-chain rule. The reach column is on today's scale and P17-10 sets the final value from the P17-02 rule, see the hit check.)

  Reading: the slowest new row starts on tick 14 (the P18-02 tier-2 range is 6 to 13, the strong rows 10 to 17, so Bananeira is a readable, escapable step like the strong ones), no row has a damage per tick or an advantage outside the band of its category (probe (b): 21 of 21 inside), 8 rows are tier 1 and 13 tier 2, and the 11 earlier style rows (Chain Punch ... Overhand) are unchanged. The `duck` flag and the `step` of the two lunging punches are the only fields beyond the base shape.

**Skins that change more than a name** (they move the limb or the reach of a shared frame row, so the P17-02 check applies to them with these values): Boxing Liver Shot (`heavy_body_kick`, RL to RA, reach 1.2), Clinch Shove (`heavy_front`, LL to LA, reach 1.1), Counter Uppercut (`counter_kick`, LL to RA, reach 1.2), Leaping Cross (`jump_kick`, RL to RA, `airCross`, reach 1.25); Wrestling Shoulder Charge (`heavy_front`, LL to RA, reach 1.15), Knee Lift (`front_kick`, reach 1.05), Underhook Lift (`heavy_uppercut`, RA, reach 1.1); Judo Push-Out (`heavy_front`, LL to LA, reach 1.2), Lift-Up (`heavy_uppercut`, reach 1.1); BJJ Guard Push (`heavy_front`, LL to LA, reach 1.2); Capoeira Tapa (`jab`, reach 1.2). Every other skin keeps limb, reach and width of its row.

**The vocabulary of each style** (name (`row_id@anim`) and the positions where the string tables use it; `[shared]` = also played by another style, justified in the share list; positions as in DECIDE 2d, `c` = Crouch held; every position is listed, a position that is not named holds `-`). The tokens are the starting tables for P17-10. A probe over them (c) shows: no two tables equal, every row prefix-consistent, every strong row in its allowed position (Wrestling and Judo at step 2), **no High row in a crouch position**, 3 to 11 own moves per style on strike moves alone, 53 new strike animations, 19 shared identities all on the share list, and the highest overlap of any pair Kickboxing with MMA at 0.71 of the smaller style (Boxing with Kickboxing 0.67 and Kickboxing with Muay Thai 0.58 are the real-art shares of the owner's own example). Hapkido's Spinning Heel Kick stays where P14-01 put it, in the **crouch** K K row (as the standing K K step it was a light move and Hapkido won 30 percent, html 4540); its standing K K step is the Foot Sweep.

  Everything else, in every table, is played by one style only. Entries are checked as a subset: a move on the list that nobody plays fails the test (no dead entries), a move two styles play that is on no entry fails it (no silent sharing).

**Context moves, grabs, throws and holds per style** (counters are skins of `counter_strike` and `counter_kick` and keep their frame row: 4 / 3 / 8 stagger and 5 / 3 / 10 knockdown, perfect-block counters only; names are what the Move list and Training show; a signature line names only identities that one style plays: its own strikes, counters, throws, holds and grab; the clinch knee and elbow of the shared clinch sets are in the Move list but not in a signature line):
