  New throw poses (one derived line each, `THROW_POSES[name] = derive(base, spec)`, plus the `carry` key): Clinch Shove-Off, Foot-Sweep Throw, Hook Trip, Trap Trip, Arm-Drag Takedown, Rasteira Takedown, Bear-Hug Slam (the seven strikers), Ura-Nage, Leg Hook Throw, Wrist-Lock Throw, Cage Takedown, Clinch Sweep, Knee-Lift Dump, Wrist Takedown, Collar Takedown, Fireman's Carry, Single-Leg Takedown (17). A throw skin changes the name, the pose and the carry key only; `dmg`, ticks, flight and `escapeUntil` stay those of the grip row (the existing test of ranges for style throws applies to all 17, and a new check flies each one through its carry path with the 0.5 m body gap).

**Stance retune** (probe (a): the eight guard fields only; old to new; minimum pose distance 0.399, largest-joint minimum 0.150, from 0.133 and 0.08): Boxing twist -0.08 to -0.10, crouch 0.14 to 0.15; Kickboxing hands 0.14 to 0.06, tight 0.06 to 0.10, lean -0.02 to -0.03, crouch 0.02 to -0.03, width 0 to 0.01, split 0.04 to 0.11; Karate hands -0.06 to -0.16, tight -0.12 to -0.05, crouch 0.10 to 0.11; Wrestling hands -0.22 to -0.17, tight -0.55 to -0.56, split 0.02 to 0.05; Judo width -0.02 to -0.04; Brawler hands -0.10 to -0.13; Capoeira hands -0.18 to -0.10, twist 0.10 to 0, lean 0.20 to 0.22, crouch 0.22 to 0.23; Wing Chun tight 0.30 to 0.31, split 0 to -0.02; Muay Thai hands 0.30 to 0.32; Taekwondo hands -0.28 to -0.29, tight -0.30 to -0.27, width 0.08 to 0.12; Sambo unchanged; BJJ hands -0.20 to -0.30, tight -0.35 to -0.39, twist 0.10 to 0.13, lean 0.12 to 0.10, crouch 0.26 to 0.33, split -0.04 to -0.08; Hapkido hands 0 to 0.01, twist -0.10 to -0.09, crouch 0.10 to 0.06, width 0.04 to 0.08, split 0.08 to 0.06; Kali hands 0.04 to 0.10, twist -0.14 to -0.20, crouch 0.12 to 0.21, width 0 to -0.02, split 0.08 to 0.13; Krav Maga hands 0.10 to 0.20, tight 0 to -0.05, lean 0.10 to 0.11, crouch 0.06 to 0.03, split 0 to -0.04; MMA hands 0.06 to 0.03, tight -0.02 to 0.06, twist -0.02 to 0.04, lean 0.08 to 0.10, crouch 0.16 to 0.19, width 0.04 to 0.08, split 0.06 to 0.04. These are starting values: P17-10 verifies them with the idle pose (the test of F07) and nudges inside the same boxes if the toned-down strike poses of P17-02 move a guard. The guard is the pose every strike starts from, so the retune changes every strike's look slightly and no number of any fight.

**Motion character** (what the player should read at a glance; the words are the brief for the animation specs): Boxing bladed, high double guard, chin tucked, bouncing, head moves (slip and bob); Kickboxing long upright guard, lead-leg bounce, kicks that snap back; Karate long low front stance, very still, chambered snap kicks and hip-driven reverse punches; Wrestling low and wide, hands forward hunting ties, short heavy steps; Judo upright and relaxed, hands at the collar, smooth weight shifts; Brawler loose and square, rolling shoulders, big slow loops; Capoeira the ginga rock with one arm guarding, wide low whipping kicks, hands to the floor and body upside down for the acrobatics; Wing Chun narrow, elbows in, centre-line chain punches and short kicks; Muay Thai planted, high guard, long shin kicks with the whole body behind them, clinch; Taekwondo light and bouncing, hands low, head-height snap kicks, spins and jumps; Sambo square jacket stance, boots and hooks; BJJ crouched low and wide, a seated Guard Kick, sweeps; Hapkido circling, open palms, low kicks and heel spins; Kali weaving angles, a live hand, thrusts and wide Redonda swings; Krav Maga forward-leaning, defend-and-strike in one, kicks to the low line; MMA between ranges: punches, a Superman Punch, oblique kicks, takedowns.

**AI use per style** (the knobs of DECIDE 10; the signature moves are the `aiw` list, weight 1.3 unless noted):

| Style | Moves the CPU favours, and when |
|---|---|
| Boxing | Bob and Weave Hook is the K opener and x1.8 while a High strike winds up (the bob), otherwise 1.3; Body Hook as the second step; Liver Shot as the K K K finisher |
| Kickboxing | Switch Kick (PK2) and Spinning Backfist (PKP3) from mid range; Teep at the edge of range |
| Karate | Step-In Punch / Lunge Punch from range 1.6 or more (their `step` closes it), Mae-geri and Side Thrust Kick as range keepers; Karate CPUs stand still between bursts (existing `circle` weights) |
| Wrestling | Snap Down to hold the foe at tie range, then the grab (existing `st.grab`); Underhook Smash / Lift and Shoulder Charge as the short-string finishers |
| Judo | Collar Pull and Foot Sweep, then a grab; Push-Out and Lift-Up as the second step: Foot Sweep x1.3 against a foe that blocks High |
| Street Brawler | Haymaker and Head Butt at close range; Low Stomp (the only Low Brawler row) on a downed foe, since the Mid Stomp cannot hit one; slow and loud (the wind-up x2) |
| Capoeira | Armada, Au Batido from 1.4 to 2.0 m (weight 1.0, they are slow); Bananeira is the crouch opener and x1.8 against a High wind-up; Rasteira (the crouch Punch opener, so the CPU and the touch auto-height reach it) against a blocking foe; the acrobatic rows are at most 30 percent of its strike starts |
| Wing Chun | Chain Punch and Trap Strike chains at close range (the chain press rate of thinkChain, now weighted) |
| Muay Thai | Teep to hold range, Shin Round Kick and Elbow in; Heavy Shin Kick as the K K K finisher |
| Taekwondo | Dollyo Head Kick from range, Jump Round Kick and Spinning Back Kick in strings, Axe Kick as the K K K step; Tornado Kick as its air attack |
| Sambo | Boot Kick and Leg Pick, then a grab: Boot Push when the foe blocks |
| BJJ | Guard Kick and Snap Down, then the grab or the hold on a downed foe (existing `AI_SUBMIT`); Scissor Sweep as the crouch finisher |
| Hapkido | Palm Strike and Foot Sweep at close range, Spinning Heel Kick from the crouch K K row, Reaping Foot Sweep against a standing foe |
| Kali | Thrust and Angle 1 / 2 chains, Redonda as the finisher; Long Thrust from range |
| Krav Maga | Parry Strike then Hammer Fist in one chain; Groin Kick as the opener; Stomp Kick as the finisher |
| MMA | Overhand and Superman Punch from range (the lunging punches), Oblique Kick to the line, then a takedown (existing grab scores) |

**How the new moves must pass P17-02's hit check** (the check P17-02 builds: for every move of every style against a standing foe at the move's range, the limb tip at the first active tick lies inside the hit volume):
  1. A row's `limb` is the limb that really extends in its animation (the check picks the key point from it: hands 9 to 14 for LA and RA, feet 15 and 18 for LL and RL); a skin that moves a row to the other limb gives its own `reach` (list above).
  2. The check is made on the **contact pose** (`ext` = 1 at tick `mStartup + 1`). The recovery turn of `STRIKE_SPIN` (Armada) and `STRIKE_ROLL` (Au Batido) and the un-inverting of Bananeira are not checked; the pose at contact is, with the yaw 1.4 and the roll at 3.14 of Au Batido and the pitch 3.0 of Bananeira put there on purpose so that the foot passes the line to the foe at that tick.
  3. A new row's `reach` is not written by hand: it is set from the probe, so that the front of the volume (`reach` x the Reach stat, plus the foe's radius 0.34) equals the tip's forward distance **measured from the fighter's centre at the contact tick**, within the slack P17-02 allows, rounded to 0.05. `step` is **not** added: it moves the fighter's centre during the startup (`updateAttack`) and `Combat.evaluate` measures from that moved centre, so adding it would double count it (Superman Punch and Step-In Punch would whiff by 0.8 and 0.5 m). `step` appears only in the AI's range check (`inRange`), where it widens the distance from which the CPU starts the move. The values in the table above are today's scale and are only the first guess. If P17-02 lowers `reach` and `radius` across the table (its choice 2), the new rows are scaled by the same factor and the bands above (reach at most the Push Kick) are applied after it.
  4. Height: the tip at contact lies in the height class of the row (F03): High 1.1 to 1.7 m, Mid 0.7 to 1.3 m, Low up to 0.5 m; Armada and Axe Kick are High and must put the foot there, Bananeira is Mid. Spin and roll moves must also pass the check from both sides of the body (the fighter can be on the left or right of the foe's line); the test runs the move at a lateral offset of 0.3 m too. The animation base follows the row's height (DECIDE 2c).
  5. The skeleton must stay put together: the lowest foot or hand over every tick of every move never below the floor by more than 0.03 m. The plant of an inverted pose is **not** assumed: `PLANT_SPEED` 1.5 m/s moves the root 2.5 cm a tick, so Bananeira (14 startup ticks) could close 35 cm only. P17-10 measures the error of the root height of Bananeira and Au Batido tick by tick first; while a `plant` move runs the plant speed is raised to a constant `PLANT_SPEED_MOVE` (start 6 m/s, 10 cm a tick, the least that holds the rule below), and the rule is: at no tick below the floor by more than 0.03 m, at the contact tick of Bananeira the lowest part (a hand) within 0.05 m of the floor, and at most 0.35 m above it while Au Batido is in the air (the cartwheel may leave the floor). No joint turns further than the P17-02 table allows for its class.
  6. P17-10 runs after P17-02. If a P17-10 value fails the check, the animation spec is changed (lean, step, `fwd`), not the check.

**Self-tests P17-10 must add** (section "Styles (P17-10)"; none may skip, weaken or delete an older test; the old `STYLE_MOVE_OWNERS` test is replaced by tests 1 to 3, because the single-owner rule it checks becomes the share list):

  1. `Styles (P17-10): no two styles play a move outside the share list` - build the identity map `row_id@anim -> styles` from the string tables, counters, air moves, grabs, clinch sets, holds and the throw cells each style favours (table above); every identity played by two or more styles must lie inside one entry of `SHARE_LIST` (subset of its style set); every entry must be played by at least two of its styles.
  2. `Styles (P17-10): every style has moves of its own` - each style at least 3 strike identities nobody else plays and at least 5 own identities counting counters, air move, throw, hold and clinch set; Capoeira at least 4 acrobatic identities (`armada`, `cartwheel`, `handstand`, `airFlip`); Boxing no identity with a leg limb anywhere (string tables, counters, air move, clinch strikes, throw, hold) except the G4 fallback while armed or carrying; every signature entry is an identity only that style plays.
  3. `Styles (P17-10): string tables` - no two tables equal; every row prefix-consistent; `-` only in Wrestling and Judo at 3-step positions (and then every longer row with that prefix is absent too); the 8 strong rows only in the P18-02 positions (Wrestling and Judo: step 2), each style playing 3 to 7 of them; **crouch positions hold only rows of height Mid or Low (by the row, not the skin)**; the count of strong rows (tier 3, not a context move) stays 8; every signature entry reachable from the tables; the pair overlap (shared identities over the smaller style's count) at most 0.75 (design: 0.71).
  4. `Styles (P17-10): skins change no number` - every skinned move equals its frame row in every field except name, anim, limb, reach, width and cat; ids unchanged; the cache is built once per style and row (no allocation in a tick); the animation base of a skin has the height of its row.
  5. `Styles (P17-10): frame data of the new rows` - the damage-per-tick and advantage bands of the old style-move test over all 21 rows (restated with the new row set), tier 1 only with startup 9 or less and raw block -8 or better, reach at most the Push Kick, startup at most 14, the `duck` rows two only; **the P18-02 link classes (always true, true if prompt, never true) recomputed for every row of all 16 string tables (about 230 distinct rows, not only the 21 new ones) and asserted**; the P18-03 links test, which pins the 42 rows of the old tables, is updated in the same commit.
  6. `Styles (P17-10): the new moves pass the hit check` - the P17-02 check over the 21 rows, the skins with a new limb or reach, the counters and the air moves, for all 16 bodies of a style's preset, centre and 0.3 m lateral offset (limb tip at contact, height class, floor rule of point 5, the joint limits of P17-02).
  7. `Styles (P17-10): guard poses` - all 120 pairs at least 0.38 rad by pose distance and 0.14 on the largest joint, on the style rows without jitter; the same pairs with the idle probe of F07 (at least 0.25 on the largest joint including motion).
  8. `Styles (P17-10): the duck rows` - a High strike thrown at a fighter in the startup of Bob and Weave Hook or Bananeira whiffs, hits on the first active tick, a Mid and a Low strike hit during the startup, a grab connects, a thrown item still hits the head; Block and Sway fighters behave as before.
  9. `Styles (P17-10): throws, holds and clinch` - each hold style plays its own hold pose at least 0.3 rad from every other; every style plays its own throw in each cell of the throw table above and the generic one elsewhere, a throw skin changes only name, pose and carry key (stats and ticks equal the cell it replaces), each of the 17 new throws is flown through its carry path, sits in the ranges of the existing ones (damage, ticks, `escapeUntil`, flight) and keeps the 0.5 m body gap; the 8 clinch pose sets differ pairwise by 0.3 rad at the strike.
  10. `Styles (P17-10): the CPU plays its signature moves` - P0-06 runs (`SimRunner.run`, 12 seeds a style against three foes, Hard): every identity of a style's `aiw` list is started; openers and second steps make 3 to 45 percent of its strike starts, together at least 20 percent (third-step identities only need to start once; the bands follow the first measurement, DECIDE 10); no string above 35 percent of the damage; the duck clause (Boxing and Capoeira CPUs use a `duck` row at least once against a High wind-up) only if the first measurement of DECIDE 10 supports it.
  11. `Styles (P17-10): Move list, Training and text` - the Move list and the Training line show each style's signature line from the table; the P17-05 scan finds no design-note wording in the new names or descriptions; Boxing's Move list names the K slot "Body punches".
  12. `Styles (P17-10): armed and carrying` - a Boxing fighter with an item or a crate in the arms plays the G4 kick rows; every other style keeps its own Kick steps; the weapon tables (`weaponTable`) still replace only the Punch steps.
  13. `Styles (P17-10): determinism` - two runs with one seed give identical event streams; no `Math.random`, no setting read; the skin cache gives the same objects; `thinkChain` draws exactly one random number per pick as before.
  14. `Styles (P17-10): inverted and rolling poses` - Bananeira and Au Batido tick by tick: lowest part never more than 0.03 m below the floor, at most 0.05 m above it at the contact tick of Bananeira, at most 0.35 m above it for Au Batido, the plant never jumps (a step of the root of at most `PLANT_SPEED_MOVE` a tick).
  The two old tests that name moves by owner (`Styles: the new moves have frame data ...`, `... each style with attacks of its own uses them in AI matches`) are rewritten onto tests 5 and 10, not removed.

**Balance targets P17-10 must keep and how it proves it** (the P14-01 table above; every row below is re-run, none loosened, outliers fixed by data in this order: the `duck` flag first (a rule), then a row's damage by 1, a startup or recovery by 1, an AI weight, and never a special case per matchup):
  - Style pairs, all 136 (mirrors included): 40 to 60 percent at Hard and Expert, 35 to 65 at Easy and Normal, **200 per pair at all four difficulties** (136 x 200 x 4 = 108,800 matches) with `BREAKPOINT.simulate`, `evenDamage`, KAI's body and attributes with the style swapped, exactly as P14-01 recorded it. It is a long block of heavy-slot time: it is run in fixed seed ranges (1 to 200 per pair) over several sessions through `heavy.mjs`. A first pass of 60 per pair at Hard may be used only to find outliers early and to order the work; no row is accepted on it and the recorded result is the full run. (The owner can lower n for the screen only by changing this line.)
  - Presets: starters, unlocks and rivals 42 to 58 percent, bosses 50 to 62, each against all 31 others at Hard, 20 per pair. Block vs Sway 45 to 55 percent at every difficulty, 600; P18-03/04 land first and have re-tuned the Sway rows, so this is measured on their numbers.
  - Strings: no string above 35 percent of a fighter's damage. Time to KO, 1v1: median round 20 to 40 s, time-outs at most 15 percent, under 2 percent of rounds end before 8 s, median match 50 to 110 s. Momentum boost 0.5 to 2 per fighter per round, every pair inside its band.
  - Items: the armed win rate 55 to 70 percent per weapon, none above 72, and the clear rate of brawls on and off within 15 points; the weapon tables take their Kick steps from the style, so Boxing's G4 fallback and every style's new Kick steps are inside this run (50 per level, 200 per weapon).
  - Body options, weight classes, uneven teams, hazards, brawl, survival, Arcade length, difficulty ladders (P14-05) and grades: re-run the rows that read a style (all of them use KAI or the roster); each must stay in its band. The P0-06 rows of the P18-33 table (Press, Mash, AI at Easy to Expert, 1v1 to 1v6 and waves) are repeated once at the end.
  - Links and fingerprints: the new tables change which step follows which (the strong rows moved, the Wrestling and Judo shape is kept but their steps differ), so the P18-02 link classes of **all** rows are recomputed (test 5), and every pinned hash that includes a fight changes, because the string tables change which move ids are played. Process, as P18-02 laid out for the Sway rule, after P18-03/04 and P18-01 have landed and re-recorded their own: (1) land the engine pieces with the old tables and show every recorded hash and test unchanged; (2) land the new tables behind one local switch that restores `STYLE_STRINGS` and the old skins, run with the switch off (all hashes as recorded) and on (list which fail), and show for each failing one that its first differing event is one of: a different move id, or a hit or whiff that a skin's `reach` or `width` changed (a skin that moves the volume changes the first contact before it changes anything else), or a spacing choice that follows from a new `step` or `reach` in the AI's range check; (3) only then update the links test and re-record the hashes in a separate commit that names each test and says "style moves (P17-09)". A hash of a run with no strike in it (menu, scene, hazards-only) failing is a bug, not a re-record.
  - Performance: `BREAKPOINT.benchmark` (P0-04 method) must not drop: the skin cache and the ANIM_SPECS builders are built at load or once per style, nothing allocates per tick; the idle pose cost is unchanged.

**What P17-10 costs in bytes** (LF bytes of `breakpoint.html`, BOM counted; estimates from the lengths of the existing equivalents: a `defAttack` row 235 bytes, a `STRIKE_BUILDERS` line 330, a `STYLE_TECH` row 330, a `THROW_POSES` entry 230; tests are in `breakpoint-tests.js` and not counted, about 9 KB there):

| Piece | Count | Bytes each | Total |
|---|---|---|---|
| Engine: skin cache and `moveFor`, `variant`, `ANIM_SPECS` builder with height bases, the five spec keys, `STRIKE_ROLL`, `PLANT_SPEED_MOVE`, `duck` term, `aiw` read in `pickMove` and `thinkChain`, position parser, clinch-set plumbing | - | - | 2.1 KB |
| New frame rows (`variant` form, with name, tier and flags) | 21 | 140 | 2.9 KB |
| Strike animations (derived specs) | 53 | 72 | 3.8 KB |
| Context animations (`slipCross`, `airCross`, `sprawl`, `airFlip`, `tornado`, `airKnee`, a boxing body shot) | 7 | 75 | 0.5 KB |
| Skins (style, row, anim, name) | about 120 | 26 | 3.1 KB |
| Counter, air and grab skins | about 50 | 27 | 1.3 KB |
| Position tables (16 x 18 tokens), minus the old `STYLE_STRINGS` block | 16 | 165 | +2.6 KB - 2.4 KB = 0.2 KB |
| Signature lines, descriptions, `GROUND_NOTES` for Judo and MMA | 16 | 45 | 0.7 KB |
| `aiw` data (the signature strikes of 16 styles, `row@anim:weight`) | 16 | 60 | 1.0 KB |
| Throws: 17 poses with a carry key, plus about 26 name entries | 17 + 26 | 119 + 40 | 3.0 KB |
| Holds `kesa`, `mount` and three clinch sets (`boxing`, `capoeira`, `kali`) | 5 | 280 | 1.4 KB |
| Stance retune | - | - | 0 (numbers change) |
| **Total** | | | **about 20 KB** (17 to 23; package A about 13.8, B about 3.2, C about 3.0) |

  What the budget then looks like: about 4.6 KB left now (after P18-19); the gate is 30 KB free after P17-17 (DECIDE 11), so P17-17 must free at least about 26 KB; with its target of 40 KB there are about 44.6 KB before P17-10, P17-10 takes about 20, P18-43 up to 7.6, leaving about 17 KB for P18-03/04 follow-ups, P18-05, P18-07, P18-09, P18-21, P18-24, P18-13, P18-29, P18-31 and online play. If P17-17 frees only about 20 KB (24.6 KB headroom) the gate is missed and P17-10 plus P18-43 would be about 3 KB over the limit, which is the F14 wall; the optimization list of DECIDE 11 starts then, and the owner's split comes after it. P17-10 may not shrink by dropping package B or C and leaving the share list with holes: either all three packages or the owner's call.

**Forks, with the pick**

| Fork | Options | Pick and why |
|---|---|---|
| What "different" means | A new frame row per move (about 140 bytes a row, new balance numbers); B skin = look and name over a shared row; C both | C: B for the identities that play like their row (cheap, balance stays anchored), A only for the 21 where the mechanic differs (a step, a duck, a spin, a sweep, a different height) |
| Kick-less Boxing | A body punches on the K press, air and clinch (pick); B keep kicks | A: the owner's example; the fallback while armed is G4 |
| Strong moves | A nine or more own strong rows; B the 8 rows skinned per style | B: the 8 rows carry every guard-break number of P18-02 and P14-01 |
| Grapplers' strikes and string shape | A invent punches and 3-step strings; B grip moves, two steps for Wrestling and Judo | B: the real arts have no strikes; the weight is in throws and holds; P14-01 balanced the short string |
| Crouch positions | A any row; B Mid and Low rows only | B: crouch is the low line on touch (`touchAttackHeight`) and a High strike whiffs on a crouched foe |
| Counters and air moves | A 16 skins of everything; B counters for all, air for the 6 that have one, lunge for none | B: a counter is seen after every perfect, a jump rarely |
| Stance metric | A largest single joint at 0.25 for all pairs (not reachable inside art-plausible boxes, best 0.20); B pose distance at 0.38 plus largest joint 0.14 | B: the eight guard fields cannot give 0.25 on one joint for 120 pairs without absurd poses; the distance reads as "looks different" |
| Where the new code goes | A per-style code; B data (skins, specs, positions) | B: the budget |
| Early stop | A build part and share the rest | no: the share test is exact and half a roster fails it; either all three packages or the owner's call on the split, after the optimization list (DECIDE 11) |

- Open (for the owner; each one is decided above and the owner can change it): (1) Boxing has no leg moves; the Kick button plays body punches, in the air and in the clinch too (DECIDE 4); the button caption is unchanged, which a phone check must confirm. (2) The grapplers' P and K slots are grip moves, and Wrestling and Judo keep two-step strings (DECIDE 5). (3) The size gate: P17-10 needs about 20 KB and starts only with 30 KB free after P17-17 (P17-17 must free at least about 26 KB from the 4.6 KB left after P18-19); below that the optimization list of DECIDE 11 comes first and the split into several files (owner rule 2026-11-02) only after it is reported exhausted, not a smaller roster. (4) `duck` on two opener rows (Bob and Weave Hook, Bananeira) is the one new combat rule; it comes off first if a style pair leaves its band. (5) For the P16-03 device list (the coordinator adds them): Boxing's K slot on a phone; Capoeira's Au Batido and Bananeira on a phone at 640 x 360 (the camera and the floor line); the 8 clinch sets and the 6 holds side by side.
- Checks: probes only. (a) The guard of every style rebuilt from `GUARD_ORTHO` and `applyStyleGuard` and compared in all 120 pairs before and after the retune (a seeded search inside the art boxes; pose distance 0.133 and largest joint 0.08 before, 0.399 and 0.150 after). (b) The 21 rows against the bands computed from the 8 base rows of the old style-move test: 21 of 21 inside. (c) The 16 position tables expanded to identities: no two tables equal, prefix-consistent, no High row in a crouch position, strong rows only in their positions, own strike identities per style 3 (MMA) to 11 (Capoeira), 19 shared identities all on the share list, highest pair overlap 0.71; the number of new strike animations is 53. No game file, test file, browser or server was touched; `breakpoint.html` was not touched (1,189,162 LF bytes at the base commit 2895a01; main has since grown to about 1,195,436 with P18-19), so no self-test run was needed.

### P18-05 Difficulty in every mode with a CPU decided (2026-11-06)

- Owner (2026-11-01): "difficulty selection in all modes, before starting"; one choice before the game starts, not only at the end of Character Select in Arcade. Decided by the task's recommendation (BR-001); the owner can change each line.
- The step and the name: one row named "Difficulty" (Easy, Normal, Hard, Expert), the same row control everywhere (`optRow`: a tap or Enter steps it, left / right step it, a gamepad moves the focus to it and steps it), on the first screen of the mode, before the fight. Arcade and Survival: their first screen is the fighter screen, so the row sits above the cards (below 32 cards it would not be found); Free Fight: row 4 of the first screen (P18-19, unchanged); Tournament: the join screen, as a row like the others (it was a button with a longer caption). Training has no CPU level to choose (the dummy), so it has no row; Free Fight dims the row when the lineup has no CPU, as before. "Arcade difficulty", the button "Difficulty: ..." and the words "CPU level" are gone from the screens.
- Saved per mode in P18-19's `breakpoint.v1.setups`: `setups.freeFight.difficulty` (exists), `setups.arcade.difficulty`, `setups.survival.difficulty`, `setups.tournament.difficulty` (what P18-20 + P18-37 call P18-05's `difficulty`); written when the row changes (Free Fight: when its screen is left, as before), read at start-up (`Game.loadLevels`); a missing or damaged value is Easy. Old saves: Free Fight keeps its level; Arcade, Survival and Tournament never saved one and start at Easy, as a new player did (Tournament and Free Fight shared one field before, now each has its own); a saved Arcade run and a saved bracket keep the level they were made at.
- Arcade now offers Expert too (the same four values everywhere): every stage plays at the chosen level as before; ZERO as the final boss has `bossAttrs` for Easy, Normal and Hard only, so at Expert he uses the Hard attributes and the Expert AI is the extra step (P18-21 measures the rate).
- Survival: the chosen Difficulty shifts today's ramp as P18-20 + P18-37 decided: rung of the fight (Easy under 2 wins, Normal under 4, Hard under 7, Expert from 7) plus 0 / 1 / 2 / 3 for Easy / Normal / Hard / Expert, capped at Expert; Easy is today's ramp exactly. Built in this task (a few bytes). Everything else of Survival (the type row, brawl groups, allies, bigger first groups, records apart) stays P18-21's.
- Checks: self-tests "Difficulty everywhere (P18-05)".

### P18-40 critical issue sweep (2026-11-05)

- Result: 1 Critical and 1 High found and fixed (the first review of this sweep found them; the first pass of the sweep had listed both as "seen, not critical"). Critical = a mode or choice that cannot be used, a soft lock (no way forward or back), lost progress, a crash. The sweep covered the main menu, the mode list and the eight modes (Arcade, Survival, Training, Free Fight, Versus, Brawl, Co-op Brawl, Tournament with Continue tournament), their fighter, rules, Players and teams, arena, join and bracket screens, fights (win, loss, clock, Quit from pause), results, Rematch, Change fighters, Continue and Main menu, and Controls, Move list, Settings, Unlocks, About and the creator (with its Fight preview) on keyboard 1, keyboard 2, gamepad and touch / mouse.

| ID | Sev | Area | Location | Reproduction | Cause and fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E01 | Critical | Lost progress | `UI.confirmFighter` (Arcade), `UI.confirmTournamentPick` | Play, Enter (Arcade is the default button), Confirm with a saved Arcade run at stage 5: the run is replaced at once. The same with the last fighter confirm of a new Tournament over a saved bracket. No warning, no undo. | `startArcade` and `playTournament` write the new save as soon as they start. Fix: with a save present the confirm opens a new screen `replace` ("Replace your saved run: NAME, stage 5 of 7?" / "Replace your saved tournament: 8 fighters, next: Semi-final?") made of the game's ordinary panel and menu buttons: Keep (the default, it is Back, so Esc, B and a tap on Back all keep) returns to the fighter screen with the save and the pick as they were, Replace starts the new run. The screen has the double tap guard of Pause and Results. | [x] fixed, self-tests added |
| E02 | High | Gamepad | `UI.update` (join screen branch), `Inputs.lobbyPad`, `UI.onLobby` | Gamepad only on the Tournament or Co-op join screen: the Entrants row and the CPU level button cannot be reached or changed (the pad reads only the lobby there). | Fix: `Inputs.lobbyPad` also reports up / down (focus moves) from any gamepad, joined or not, left / right from a gamepad that has not joined (`adjust`; a joined one's `cycle` is the same press) and A on a joined one (`press`). `UI.onLobby`: up / down move the focus; left / right from a gamepad (events marked `pad`) change the Entrants row in focus (and the CPU level in a Tournament), else choose a joined pad's fighter as before; a key reaches the page twice (the lobby and `UI.onKey`, which already steps the row), so key events never change the row here (a review found Entrants stepping twice and staying put after a keyboard joined; a self-test now checks one step per press for both keyboards); A changes the row in focus once the focus was moved to it. Co-op keeps left / right for the joined pad's fighter, join, leave and Start are as before, and Tournament seat cards still do not cycle (P18-41). The hint text says so. | [x] fixed, self-test added |

- Method: 21 self-tests in the new section "Mode flows (P18-40)" of `breakpoint-tests.js`. They press only what the screen on show offers (the `data-act` of its enabled buttons: what a tap, Enter and a pad's A all press), through `UI.act`, `UI.onClick`, `UI.onKey` (Esc, arrows, Enter) and the lobby events of fake gamepads. The page is stood in for: the real `Game.go` with `GS_TRANSITIONS`, then `UI.onGameState` on stand-in elements; the screen's focusable elements are listed as `UI.navItems` lists them and `document.activeElement` follows the focus, so arrow keys and a pad's stick move it and Enter or A clicks it. A reach check fails when the keys or the pad (a pad's stick down, once per element) never land on an enabled element of a screen. Fights are played to their end by the match itself (a real knock-out or the clock, then the game loop's VICTORY to RESULTS step, Punch skipping the pose); every screen drawn is checked for a way out and for no enabled button without `data-nav`. At the end of a test on a page the game state goes to the menu the real way, so the music and the touch buttons follow the menu, and the frame loop numbers (`Game.last`, `acc`, `frameAvg`, the `Diag` frame stats) and `Game.tournamentResume` are put back.
- Tests: one flow per mode (Arcade, Arcade with all scenes to the clear and the ending, Survival, Training, Free Fight with and without a lineup, Versus, Brawl, Co-op Brawl with a keyboard and a pad, Tournament on touch to the title, Tournament left and continued, Tournament for two devices, two people in one bout); the two replace questions (Keep, Back, Esc, the pad's B, a tap straight after the question, Replace, for an Arcade run and for a bracket of one and of two); the join screens by the arrow keys and a pad (joined or not); lineups that cannot start; three and four people on pads; Back and Esc from every screen of every mode; the side screens and the creator; and seeded random walks (presses, taps and keys on option rows, Esc, joins, pauses, fights; custom fighter, saved run and bracket, story, ring-out, items and hazards settings on some seeds). After every press a walk checks: the game state and the screen agree, no state change was refused, `Diag.errors` is unchanged, a joined person is read from a device in play, the join screen has co-op input and the lobby on, and the saved progress (records, unlocks) equals the memory copy and never falls. Now and then and at the end, Back / Quit / Main menu alone must lead to the main menu.
- On the old game (HEAD) the Arcade and Tournament replace tests and the join screen test fail ("Confirm did not ask", "the confirm did not ask", "right on a gamepad that has not joined did not change Entrants"), and so does the Co-op test through its reach check ("the gamepad never reaches back"); all four pass now. Four deliberate breaks of the game made earlier (Back from the arena, the Tournament confirm, Back from the bracket, Quit in the pause menu) were each caught by 3 to 11 tests.
- Evidence: 592 walks of 100 steps passed before the fixes (seeds 1-150, 1000-1011, 2000-2199, 3000-3039, 4000-4039, 5000-5149); after them 60 more (seeds 6000-6059) with the new checks and 30 (seeds 7000-7029) with the final form of the join-screen code. The suite keeps 5 walks (seeds 1-5) because a walk costs 2 to 3 s.
- Two false alarms of the harness were found and fixed in the tests, not in the game: a walk that pressed Skip on a scene with a question never answered it (Skip still asks the answer, and the answer buttons are on screen), and a wave scene that pauses a fight (Arcade stage 3) must be played through before the match goes on.
- Seen, not critical (not fixed):
  - Co-op Brawl cannot be started with touch or mouse alone: a device has to join with Punch or A first, and "Choose arena" stays off until one has. By design (the mode list says "keyboards and gamepads", the join screen says "Touch controls are not used in co-op"); Brawl with one player covers a solo crowd fight on touch. Versus and a lineup with two or more people likewise need a second keyboard layout or a gamepad.
  - A saved Tournament bracket with two or more people waits on the join screen until as many devices have joined; with fewer devices Back is the only way on, and the bracket stays on the mode list until a new Tournament is drawn over it (which now asks first).
  - Back from a fighter screen to the mode list leaves the last `Game.run` set (Quit to main menu clears it); every mode starts its own run, so nothing reads the old one.
  - A Versus lineup that cannot start (the second slot set to CPU on Players and teams) is not stopped on the Versus rules screen: "Choose arena" is on, the arena screen's "Start fight" says why (toast) and stays, and Back leads to the lineup to fix it.
- Checks: headless run with `BP_FILTER="Mode flows"`: 21 / 21; full headless run through `heavy.mjs`: 501 / 518, exactly the 17 known failures (16 "needs the main menu", 1 "blank faces"), listeners 25 / 25 before and after; `breakpoint.html` is 1,173,238 bytes (LF, BOM counted) of 1,200,000. Still open: the same tests in Chrome (the stand-in page elements are used there too; on a page each test ends by taking the game state to the menu the real way), and the owner's device checks, which now include the replace question and a gamepad on the Tournament join screen.

Nothing else was touched: no code, data, CSS or player text.

### P18-38 ground submissions (2026-11-05)

- Owner, 2026-11-03: "On a ground armbar i float above the ground." Measured with the new self-test (lowest drawn part of each fighter above the floor, from the drawn matrices): armbar holder (Brazilian Jiu-Jitsu, MMA) up to +53 cm in the air, the kneeling pin (Wrestling, Judo) down to -14 cm and the Hapkido wrist lock down to -12 cm into the floor, the Sambo leg lock +1 cm, the held fighter +2 to +5 cm face up; a knocked-down or knocked-out fighter (unheld) lies 2 to 5 cm above the floor face up and 7 to 16 cm into it face down.
- Cause: `rootHeight()` puts the root where the legs of a STANDING body reach the floor, and only the `lie` blend (the knockdown pose) overrides it. The four `HOLD_POSES` (pin, leg, arm, wrist) kneel, sit or lie but set no `lie`, so the body hung at the standing height of its leg angles: the armbar lay back with its legs up and the root stayed 50 cm high. `LIE_HEIGHT` (0.17) is one value for every build, and a face-down body is deeper than a face-up one, so no single value fits.
- Fix 1 (`Fighter.animate`, `plantFloor`, `lowestPart`, `skeleton`, `part`, `probeLow`): on every tick a fighter in a hold (the holder in SUBMIT) or lying down (DOWN held or not, KO, and the GETUP out of them) is walked once without drawing (`part`, `pieces` and the held item record the lowest height of every box, ball and cylinder instead of queuing it, `PROBE_LOW`), and `plantShift` lifts or lowers the drawing so the lowest drawn part is on the floor whatever the pose, body and build. The lowest part moves to the floor from where the last tick drew it, and back to the pose's own lowest `PLANT_RELEASE_TICKS` (20) ticks after the pose ends, at most `PLANT_SPEED` (1.5 m/s, 2.5 cm a tick), so it neither sinks nor jumps while the pose blends (review finding: an ease of the shift at a fixed rate let the half-blended pose sink 16 to 18 cm into the floor and jump 13 cm in one tick at the start and the end of a hold; the first version of this fix). Everybody else is not measured, so nothing else pays for the extra pass. `frameScale` returns a reused array (the measuring pass calls it too). Drawing only: the simulation, hit boxes, timing and key points used by hits are unchanged (the key points feed only effects and scene placement).
- Fix 2 (`HOLD_POSES`): the armbar lies flat on the back (`bodyPitch` -1.5, `lie` 1, head up) with legs and arms keeping their old directions in the room (hips 1.5 and 1.3 became 0.9 and 0.7, shoulders 1.2 became 0.6, because the body lies 0.6 further back); the leg lock sits back on the floor with the legs raised (`bodyPitch` -1.1, `lie` 1); the pin kneels (knees 2.5, so the shins lie along the floor instead of a deep crouch). The wrist lock stays a low crouch, now with its feet on the floor.
- Check: `Ground submissions (P18-38)` in `breakpoint-tests.js`. Test 1 plays every style that can hold (6 styles, 4 hold poses) on the 8 test bodies, the foe face up and face down alternately, and the 3 one-handed items (bat, chain, bottle) in the holder's hand: both fighters stand 30 ticks, the foe is knocked down, Grapple 2 ticks later, 100 ticks of hold, then the holder lets go and both get up; the lowest drawn part of both is read on EVERY tick until both have stood 30 ticks. It fails if the part is deeper than 0.2 cm under the floor while lying (or under the standing stance it came from), more than 3 cm under the stance while standing, more than 3 cm above the floor 25 ticks into a lying pose, or moves more than 3 cm in one tick (the get-up is checked too). Worst after the fix: 0.0 cm in every hold and lying pose; at the start and the end of a hold the part is only at the standing stance's own toe sink (-3.4 to -8.0 cm, as when standing), and the biggest one-tick move is 2.5 cm. Test 2 checks that nobody is measured while standing and that the measuring is gone once both stand again. Test 3 lays a knocked-down and a knocked-out fighter, face up and face down, on the 8 bodies, 40 ticks after the fall (a knock-out needs 26), and needs -0.2 cm to +3 cm; on the old code it fails with +1.8 to +4.8 cm face up and -7.5 to -15.9 cm face down, after the fix every one is 0.0 cm.
- Left alone: a standing guard stance draws the toes up to 8 cm below the floor line (the standing toes were not part of this task); `LIE_HEIGHT` and `liePose` are unchanged, the shift corrects the drawn height on top of them. The flight of a throw is not corrected. The get-up now stays on the floor too (it sank 28 cm into it on the old code); for the 20 ticks the pose blends back to the stance the lowest part is held at the depth of the fighter's guard stance (`stanceLow`), not at 0, so a fighter who is up again is not lifted by his toe sink (review finding). The test also fails if a standing row is more than 3 cm above the stance, and once 25 ticks into a lying pose it needs -0.2 cm.

### P18-36 crowd camera and body stacking (2026-11-05)

- Found during P18-34: the `Brawl camera` hidden-player test (warehouse, 141 ticks in a row, limit 54) failed on seeds 3 and 8 on the code before P18-34 and on 5 and 7 after it, and the `1v6 brawl` test (two bodies closer than 0.3 m) failed on seed 2 before and on 5 and 9 after. Both tests were pinned to seed 6 to pass. They now loop over seeds 1 to 12 (`CROWD_SEEDS`); on the code before this task they fail on seeds 5 and 9 (1v6, 0.24 m and 0.22 m) and on seeds 7 and 8 (camera, warehouse, 141 and 139 ticks).
- Camera cause: the warehouse metal rack at x -10.9, z -4.0 (1.2 m x 2.6 m, 2.4 m tall) stands against the west wall. A player in the corner just behind its end (x -11.1, z -5.7) is in line with it as seen from the usual eye position (the eye is held 0.6 m off the side wall, 0.5 rad of pitch). Every pose in `CAMERA_OCCLUSION_TRIES` stayed hidden: the azimuth swing is limited to +-0.55 rad round the base (a clear view needs 1.0 to 1.4 rad from the side), and the pitch was capped at 1.15 rad (66 degrees), which still looks along the rack. The camera then waited for the enemies to move, up to 141 ticks.
- Camera fix (`CAMERA_STEEP_SWING`, `CAMERA_ELEV_STEEP_MAX`, `swungElev`, `CAMERA_OCCLUSION_TRIES`): the list ends with two nearly straight-down views, `[0, 0.9, 0]` and `[0, 0.9, 1]` (normal aim, then aimed at the players). A pitch swing of 0.9 or more may reach 1.4 rad (80 degrees); every other swing and the normal camera keep the cap `CAMERA_ELEV_MAX` of 1.15 rad (66 degrees), so every earlier pose is exactly as before (a first version of this fix raised the one cap to 1.4, which also let the 0.7 swings reach 1.16 to 1.25 rad; the review caught that). The two are tried last, so every case that was clear before behaves as before; the swing releases 0.6 s after the normal view is clear, as always. A pure pitch swing keeps the camera azimuth, so the stick directions the player has learned do not turn. The limit in the test (grace 0.3 s plus 0.6 s) is unchanged. The 1v1, Versus and Training camera does not use the swing.
- Body cause: `MatchSim.separate` skipped every pair with a THROWN body, however low it was. A body that is thrown (or juggled) lands in `afterCollision`, after the separation of that tick, so one that came down on a standing or walking fighter stayed inside it for the whole landing tick (0.22 to 0.24 m apart; the next tick pushed them apart, so it lasted exactly one tick in every case found). In the test seed 5 was a thrown enemy landing on the standing player, seed 9 the thrown player landing on a walking enemy.
- Body fix (`MatchSim.separate`): a THROWN body counts as flying only while it is above its footing or rising; once it is at its footing and not rising (the same test that lands it in `afterCollision`) it is pushed apart like any body on that tick. The push, the height test (1.25 m) and the clinch, hold and grapple exceptions are unchanged; a body still in flight passes over the others as before.
- Balance: no combat number changed. The simulation changes only on the landing tick of a throw that lands inside another body.

### P18-44 raised ground (2026-11-05)

- Audit: every raised surface of the 8 levels (19 decks, rings and scaffold tiers and 23 stair slopes) was played with both fighters on it, one on it and one on the floor beside it, and on two steps of each stair slope, for every strike class (Punch, Crouch + Punch, Kick, Crouch + Kick, jump kick, running lunge), every one of the 14 items swung, the grab and its throw, the tackle, the stomp, the ground hold, the pick-up (person and CPU seat), the thrown item and the set-down item, and for a CPU seat (how it sees the foe, its strikes and its grab). On the old code 8 of the 11 tests failed, on the new code none.

| ID | Sev | Surface | Action | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R01 | High | every deck, scaffold tier, ring, stage and stair step above 0.3 m, every level | Grab, throw, clinch (player and CPU) | `Combat.evaluate` refused a grab when `att.y > 0.3` or `def.y > 0.5`, measured from the floor: a fighter standing on a footing above 0.3 could never grab (65 of the 99 runs wrong) | measured from the fighter's own footing, plus the grab ledge (`breakpoint.html` `Combat.evaluate`) |
| R02 | High | the same | Mid and Low strikes, crouched or not, a weapon swing (all 14 items), jump kick, lunge | the same rows: Low missed at `def.y > 0.35`, Mid at `def.y > 0.9`, so on a deck of 1.0 m and more a Mid strike missed and a Low strike never landed; on the mezzanines (2.5 m) only High strikes landed (320 wrong results of the strike presses, 66 of the weapon swings) | measured from the attacker's footing (`rise`) |
| R03 | High | the same | Stomp on a downed foe | no height check at all for a foe lying down, so a fighter on the floor stomped one on a 2.5 m mezzanine and the reverse (96 wrong results with the effect checks) | the Low rule and the strike ledge apply to the stomp |
| R04 | Medium | the same, one above the other | Any strike across a tall ledge | nothing compared the two footings: a fighter under a mezzanine hit one up on it with a High strike, and the reverse | strike ledge 1.05 m, grab ledge 0.55 m (Appendix C) |
| R05 | Medium | the same | Heavy tackle | `Combat.tackle` read `att.y > 0.3` and `def.y > 0.5` from the floor (51 runs wrong) | from the footings, plus the grab ledge |
| R06 | High | every raised surface | CPU: grab, Low strikes | `AIController.observe` stored the foe's height above the floor, so a CPU on a deck saw a foe on the same deck as 2.5 m up: it never planned a grab and rated every Low move 0.1 (82 runs wrong; a forced grab plan held nobody in 19 surfaces) | the foe's height above the CPU's own footing (`AIController.observe`) |
| R07 | Low | every raised surface | Effects of a stomp, clinch, throw, escape, hold, tackle, KO, impact, wall slam, fall damage | fixed heights above the floor (0.2 to 1.1 m): the flash and dust were drawn inside the deck | `def.y + height` |

- Checked and fine (no failure): picking up an item at the feet of a fighter on any surface (person and CPU seat), the thrown item leaving the hand, the set-down item coming to rest on the surface it fell to, a ground hold within 0.6 m, a grab pulling the foe down a stair (no fall).
- Left to the owner: nothing to decide. The numbers (1.05 / 0.55 / 0.35 / 0.9) are in Appendix C; the strike ledge could be raised to 1.55 to let a floor fighter reach a foe on a scaffold tier.

### P17-01 whole-game audit (2026-11-06)

- Base: `main` at 775c3aa (P18-40, P18-38, P18-33, P18-36, P18-25 + P18-32, P18-44 and the decisions P18-18, P18-02, P18-42 are in; P17-03 to P17-08 are done in code). No game code and no test was changed: nothing found is Critical. No browser was run (this task may not start one), so everything about looks is judged from the drawn poses and their numbers, and the owner's device checks (P16-03) still have the last word. Line numbers are `breakpoint.html` at this commit.
- Scope: the owner's list of 2026-10-31 and the later owner feedback, each checked on the game as it is now: animation scale, attacks that visibly miss, defence timing, menus and selection, controls on mobile, props, level bounds, style identity, player text, size headroom, determinism, and the rows of this appendix that still say open.
- Result: 0 new Critical, 5 High, 7 Medium, 3 Low, 5 Informational (20 findings). Critical issues fixed by P18-40 (listed as fixed): E01 (a new Arcade run or bracket replaced the save at once) and E02 (a gamepad could not reach the Entrants row and the CPU level on the join screens, High); both stay fixed (their self-tests pass in the full run below).

| ID | Sev | Area | Location | Evidence | Fix task |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Method A (pose; the 32 roster bodies with every move of their style's string rows plus the air, run and counter moves, 594 pairs; the limb tip at its furthest active tick; the front of the volume is `reach` times the Reach stat from the attacker's centre, less the move's `step`): means per move of how far the hand or foot stops short of the front of the volume: Jab 0.45 m, Cross 0.58, Hook 0.69, Uppercut 0.62, Roundhouse 0.53, Heavy Roundhouse 0.57, Front Kick 0.58, Low Kick 0.55, Elbow 0.85 (a 1.1 m volume), Chain Punch 0.81, Angle 1 0.96. - Method (probes were temp files, not kept in the repo; each can become a self-test): A. The game script run in a stub browser; a `Fighter` per roster body, state ATTACK, the move set the way `startAttack` does (`moveSpeedMult`, `mStartup`, `mActive`, `mRecovery`), `moveTick` stepped over the active ticks, `computePose()` then `skeleton(0, 0, 0, 0, false)`, and the key points (`pts` 9 to 20 hands and feet, 21 to 23 the item's far end) read in the fighter's own frame (z ahead). B. `Combat.apply` wrapped to record the key point of the attacker's limb and the foe's position for every unblocked strike of 32 `SimRunner.run` matches (one fighter per style, each against the next and the fifth one along, seeds 100 and up, 25 s). C. `stringsFor(style)` for the 16 styles: move sets, rows, signature entries. D. The largest difference of each joint from the guard over a whole move, for every move of every roster body. E. Constants and CSS read as text; the touch layout formulas worked out by hand for 844 x 390 and 360 / 390 wide portrait.
- For P17-02: the number to bring down is the gap of F01. A self-test can take Method A as it is (for each of the 32 bodies and each move: the limb tip at the first active tick within a set distance of the front of the volume, and not past the foe's centre; plus the height class of F03). The choices are to lengthen the visible reach (lean and step into the strike, `rootFwd`, longer arm and leg travel), to cut `reach` and `radius` (changes every balance number and fingerprint of P14-01 and the AI spacing, so a re-measure with `SimRunner` and one re-record), or both; weapon swings (F16) already do the first. Changing `reach` before P18-03/04 forces that task's re-record to be done on the new numbers.
- For P17-09/10: F04 to F07 give the baseline (11 own moves, 3 identical pairs, 2 style throws, 4 hold poses, closest guard 0.08 rad). New moves must sit in the damage-per-frame and frame-advantage band the existing style-move self-test checks, and cost size (F14).
- Checked and fine (owner items of 2026-10-31 with no finding): (1) Sway only back / spine too far back (P17-03): `Fighter` 6027 takes `swayDir` from the stick side and 6041 / 6042 move the body back or sideways by it; `SWAY_POSE` 4577 caps the back lean at 0.3 rad and the side slip bends toward the side with no back tilt; the self-tests `Sway pose (P17-03)` (back lay-back at most 0.35 rad, a side sway never tips the torso back, root step 0.15 to 0.5 m) and `Sway: ... the lean follows the stick` pass in the full run. (2) Move list confusing (P17-04): one list for the current fighter and style, three columns at most; the five `Move list (P17-04)` tests pass. (3) Developer notes in player text (P17-05): the owner's example sentence is gone (14062 keeps only the plain help "Strong moves cost more stamina and leave you open longer if they miss" that P17-05 left on purpose); `rg` finds no task id, TODO, tuned or design-note wording in the player strings, and the `Player-facing text (P17-05)` test over 40 or more screens passes in the full run.
- Checks: full headless run through `heavy.mjs` (extract.js, harness.js): 528 / 545 passed, exactly the 17 known failures (16 "needs the main menu", 1 "blank faces"), listeners 25 / 25; `breakpoint.html` 1,189,162 bytes (LF, BOM counted) of 1,200,000, unchanged by this task. The browser self-test was not run in this task (no browser); the coordinator runs it before the tick.
